# ComfyQ — project memory

Portable project knowledge for ComfyQ (multi-user ComfyUI workflow scheduler). The **v2 rebuild is now the active code on the default `main` branch** — the standalone `v2` branch is a stale snapshot (behind `main`), and the original v1 monolith lives on `poc1` / `poc2`. So: work on `main`; ignore any older doc text that says "checkout v2". This file lives in the repo so it travels with the project across machines/drives — keep it here, not in any machine-local `~/.claude/` folder. Paths below are **relative to the repo root** (the drive letter changes between machines, so never hardcode `D:\`).

Detailed, evolving plans live in [implementation_plan.md](implementation_plan.md); user-facing overview in [README.md](README.md). This file is the durable, auto-loaded summary + the "why" behind decisions that aren't obvious from the code.

Every launch confirmation now shows the hours the balance covers (⚠ under 4 h), and **a launch the balance cannot pay for one hour is refused (402)** before anything is created — RunPod stops a Pod at zero balance. The RunPod launch had no cost confirmation at all before this. Worth setting on Thunder's website: its credit-balance alert (Settings → Billing), which emails when the credit drops below a threshold.
  - The ledger started 2026-10-20 ~10:00, so that day's and month's totals leave out the morning's two Thunder tests (~$0.80). The bar says "counted from …".

Thunder tab = workflow picker, card table (cheapest that fits and is in stock), vCPU/disk, Launch → create (`template: base`; Thunder returns the private key once → `~/.comfyq/thunder-keys/<uuid>.pem`) → wait RUNNING → SSH (`ubuntu`, else `root`) → a bash script on stdin pulls and runs our image (secrets via a 0600 env file, never a command line) → `PATCH /v1/instances/{id}/ports` adds 3000 → probes `https://<uuid>-3000.thundercompute.net` like a Pod. *Retry setup* (idempotent), *Download results* (the instance zips its own `pods/<COMFYQ_POD_ID>` folder), *Delete* (type the uuid's first 8 characters). Launches go in the Attempts tab. Records: `~/.comfyq/thunder-instances.json`. Tests: [thunder.test.mjs](tools/runpod/thunder.test.mjs) 27 checks (in `test:storyboard`); [tests/thunder-console.mjs](tools/runpod/tests/thunder-console.mjs) 14 end-to-end checks against a fake Thunder + fake ssh + fake ComfyQ (`--browser <chrome.exe>` adds 3 headless-Chrome checks). Hooks: `THUNDER_API_URL`, `COMFYQ_THUNDER_PROXY`, `COMFYQ_THUNDER_SSH` (a `.mjs` stand-in is run with node).

**Thunder facts learned the hard way**
1. ★★ **`rsync -a` exits 23 on Thunder even inside our own container** — setting folder permissions fails there (their dockerd itself runs in a container). Every file was copied, then the entrypoint's `set -e` restarted the container in a loop and the student URL showed Thunder's "Nothing running here". **Fixed in the image: `rsync -rlt`** ([entrypoint.sh](docker/entrypoint.sh), `sha-85a1fe2`).
2. ★ The setup script used to say "started" while the container was restarting; it now waits 20 s, checks `Up`, else prints the container's last lines and exits 21 (setup shown as failed, the instance still billing).
3. Docker needs `sudo` for `ubuntu` (the script falls back to `sudo -n docker`); each `docker run` takes ~1 min there; `--gpus` is unsupported (use `--device nvidia.com/gpu=all`); Compose does not work.
4. The API has **no exec**: anything inside the instance goes over SSH. Calls copied from Thunder's own CLI (github.com/Thunder-Compute/thunder-cli): `GET /v1/instances/list[?update_ips=true]` (an object keyed by numeric id), `POST /v1/instances/create`, `POST /v1/instances/{id}/delete`, `PATCH /v1/instances/{id}/ports`, `POST /v1/instances/{id}/add_key`; public `GET /v2/specs|pricing|status`.
5. Thunder publishes stock twice (`specs.<type>_x1` and `gpu_type.<type>["1"]`) and they **disagree** (A6000: one "available", one "unavailable") → the console says *unclear*.
6. There is **no stop**: an instance is running (billing) or deleted (disk gone). Launch early on the day; *Download results* before Delete.

**⚠ Never stop serving the main workflow with jobs in the queue (owner, 2026-10-20).** On the Thunder test the owner pressed *Stop serving* on SDXL — the **primary** lane, because it was first in `COMFYQ_SERVE` — and ComfyQ restarted into admin mode: the LTX 2.5 lane went with it, the job rendering failed `server-restart`, and the queue sat waiting. That is the button's design ([RunningWorkflows.jsx](client/src/components/admin/RunningWorkflows.jsx): primary = stop the machine, extra = close only that lane), but **it has no confirmation and no warning about queued jobs**. Written in the README's *Running* bullet. **Guard added (owner, 2026-10-20):** *Stop serving* / *Reset to admin* (Running card, workflow card, header) now reads `/jobs?status=processing` and `?status=scheduled` first and, if anything is there (or the queue cannot be read), asks in a window naming how many renders will fail and how many queued jobs will wait — *Keep serving* / *Stop serving anyway*; with an empty queue it stops at once as before ([AdminConfig.jsx](client/src/pages/AdminConfig.jsx) `requestStopServing`). Built and compiled here; not yet clicked on a serving machine (this PC has no GPU) — check on the next Pod/instance. In image `sha-2df128e`. Also: put the workflow students will use most **first**, so it is the primary.

**Student links — found on the real instance:** a student page books only the workflow its address names, and the working form is **`/user?workflow=<id>`** — the bare `/?workflow=` does NOT work (`/` redirects to `/user` and drops the query). With two workflows the console's single *Student page* link reached only the main one, so **the console now lists one link per workflow** (RunPod Pods tab and Thunder tab; `studentLinks()` in [lib.mjs](tools/runpod/lib.mjs)).

**Decisions the owner made (keep them)**
- Safest route by default; short "go" approvals; verify on real machines. **Ask once, with the cost, before any spend** (RunPod or Thunder).
- **Backup provider = Thunder Compute**; cheap over EU-only; **no snapshots**.
- Phase I: results and each Pod's workflow copy stay **on the volume, per Pod**; a download lock is abandoned only after 10 min stale + owner heartbeat gone + `.part` quiet 2 min. I6: **a running Pod reads the volume**.
- **Keep one volume in EU-RO-1; no second volume.** Plan B (*No network volume*) is the RunPod fallback; Thunder is the next one.
- Stock history via **GitHub Actions**. Lane test uses **LTX 2.5 image-to-video as it is**.

**RunPod facts learned the hard way**
1. Scoped to one data centre, a missing `stockStatus` means **none**. 2. Stock changes within seconds; no queue or reservation. 3. Three wordings for "no machine" (`isNoMachine()`). 4. A Pod's volume is `networkVolumeId`; its image is `imageName`. 5. A cheap Pod can sit ~12 min "rented" with no machine. 6. The API cannot tell a read-only key from a read/write one without trying a write. 7. A network volume pins every Pod to its data centre. 8. RunPod keeps no stock history. 9. On 2026-10-19 evening no 48 GB+ card was free anywhere in Europe for hours.

**This machine**: `npm install` done in `server/` and `client/`; `better-sqlite3` fetched with `npx prebuild-install`, so `npm run test:storyboard` runs here in full. ⚠ **A console started before a code change must be restarted** (its routes don't reload) — it bit twice on 2026-10-20 ("failed to fetch", then missing links). The console uses Windows' built-in `ssh.exe` for Thunder.

- RunPod's **third** "no machine" wording ("This machine does not have the resources to deploy your pod") — all three in `isNoMachine()`.

**RunPod facts learned the hard way**
1. Scoped to one data centre, a missing `stockStatus` means **none**. 2. Stock changes within seconds; there is **no queue or reservation** — a freed card goes to whoever asks first, so poll every few seconds. 3. Three wordings for "no machine". 4. A Pod's volume is `networkVolumeId`; its image is `imageName`. 5. A cheap Pod can sit ~12 min "rented" with no machine. 6. The API cannot tell a read-only key from a read/write one without trying a write. 7. A network volume pins every Pod to its data centre. 8. RunPod keeps no stock history (hence the recorder). 9. On 2026-10-19 evening no 48 GB+ card was free anywhere in Europe for hours (one B300 at $8.99/h in EU-NL-1).

**This machine**: `npm install` done in `server/` and `client/`; `better-sqlite3`'s native binary was fetched with `npx prebuild-install` in `server/node_modules/better-sqlite3`, so **`npm run test:storyboard` now runs here in full and passes** (exit 0, 2026-10-19). Puppeteer for browser checks lives in the session scratchpad, not the repo.

### What was built today, in order

**RunPod: a Pod configured by its environment, a launcher, and a local console** — owner: *"is it possible to automate the configuration and launching of a pod?"*, then *"a front that will run locally… enter all the credentials and launch a pod and also monitor it and also choose the right gpu"*.

- **[server/cloud/podPlan.js](server/cloud/podPlan.js)**: the entrypoint runs [podEnv.js](server/cloud/podEnv.js) once per container start. It stores `COMFYQ_ADMIN_PASSWORD` / `COMFYQ_ACCESS_PASSWORD` / `COMFYQ_HF_TOKEN` (or `HF_TOKEN`). When `COMFYQ_SERVE=<id>[,<id>…]` is set, it writes `server/data/pod-plan.json` and clears both serving markers, so ComfyQ boots in **admin** mode; admin boot then runs `runPlan()`. That fetches what the workflows are missing through `ModelDownloader`, re-checks the disk, sets `activeWorkflowId` + `extraLaneWorkflowIds`, and calls `exitForRestart('student')`. ★ The fetch runs inside the listening server so `GET /admin/pod-plan` can report progress file by file. ★★ A password is stored again only if it changed: bcrypt salts every hash and the student token derives from it, so re-hashing on each start would log every student out on every restart. ★ **`COMFYQ_SERVE` is refused without an ADMIN password** unless `COMFYQ_ALLOW_OPEN=1`. **The student password is optional** (owner: *"i don't need a student password"*).
- **[tools/runpod/runpod.mjs](tools/runpod/README.md)**: the command-line launcher (`launch / status / list / volumes / stop / start / terminate`).
- **The RunPod console**: `RunPod-Console.bat` / `npm run runpod:console` → `http://localhost:4777`. [server.mjs](tools/runpod/console/server.mjs) has no dependencies and shares [lib.mjs](tools/runpod/lib.mjs) with the CLI. Credentials live in `~/.comfyq/runpod-console.json`; the page only learns whether each is set, plus the last 4 characters of the two long tokens, **never of a password**. ★ **Three locks on a local page holding a money-spending key:** it binds to `127.0.0.1`, checks `Host`, and requires a custom header on `/api`; Terminate also needs the Pod id typed back. **GPU choice:** VRAM need is each workflow's calibrated `vramPeakGb` (else `minVRAM`), added up across lanes, + 2 GB. Prices and stock come from GraphQL `gpuTypes { lowestPrice(input:{dataCenterId}) }` in the volume's data centre. The cheapest card that fits and is in stock is recommended, and the next 3 are ticked as fallbacks. Pods get `allowedCudaVersions ["12.8","12.9","13.0"]`, because the image is CUDA 12.8.
- ★★ **RunPod quirks learned the hard way:** (1) asked about one data centre, RunPod returns **no** `stockStatus` for a GPU it has none of there, so a missing figure means *none* — reading it as "unknown" recommended a card EU-RO-1 lacked and the Pod was refused; (2) stock there changed between two reads 3 s apart; (3) "no machine free" comes in **three** wordings (*"could not find any pods with required specifications"*, *"There are no longer any instances available with the requested specifications"*, and — seen in the evening — *"This machine does not have the resources to deploy your pod. Please try a different machine"*); all are in [lib.mjs](tools/runpod/lib.mjs) `isNoMachine`; (4) a Pod's volume is `networkVolumeId`, not an object; (5) a cheap Pod sat ~12 min "rented" with no machine before our image even ran; (6) the RunPod API cannot tell a read-only key from a read/write one without trying a write.
- Tested against a stand-in RunPod (fake REST + GraphQL + a Pod proxy), driven in headless Chrome. That found *Test the key* wiping passwords typed but not yet saved. Test hooks: `RUNPOD_REST_URL`, `RUNPOD_GRAPHQL_URL`, `COMFYQ_POD_PROXY`.

**Phase I — one network volume shared by several Pods** — owner: *"I would like to be able to create a shared storage to be able to use it with multiple instances"*.

- **I0, stage 0 on two real Pods** ([tools/runpod/stage0](tools/runpod/stage0/run.mjs)): an exclusive create had exactly one winner in 400 races (194/206); a file one Pod wrote was visible to the other in 7.4 ms median, 15 ms worst; renamed and replaced files were never read partial; two Pods reading one model at once got 877 + 757 MB/s against 921 alone.
- **I1 + I2, layout and conversion** ([volume-layout.sh](docker/volume-layout.sh), 34 checks in [test-volume-layout.sh](docker/test-volume-layout.sh)). `shared/{models,assets,_model_quarantine,pods}` for everyone, `pods/<RUNPOD_POD_ID>/` per Pod (`pods/default` outside RunPod). An old volume is converted once by **moving, never copying or overwriting**. One Pod converts, under an exclusive-create lock; it waits while the old queue folder is still being written; and it leaves a `comfyui/models` link for older images. ★ A Pod restarted mid-conversion used to wait 15 min for its **own** lock; its own id is now taken back at once. **Verified:** the first Pod on the new image converted `comfyq-models` with 0 models missing and its 8 jobs kept.
- **I3 + I4, download lock and prune guards** ([sharedVolume.js](server/cloud/sharedVolume.js), 29 checks). Heartbeats are written to `shared/pods/<id>.json` every 30 s and count as alive for 3 min. The download lock is `<model>.lock`, by exclusive create, refreshed every 30 s. Another Pod needing the model gets status `waiting`, then `done` with `installedBy: 'another Pod'`. Takeover happens only on the Pod's own id, or when all three signs agree; the lock is renamed aside and verified first. Prune / quarantine restore / quarantine empty return **409** while another Pod is alive, the Maintenance tab says who else is on the volume ([VolumePods.jsx](client/src/components/admin/VolumePods.jsx)), and the image records `COMFYQ_IMAGE_SHA`. ★★ **A stalled transfer hung forever:** Node 24 keeps a quiet connection open, so a download read "downloading" indefinitely — and under a lock every other Pod would have waited on it. Downloads now fail after 2 min without data, keeping the `.part`. This was the real cause of the old `modelDownloader.test.js` failure, now 32/32. **Verified on two real Pods:** one downloaded the 9.1 GB model, the other found it installed; each listed the other; prune was refused on both; both captioned at once.
- ⚠ **Extra lanes skip the VRAM check**: they reopen through `restoreRemembered()` with `force: true`, so pick workflows whose measured VRAM fits together.
- **Tests added today:** [podPlan.test.js](server/cloud/podPlan.test.js) 29, [sharedVolume.test.js](server/cloud/sharedVolume.test.js) 29, [lib.test.mjs](tools/runpod/lib.test.mjs) 29, [test-volume-layout.sh](docker/test-volume-layout.sh) 34 (bash, needs real symlinks: on Windows run with `MSYS=winsymlinks:nativestrict`), [modelDownloader.test.js](server/models/modelDownloader.test.js) 32.

## Latest progress (2026-10-13)

**ComfyQ as a Docker image for RunPod — built on GitHub, verified on a real Pod with an SDXL smoke test (owner request, 2026-10-13)** — owner: *"the main goal is to be able to launch this docker on runpod or an equivalent"*, then *"i need a solution in order to build a new docker when needed"*. This copy now lives in **[urbandronedesign/comfyQ_docker](https://github.com/urbandronedesign/comfyQ_docker)** (public), with `upstream` → `b2renger/ComfyQ` kept fetch-only so b2renger's work can still be pulled. Commits here are authored as `urbandronedesign` (repo-local git config).

- ★★ **RunPod Pods fit; Runware does not.** A Pod is a long-lived GPU machine running our own image with a persistent `/workspace` volume and a public HTTPS proxy per port (`https://<pod-id>-<port>.proxy.runpod.net`) — exactly ComfyQ's shape. Runware's Serverless is request/response (one POST per task, workers scale to zero, no documented websockets, UI or local state), so it could only ever be a remote *backend* for ComfyQ, which would be a new worker type, not a deployment.
- **[docker/](docker/README.md)**: CUDA 12.8 runtime on Ubuntu 24.04, Python 3.12 venv in `/opt/venv`, **torch 2.8.0+cu128** (same as the rigs, held by [constraints.txt](docker/constraints.txt) against every pack's `pip install`), ComfyUI **v0.39.2** cloned to `/opt/ComfyUI` (`COMFYUI_VERSION` build arg), node packs from [custom-nodes.txt](docker/custom-nodes.txt), and onnxruntime forced back to the GPU wheel after the packs. **No model is in the image.**
- ★ **The pack list was read off the rig, not guessed.** Most packs on the Windows install were installed from the registry as zips, so `git remote` on them reports ComfyUI's own repo; each URL came from the pack's `pyproject.toml` `Repository`, its own remote, or `api.comfy.org/nodes/<id>`. 22 packs: 14 used by the bundles, ComfyUI-Manager (the missing-pack check reads its `extension-node-map.json`), and small utilities. **Left out:** Trellis2 + Pixal3D (their bundles are gone and they need CUDA extensions a CPU runner cannot build), CrossDriveViewFix (Windows drive letters), and HR-Endless-Sampler + Dynamic-Lora-Scheduler (no published source, no bundle uses them).
- **Build:** [.github/workflows/docker.yml](.github/workflows/docker.yml) → `ghcr.io/urbandronedesign/comfyq`, started from the Actions tab or a **`docker-v*`** tag (`v*` belongs to the desktop release). Tags `sha-<commit>` plus `latest` on main. First build **12 m 43 s**; the layer cache lives in the registry (`:buildcache`). ★ The package came out **public on its own**, inheriting the public repo's visibility; verified by an anonymous manifest pull.
- **[entrypoint.sh](docker/entrypoint.sh)** links `config.json`, `server/data`, `.comfyq-lanes`, `workflows/` and ComfyUI's `models/input/output/user/comfyq_ingredients` onto `/workspace` (seeding without overwriting), rewrites the ComfyUI paths in config.json on every start (`installation_type: system`, absolute venv python, quarantine on the volume), and runs ComfyQ in a restart loop in place of nodemon. **Workflows on the volume are only ADDED to by a new image**; `COMFYQ_SYNC_WORKFLOWS=1` overwrites them from the image.
- **[server/index.js](server/index.js) now serves `client/dist` when it exists**, so the container needs one port (3000). Only the SPA's own pages (`/`, `/admin`, `/user`, `/user/dashboard`) fall back to index.html, never a prefix: `/admin/*` is Express, and a download link is a browser navigation sending `Accept: text/html` too. `npm run dev` has no `client/dist` and is unchanged.
- ★★★ **The first real Pod failed every job in 0.8 s with `[Errno 32] Broken pipe`, and the cause is general.** ComfyUI's stdout/stderr are pipes into the ComfyQ that spawned it. "Activate & serve" restarts ComfyQ; ComfyUI survives with both ends gone; the new ComfyQ adopts it as already running; the first line ComfyUI prints during a job raises BrokenPipeError. **Fix (container only): the restart loop stops every `/opt/ComfyUI/main.py` before the next ComfyQ starts** (`procps` added for pkill/pgrep). The cost is a model reload after each mode switch. ⚠ **The Windows rigs dodge this by luck, not design** — the right general fix is to spawn ComfyUI with its output to a log file rather than a pipe; not done.
- ★ **New [image_sdxl_smoke_test](workflows/image_sdxl_smoke_test/)** (experimental, t2i): one public 6.94 GB model (`sd_xl_base_1.0`, link HEAD-checked, no token), core nodes only, so it works even if a pack fails to install. Rendered on this PC's 5090 through a throwaway ComfyUI on port 8199: **29 s cold, 3.3 s warm**, and the picture checked by eye. Template generated with `api-to-ui`, round-trip identical. **On RunPod it produced an image on the second Pod** (after the fix above) — the end-to-end proof of the image.
- **Known limits on RunPod**, all in [docker/README.md](docker/README.md): the proxy cuts any request at **100 s** (some admin actions waiting on a cold ComfyUI may 524, then succeed); **"Open in ComfyUI" builds `<host>:8188`**, which is not RunPod's port naming; **"Update ComfyUI" refuses** (portable-only) — rebuild with a new `COMFYUI_VERSION`; a pack installed from the panel lives on the container disk and vanishes at restart; fleet discovery cannot see a Pod.
- **Setup found on this machine, recorded so nobody repeats it:** the copy carried `pre-commit`/`pre-push` hooks and a `DISABLED-read-only-mirror` push URL marking it a read-only reference for a `Desktop/comfyq-runpod-docker` working copy that only existed on the laptop. The owner chose to unlock this copy; the hooks are gone. ⚠ Git Credential Manager here held a stored GitHub entry whose *username* is a personal access token — it should be revoked on GitHub and removed from Windows Credential Manager. `gh` is installed and logged in as urbandronedesign, and git uses it for github.com. `better-sqlite3` copied from the laptop has no native build for this machine (`npm rebuild better-sqlite3 --prefix server`), so `test:storyboard` stops at its first sqlite test; the bundle tests pass.

## Latest progress (2026-10-12)

**Preparing the drive to be cloned — docs rewritten for the people who read them, the library swept clean, and the sweep's own results kept as a browsable example per workflow (owner, 2026-10-12)** — owner: *"check the state of the code vs the docs and specifically the readme users see. We need to clean everything to duplicate… run a sweep of the workflow to be sure everything runs, clear models quarantine, update docs and readme… readme.txt at the base of J:"*, then *"I am interested in keeping the sweep inputs and outputs as examples I can look at or students can look at in the all jobs filter in the client UI to know what we can actually do with the full rig."*

### ★★★ I killed the sweep by editing `server/` — the rule was already in this file

Two hours into a 2½-hour sweep I edited `server/index.js` for an unrelated path fix. nodemon restarted ComfyQ, the run's in-memory state went with it, and the sweep died at **37 of 61**. ★★ **This file's own conventions say "Never edit anything under `server/` while a long job is running", and name the previous casualty at bundle 32 of 61.** I had read it and written part of it. The edit felt small and unrelated, which is exactly the shape of the mistake: **the cost is not proportional to the size of the change, because nodemon does not grade changes by size.**

- ★★ **It cost ~25 minutes instead of 90, because the rows were already on disk and a sweep MERGES.** The per-run `.jsonl` held all 36 finished rows; what a restart loses is the merge *base*, since the next run merges over the **published** `library-sweep-latest.json`, not over the `.jsonl`. New **[tools/maintenance/publish_partial_sweep.cjs](tools/maintenance/publish_partial_sweep.cjs)** writes the finished rows out as the published report and prints the bundles still missing — so sweeping just those produced one complete 61-row report from today rather than a partial one beside a stale one.
- ★ **Its summary is computed by `LibrarySweep.summarize`, not counted locally**, so a recovered report's figures cannot disagree with the card and the report. A second counter in a recovery script is the "two heuristics agreeing with each other" shape this file already warns about.
- ⚠ **`recover_sweep.cjs` was the wrong tool here, and it is worth knowing why.** It rebuilds a report from *side effects* — rewritten `runtime.json` files, `bench_*` outputs, the access log — which is what you need when the run records are gone. Here the rows existed; reconstructing them from traces would have discarded the ingredients, tracebacks and perf diagnostics they already carried.

### ★★ And the watch on the recovery was broken for an hour, silently

The continuation ran under a monitor that **emitted nothing because it could not run**. The poller was an inline `python -c` whose modulo I wrote as `%%` — a habit from printf and Make. **bash does not treat `%` specially, so Python received a literal `%%` and raised a SyntaxError**, which the `2>/dev/null` on the end discarded. Three successive monitors across roughly an hour reported a clean sweep by saying nothing, and I only questioned it because an unrelated monitor expired saying "no events delivered".

- ★★★ **Silence is only information if the silent path is the one you tested.** The monitor had a heartbeat *specifically* so quiet stretches would stay legible — and the heartbeat was inside the code that never executed, so the feature meant to prove liveness was itself proof of nothing. The same shape as the perf diagnostic whose only reachable path threw, and the exemption that silenced the true alarm: **a check that cannot run looks exactly like a check that found nothing.**
- **Fixed by removing the layer rather than escaping it better:** the poller is a **file** now, so no shell quoting sits between me and the code; **stderr is not discarded**; a poller that exits non-zero prints `WATCH BROKEN`; and it emits on **every** terminal state — finished, stopped, errored, and the run having vanished because ComfyQ restarted. Then it was **driven against nine fixtures** before being trusted. Two minutes; skipping it cost an hour.

### The library, swept on 0.39.2: **61 of 61 clean**

**0 failed, 0 outputs flagged, 0 produced nothing, 97 distinct weights observed**, 10:10 → 12:19 UTC, 125 minutes of GPU. The second fully clean pass. ⚠ Figures come from two halves of one sweep (36 bundles in 37 min, then 25 after the restart), which the merge presents as one report while each row keeps its own `finishedAt`.

- **Both captioners answered** — 1543 and 1300 characters, so the 2026-10-07 regression stays fixed.
- **Both Qwen 2.1 ControlNet bundles clean** — canny 16 s / 23.39 GB, depth 20 s / 24.15 GB, within noise of the 2026-10-08 figures, so the `pause_malloc_graph` fix holds.
- ★ **`utility_pid_image_upscale` kept its hand-measured 18 s** (`source: "manual"`) — verified, not assumed.
- ⚠ **The 9 stray text tiles are unchanged** on 7 bundles (`"150"`, `"3"`, `"8"`, `"11"`, LivePortrait's three tensor shapes) — still a real student-facing defect, correctly tagged rather than dropped.

### The sweep's results are now a showcase, not just a measurement

The gap the owner's ask names is real: the sweep runs every workflow and keeps what each produced, but it runs **through the calibrator rather than the queue**, so none of it is a job and **none of it is visible anywhere in the interface**. New **[tools/maintenance/seed_examples.cjs](tools/maintenance/seed_examples.cjs)** writes one COMPLETED job per workflow under the user **`examples`**, pointing at the sweep's own result and the settings that produced it. A freshly cloned machine now opens with a populated **All jobs** tab: pick `examples` and every bundle has a result you can click into — the picture or clip, the prompt, the parameters, the input files, and *Use these settings*.

- ★★ **The inputs had to be re-staged, or the examples would have lost them within half an hour.** The sweep stages media as `comfyq__bench-im__<name>` — and `comfyq__` is exactly the prefix `InputUploader.sweepStale()` deletes after 30 minutes, which the wipe also clears. So each input is copied again from its **original** source under the durable `comfyq_session__` prefix. ★ And named to the real convention (`comfyq_session__<n>_<n>__<original>`), because `ingredientsStore.originalFromUpload` parses the original filename back out of it — get the shape wrong and every example's input reads as `example_<id>_<key>.png` instead of `Woman.png`.
- ★★ **The output's kind comes from ComfyQ's own classifier, not the sweep's.** `output_stats.py` calls a `.spz` Gaussian splat `model3d`; ComfyQ classifies it `splat` and routes the two to different viewers (Spark vs GLTFLoader). Seeding the sweep's own value would have sent TripoSplat's headline splat to the mesh viewer. The seeder calls `mediaTypes.classify()` — the function the executor uses — so an example cannot render differently from a real job.
- ★ **The parameters are the EFFECTIVE values**, which is the point of showing them: calibration sends only the media and a fresh seed, so the prompt that actually ran is almost never among the sent parameters.
- ★ **It refuses to reference an input it cannot re-stage.** If an original is missing the parameter is dropped and named, rather than pointing at a staged file the wipe removes. **Driven with a fixture**, because it is a guard whose only reachable path is the case it exists for.
- **Verified three ways:** 518 assertions through ComfyQ's own read path against a **copy** of the database (every output file present where `/images` serves from, every kind one the client renders, every input on disk reading as its original name, coverage 61 of 61); then in headless Chrome against the real student UI — the user filter reads **`examples — 61`**, the grid mounts 27 images / 28 videos / 1 audio / 3 WebGL viewers, a result opens showing Relaunch info with `Woman.png` and `d6.mp4` by their original names, with **0 failed media requests and 0 console errors**.
- ⚠ **The browser half needs the machine SERVING** — in admin mode `/` renders the admin panel and the job grid does not exist — so it was done by activating the lightest bundle, then resetting to admin.

### What the wipe removes, and what it refuses to

New **[tools/maintenance/wipe_for_clone.cjs](tools/maintenance/wipe_for_clone.cjs)** and the procedure in **[docs/cloning-the-drive.md](docs/cloning-the-drive.md)**. Run: **3333 files, 5.28 GB**.

- ★★ **It does not guess which outputs to keep: it spares exactly the files the published report points at** (64 of them). Those are the examples, so keeping them and keeping the report consistent are the same act — and `bench_*` files from **six** previous sweeps go, where a `bench_*` prefix rule would have kept every one.
- ★★ **It leaves 508 files in `ComfyUI/input` alone and says so.** They are not ComfyQ's: reference images, test meshes and working material back to June 2025. 0.93 GB on a 1.5 TB drive is a bad reason to delete somebody's sixteen months of material. **A cleanup script's job is residue, not tidiness** — report it with a size and leave it.
- ★ **Two edge cases closed before running it, both found by thinking about the real machine rather than the dry run.** `VACUUM` wants an exclusive lock and ComfyQ holds the same file open, so it is attempted and a failure reported as "deleted but not compacted" rather than failing the wipe. And the rule for which run records to keep was keyed on `recoveredFrom` — **a field a MERGED report does not carry**, so after the sweep published it would have deleted today's own records with the stale ones; it is keyed on the report's earliest row now.
- **Also cleared:** 138 jobs and 828 events, past sessions' outputs, ComfyQ's uploads, `clipspace`, 2000 ingredient snapshots, lane state, logs, and both boot markers — so a clone comes up in **admin** rather than serving whatever the master served.
- **Kept, each for a reason:** `comfyq_model_access.jsonl` (it can only ever *protect* a model from pruning, and a clone starts with no history of its own) and `pruned-models.jsonl` (how to fetch a pruned model back).
- ⚠ **The quarantine was emptied first (20.31 GB).** A prune MOVES, so a batch is still on the disk and still in the image: without this the clone carries the very files someone decided to delete, to every machine written from it.
- ★ **Order matters and the first draft had it backwards:** the wipe clears the `comfyq_session__` prefix the seeder stages under, so **wipe is step 3 and seeding step 4**. The script notices seeded examples and says to re-seed rather than leaving it to be discovered.

### ★ A scan folder that cannot follow the drive is the quiet failure

`comfy_ui.*` self-heals at boot (configured root has no `main.py` → auto-detect → repoint) and `assets.dir` is repointed with it. **`maintenance.workflowScanDirs` was not**, so on a clone mounted as anything but `J:` the prune tool reasoned about a folder that does not resolve — and that is the path where being wrong is silent, because **a folder it cannot read makes every model only that folder references read as unused**.

- New `configManager.followDriveLetter()`, called from the same auto-detect block. ★ **It rewrites a path only when that path is BOTH unreachable now AND reachable on the new drive** — a folder missing for another reason is left alone so the scan reports it, rather than being quietly redirected somewhere plausible.
- ★ **Extracted rather than left inline, because the first version of its test duplicated the logic instead of importing it** — and a test that re-implements its subject proves only that the copy agrees with itself. New [configManager.test.js](server/config/configManager.test.js), **12 checks** against a real temp filesystem with an unmounted drive letter standing in for the drive the image was cloned from.
- **Verified for the record:** `config.json` carries no secrets to a clone — `adminPasswordHash`, `accessPasswordHash` and `hf_token` all empty — and `systemInfo.detectSystemInfo` re-mints the instance UUID whenever the recorded hostname is not this machine's, so clones do not all broadcast one identity to Discovery.

### Docs, checked against the code rather than against memory

- **[README.md](README.md) rewritten for whoever opens the repository**, which is not what it was for: it opened with prerequisites and an install script and reached the two modes — the thing the whole design turns on — in a parenthesis. Now: the two modes first, then students, the four admin tabs, **getting models onto the machine** as its own section (that is what goes wrong on a fresh rig), maintaining the install, Discovery, and a table into the technical docs. The troubleshooting section was kept verbatim; it was the best part of the old file.
- ★ **Three claims checked against the code and found false.** `.nvmrc` was linked by both the README and this file and **had never existed** (now written, `24`); "ComfyQ does not verify models up front" has been untrue since the readiness chip; and `workflows/exported_api_workflow_comfy/` does not exist. ★ **A doc is not checked by reading it — every path and claim has to be run against the tree**, which is how all three surfaced at once. A fourth turned up the same way in [COMFYQ-NOTES.md](tools/maintenance/COMFYQ-NOTES.md), which said the gitignored scans had gone with the deleted original; they had been rebuilt in place, and being gitignored is exactly why they travel in a clone.
- **One honest gap now stated rather than implied:** a model reachable only through a dropdown appears in no graph, so "0 models missing" does not promise every dropdown option is present.
- **[docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md) carries a dated note** — seven of the bundles it weighs are gone and the library is 61, so a worksheet people consult to decide what to cut no longer misleads. The analysis of the bundles that remain is untouched.
- **Four orphaned docs removed** (`SETUP_FIXES.md`, `UPDATE_SUMMARY.md`, `task.md`, `walkthrough.md` — all 2026-02-01, describing a layout that no longer exists). This file's links to deliberately-removed bundles were **left alone**: it is a dated log, and a dead link in it is a record, not a defect.
- **`J:\Readme.txt` rewritten** — operational and short: how to start, the two modes, what each folder on the drive is, what to do when something breaks, and where the examples are. Kept the two Notion links and the `git config --global --add safe.directory "*"` fix for ComfyUI-Manager's once-per-machine false alarm. Pure ASCII and CRLF, since it is read in Notepad on a machine that may have nothing else.

### Conventions this added

- ★★★ **A rule you wrote yourself does not protect you.** "Never edit `server/` during a long job" was in this file with the previous casualty named, and it still cost 90 minutes. The guard has to fire at the moment of the edit; the edit always feels too small to matter.
- ★★★ **Silence is only information if the silent path is the one you tested.** A watch that cannot run is indistinguishable from a watch that found nothing — and putting the liveness signal *inside* the broken code makes it worse, not better.
- ★★ **An operation that spares some files must derive the spare-list from the thing that defines them**, not from a pattern that resembles it. "Keep what the published report points at" excluded six sweeps of stale output that "keep `bench_*`" would have kept.
- ★★ **A cleanup script's job is residue, not tidiness.** Anything it did not create belongs to somebody: report it with a size and leave it.
- ★ **A path that self-heals in three places out of four is a trap**, because the one that does not is the one nobody checks — here the scan folder, where a missing folder silently converts "in use" into "unused".

**★★★ The angles bundle was starving its own reference — and "trained at 512²" is not "feed it 512²" (owner, 2026-10-12)** — owner: *"the results for the qwen multiple angle are not good, it doesn't keep the structural consistency of the image, or the character pose. It changes too many details."* Both causes were **defaults I chose**, and the 2×2 that separated them is the useful part.

- ★★★ **THE DOMINANT CAUSE: I scaled the reference down to 0.26 MP (512²).** The card says the adapter was trained at 512² and I read that as "feed it 512²". **Training size and reference size are different things.** The reference is what carries the subject; at 512² it carries too little for the model to hold on to, and the result collapses into the flat, plain-background look of the **Objaverse renders it was trained on** — a portrait came back as a black hair-blob on flat grey with cut-out shoulders. Same photo, same seed, same adapter at **1 MP**: a real photograph, earring kept, houndstooth jacket correctly continued round the back. ★ **The failure mode is the tell** — it did not merely lose detail, it reverted to its training distribution. When output looks like the training data rather than the input, suspect the input is too thin to compete.
- ★★ **THE SECOND: I defaulted to v2 because the author recommends it — but v1 matches THIS rig's base build.** The card's own v1/v2 table says v1 was trained on a **convrot8 int8** base, which is the Qwen-Image-2.1 build installed here, and v2 on **bf16 full precision**. On one photo and seed at 1 MP, v1 comes back photographic (film grain, period lighting) and v2 slightly CGI-like. Default is now **v1**; v2 is one field away and may well win on a bf16 rig. ★ **An author's "recommended checkpoint" is recommended against the base they trained on** — check which build it was, because this fleet runs int8-convrot repacks.
- **The 2×2 is what made it diagnosable** (v1/v2 × 0.26/1.0 MP, one photo, one seed): reference size dominates — *both* adapters degrade badly at 512² — and v1 beats v2 at either size. Testing one variable would have found one cause and credited it with the whole effect.
- **Recalibrated for the bigger reference: 25 s warm / 29 s cold / 18.65 GB**, up from 9 s at 512². ⚠ And the description had been *teaching the mistake* — "raising Reference size is outside what the adapter saw" — so it carries the correction now.

**★★ The server-side downloader had no button — found by testing a fresh machine (owner, 2026-10-12)** — owner: *"I want to test how to download the models on another machine… fetch the new workflows, see that it misses models and download them"*, then *"when a model is downloaded, it should place it in the right folder and with the right name (the ones the workflow will look for) for now it's not the case"*.

- **The placement logic was not the problem.** Checked all **215 downloadable models the graphs actually load**, comparing the resolver's answer against the folder each loader NODE reads (`UNETLoader` → `diffusion_models`, `LoraLoaderModelOnly` → `loras`, `VAELoader` → `vae` — **not** the meta's declared `type`, which is only a fallback): **0 wrong folder, 0 wrong name, 0 installed copies sitting anywhere else.**
- ★★★ **The problem was that NOTHING in the client had ever POSTed `/admin/models/download`.** The downloader has existed since 2026-10-05 — resume, free-space check, a `.part` renamed only once the byte count matches, 29 tests — and the only thing a missing-model row offered was an `<a href>` to HuggingFace. **That is a browser download: it lands on whatever laptop has the panel open, under the repo's own filename.** Wrong folder, wrong name — and exactly the failure the server-side downloader was built to prevent. It simply had no way to be reached for the one job it exists for. ★ **Third time in a week** for this shape (the Updates card, its pack list, now this): *a feature that is built, routed and tested is not shipped until something in the UI calls it.*
- **Fixed:** missing rows carry **fetch to rig**, POSTing with `name` = the **declared** filename. Load-bearing, not cosmetic — a repo can publish an 80-character checkpoint name a bundle renames (the Qwen multiple-angles LoRAs), and a file saved under the repo's name would still read as missing **and** be invisible to a `lora` dropdown's `optionsFilter`.
- ★ **The browser link stays but is relabelled by WHERE IT LANDS** — *“to my computer”*, not *“download”*. It is the only route for a gated repo, but called “download” it read as the way to install a missing model, which is how the owner hit this.
- **Verified end to end in headless Chrome against a genuinely missing file** (the v2 angles LoRA held aside): chip reports *1 model missing*, the panel names it, the button queues it, and it returns **byte-exact (318,819,792)** in `models/loras` under the **declared** name with nothing left under the repo's name. Zero console errors.
- ⚠ **Gap found and NOT fixed, worth knowing on a fresh rig:** a model a bundle declares but that is only reachable through a **`lora` dropdown** (the v1 angles checkpoint) **never reports as missing** — the readiness chip reads the graph, and a dropdown option appears in no graph. The dropdown then silently offers only what is installed. Harmless today; it means "0 models missing" does not promise every dropdown option is present.

**The two Qwen-Image-Edit camera-angle bundles removed — and in a REMOVAL, an observed load is a circular protection (owner, 2026-10-12)** — owner: *"we can remove Qwen Image Edit — character camera angle and scene camera angle and the associated models if they are not used by some of our other workflows"*. 63 → **61 bundles**. Their job is now done by [image_edit_qwen_2_1_multiple_angles](workflows/image_edit_qwen_2_1_multiple_angles/) on the newer 2.1 base — one card instead of two, the angle picked rather than typed.

- ★★ **Of the 68.13 GB those two bundles declare, only 20.31 GB was theirs.** Reading the metas literally would have taken `qwen_image_edit_2511_bf16` (**38.05 GB**), `qwen_2.5_vl_7b_fp8_scaled` (8.74) and the 2511 Lightning LoRA — all loaded by `utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale` on every job — plus `qwen_image_vae`, which six krea/PiD bundles load. **Exactly the Marigold shape**, caught by [exclusive.cjs](tools/model-provenance/README.md) before anything moved.
- ★★★ **The four that WERE theirs were held by a CIRCULAR signal, and this is the new lesson.** With the two bundles ignored, the only thing still claiming them was **`observedAt`** — the model access log — and the run that produced those observations is the library sweep that ran *these very bundles*. CLAUDE.md already says the log *"can only ever PROTECT"*, which is right for a **prune** (absence is not disuse). **In a REMOVAL it inverts: presence is not need, because the only thing that ever opened the weight may be the thing being removed.** Left unexamined, every weight a removed bundle ever ran is permanently unprunable and nothing is ever exclusive. The sweep stamps attribution per bundle, so the circularity is checkable — and must be checked.
- ★★ **Checking it turned up a second attribution fault.** Three of the four showed a *surviving* observer: `video_edit_bernini_r_video_editing_ref_autoprompt` — a **Wan 2.2** video bundle that names none of them (its api.json and template are pure Wan 2.2 + gemma4 + umt5). The sweep attributes weights **by time window** and that bundle runs **18–19 minutes**, so its window absorbed a neighbour's trailing access-log lines. ★ **A long-running bundle can be credited with a weight it cannot load** — the mirror of the known "a cached weight leaves no trace" direction, and the reason `observed during X` is a lead, never a verdict.
- **Four independent reads agreed before anything moved:** `exclusive.cjs`, the `/admin/models/usage` engine, per-bundle sweep attribution across all 7 sweep files, and a direct grep of the one surviving "observer" — then the move itself re-derived usage and would have refused any file still claimed.
- **MOVED TO QUARANTINE, NOT DELETED** — `J:\_model_quarantine6-10-12T08-26-23-704Z`, 4 files / **20.31 GB**, restorable from the Quarantine card. Verified after: the four shared weights are still on disk, **all 61 bundles resolve every model they need**, suite green.
- **Also committed, on its own:** the owner validated six bundles through the Validate button (both 2.1 adapters, both 2.1 ControlNets, both FastVideo H3) — their judgement, so it is not folded into a commit of mine.

## Latest progress (2026-10-10)

**A camera-control LoRA, and a second way a Qwen adapter can map (owner, 2026-10-10)** — owner: *"I would also like to have the same kind of developpent for this one"* ([akhaliq/Qwen-Image-2.1-Multiple-Angles-LoRA](https://huggingface.co/akhaliq/Qwen-Image-2.1-Multiple-Angles-LoRA)). New **[image_edit_qwen_2_1_multiple_angles](workflows/image_edit_qwen_2_1_multiple_angles/)** (experimental, **9 s warm / 13 s cold / 16.21 GB** at 512², 40 steps): one photo in, the same subject seen from a chosen camera position out — 12 azimuths × 4 heights × a close-up variant.

- ★★ **192 of 192 modules map — through a DIFFERENT route from the relight LoRA two days ago, and that is the thing worth keeping.** This adapter's keys carry a **`diffusion_model.` prefix** and address the **fused** `img_mlp.gate_up`; the QwenImage branch of `comfy/lora.py` (:326) registers *neither* — it takes a bare `transformer_blocks.` prefix and **replaces** `gate_up` with its two halves, so `gate_up` is not a key_map entry at all. They map instead through the **generic block at the top of `model_lora_keys_unet`** (:186-192), which registers every model weight under its own `diffusion_model.…` name for *all* models. ★ So the two Qwen adapters on this rig reach the same UNET by opposite paths: the relight one needs the QwenImage split (unfused halves, bare prefix), this one needs the generic prefix (fused, full width). **Checking "does this LoRA fit" by reading only the model-specific branch would have condemned this one.** Verified on shapes rather than names: model `gate_up` is [24576, 4096] and this LoRA's `gate_up` B is **[24576, 64]** — the full fused width, not a half.
- ★★★ **The prompt is assembled IN THE GRAPH, because the trigger is a silent no-op if it is missing.** The adapter answers only to `<mva> {azimuth}, {elevation}[ close-up]`, and a prompt without `<mva>` renders a perfectly plausible picture with the camera never having moved — no error, nothing to notice. So two `select`s feed a `StringConcatenate` chain whose first operand is the literal `"<mva>"`: **the trigger is structural rather than something a student has to remember**, and the 96 framings are all the documented spelling. ★ The general rule: *when an adapter has a mandatory trigger token, put it in the graph, not in a default prompt a student can edit.* A default is a suggestion; a literal is a guarantee.
- ★ **Close-up rides on the elevation list rather than its own dropdown, and the reason is a real schema limit:** a plain `select` **cannot carry display labels** — `optionLabels` is set only by `realtimeBus` for `lora` params and is not in the zod `ExposedParameter` — so a separate framing dropdown would have had to offer a **blank** option meaning "not a close-up". Eight readable elevations beat four plus a blank.
- ★ **The reference is normalised to ~512² before use** (`ImageScaleToTotalPixels`, exposed). The card's scope note says it was trained at 512² and larger canvases "were not trained on", and in this graph **the output follows image 1** — so an uncropped phone photo would have run the adapter far outside its training size with nothing saying so. The sibling's empty-latent/`ComfySwitchNode`/`ResolutionSelector` trio is dropped: the subject photo is required here, so that branch only existed for the "no picture at all" case whose size fields it then has to apologise for in two labels.
- ★★ **Verified by looking, four renders on one seed and one photo — and the control render is the honest part.** v2 @1.0 *back view* is a genuine back (no face, hair from behind, character and clothes held); v2 @1.0 *right side view* is a clean profile, so the angle control answers a specific position rather than one baked pose; v1 @1.0 is flatter and softer than v2, matching the card's own v1-vs-v2 claim. ⚠ **But v2 @0.0 — the base model on the same seed — also produces a back view.** On this subject the adapter is not the only thing that can turn an object around, which the card's step-0 samples would not lead you to expect. **Recorded rather than glossed:** the defensible claim is a controlled, repeatable camera position across a documented 96-framing grid, not that the base cannot turn anything. ★ Same shape as the Viggle lesson — the A/B exists to find out, not to confirm.
- **Both releases are installed and switchable in one field** (the `lora` dropdown with `optionsFilter: 'Qwen21-MultipleAngles'`): **v2 step 1500 rank 64** (the author's recommendation) and **v1 step 1000 rank 32**, renamed on download from the repo's 80-character checkpoint filenames. The repo publishes ten checkpoints per version plus sample grids; only these two are worth disk.
- **Licence worth stating on the card:** the adapter is **Apache 2.0** — unusually permissive here — but the Qwen-Image-2.1 base is Qwen Research, so the combination stays research/evaluation.

## Latest progress (2026-10-08)

**The two Qwen 2.1 ControlNet bundles run again — and the fix was two lines in our own pack, not the flag the worklist named; plus an Updates card, because an update that changed nothing read exactly like one that worked (owner, 2026-10-08)** — owner: *"I'll need you to look at Qwen2.1 draw from edges and draw from depth. I updated comfy and updated the node packs… I think we need to be able from the admin panel to update comfyUI and update the node packs individually."*

### ★★★ The aimdo failure was ours, and `--disable-dynamic-vram` would have been a workaround that cost real capability

`image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny` and `…_control_depth` are **clean again**: canny **17 s warm / 23.56 GB**, depth **21 s / 24.17 GB** — within a second and a gigabyte of the 2026-09-25 figures (16 s / 22.56, 19 s / 24.07), so the fix costs nothing. Both pictures were **opened, not scored**: canny traces the control image's edges exactly while the prompt pushes the material (a car's outline rendered as coastal stone), depth keeps its volume and massing and invents a photoreal render. Dynamic VRAM stays **on**.

- ★★★ **The mechanism, which is not what the worklist said.** [ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet](https://github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet)'s `single_block` patch added `img + hints[idx] * scale` **inside the model's per-block malloc scope** — and `control_layers = list(range(0, 32, 2))`, so **a hint exists on every SECOND block**. comfy-aimdo records ONE allocation pattern for that scope and replays it for every block, so block 0 allocated what block 1 did not, the compiled pattern stopped matching, and the *next* block's scope close raised `aimdo memory compile error` at `malloc_graph.py:16`. The traceback pointed at `comfy/ldm/qwen_image21/model.py:387`, the base model's own `prefetch_queue_pop` — which is why it read as ComfyUI's bug.
- **The fix is the escape hatch the model's own forward already uses**: `comfy.model_prefetch.pause_malloc_graph()` around the add, exactly as `model.py:391` wraps its prefix-cache work. Import-guarded, so the pack still loads on ComfyUI 0.37.x, which has no malloc graph to pause.
- ★★ **`--disable-dynamic-vram` was the wrong lever, and this is the lesson.** The 2026-10-07 worklist recorded *"the switch is one flag"* with a warning to measure the VRAM trade first. That warning was right and the flag was still wrong: turning dynamic VRAM off is what stops this 32 GB card streaming the 38–39 GB Qwen Edit bundles, so it would have traded a two-line bug in our own code for a capability loss across the library. **A failure that surfaces inside a vendor's component is not evidence that the vendor's component is the thing to switch off** — the stack frame where an error is *raised* and the code that *violated the contract* are routinely different, and here they were 150 lines and one repository apart.
- ★★ **The fix is a MAINTENANCE FIX, not a hand-edit on the rig** (owner's call: *"it touches the comfyUI install — on custom nodes but still the comfyUI install — so for clarity and portability the fix to custom nodes should be a maintenance fix"*). A node pack lives in the install, not in this repo, so an edit typed into one exists on exactly one machine: invisible to ComfyQ's history, lost by a re-clone or a Manager reinstall, and absent from every other rig imaged from this drive. So the patch text lives in **[fix_custom_node_patches.py](tools/maintenance/fix_custom_node_patches.py)** and the install is brought up to it — a new **Re-apply the custom-node fixes** button under Maintenance. **Proved by reverting the hand-edit and letting the script put it back**, then: run twice → *"already applied"*; pack rewritten under the patch → **FAILED with the anchor named and exit 1**, never a silent no-op (the shape CLAUDE.md already warned about for the ID-V2V repair step); pack not installed → *"nothing to fix"*, not an error; and the patched file is `ast.parse`d before it is written, so a bad edit cannot break ComfyUI's next import.
- ★ **A patched pack reads as dirty to git, and that is the right outcome:** the new node-pack updater refuses to pull over uncommitted changes, so a local fix cannot be silently discarded by an update. If that pack is ever updated deliberately: commit or stash in the clone, pull, then press the button again.
- Both bundles are still `experimental: true`. They were verified by eye on 2026-09-25 and again today, but clearing the flag is the owner's sign-off, not a side effect of a fix.

### The library re-swept on 0.39.2: **61 of 61, the first fully clean pass**

Updated to **v0.39.2** through the new button (`0.39.1 → 0.39.2`, backup branch written, nothing stashed) and re-swept the whole library: **61 bundles, 2 h 30 m (07:55 → 10:25), 0 failed, 0 produced nothing, 0 outputs flagged** black/flat/tiny/empty, **150 distinct weights observed**. Every previous pass had failures — the last two were 58/3 and 59/2, and the failures *were* these two ControlNet bundles.

- ★ **The fix holds across the version bump rather than depending on it:** canny 17 s / 23.38 GB and depth 21 s / 24.2 GB on 0.39.2, the same figures they gave on 0.39.1.
- **The 0.39.1 → 0.39.2 bump costs nothing measurable.** Warm times and VRAM move within the run-to-run noise already recorded here (the biggest mover, `video_wan22_4_key_frames` 79 → 51 s, and the Bernini editors at ±8%, are both inside known variance).
- Both Gemma captioners answered (**1195 and 1175 characters**), so the regression the 2026-10-07 sweep caught stays fixed.
- ⚠ **The 9 stray text tiles are unchanged and still a real student-facing defect** — `"150"`, `"3"`, `"8"`, `"11"`, LivePortrait's three tensor shapes — correctly tagged rather than dropped, on 7 bundles whose in-path `PreviewAny` nodes cannot simply be pruned.
- ★ **`utility_pid_image_upscale` kept its hand-measured runtime**: its progress bar counts tiles so the automatic load/generate split under-reports it, and the sweep's `source: "manual"` guard left it alone. Verified, not assumed — it is the one bundle of 61 whose runtime.json the sweep did not rewrite.
- ⚠ **Still open, unchanged:** LivePortrait reports `vramPeakGb` **1.00** against siblings at 10–30 GB. Implausible, and still unexamined — most likely a measurement problem rather than a bundle that genuinely runs in a gigabyte.

### ★★ "I updated ComfyUI" — and the update had correctly done nothing

Checked before touching anything, and the answer reframed the whole request. `update_comfyui_stable.bat` **ran, worked, and changed nothing**, which the reflog shows exactly: at **2026-10-07 21:14** it checked out `master` (87c32827), pulled, and checked the newest tag back out — **still `v0.39.1`**. `update.py --stable` lands on the newest *tag*, master had simply moved on untagged, `comfy-aimdo` is **pinned at 0.5.5 by `current_requirements.txt`** so it cannot move without a release, and the FunControlNet pack was already at `origin/main`'s tip with no newer commit to take. So the belief that the failure would now be fixed rested on an update that could not have changed it.

- ★★★ **The generalisable fault is the reporting, not the updater.** It printed *Done*. **An updater that says "Done" after changing nothing is indistinguishable from one that worked**, and a whole plan was built on the difference. Every answer the new card gives therefore carries the version **before and after** and says *"nothing moved"* in those words, and says *"nothing has been asked of the network yet"* rather than showing a reassuring green nothing.
- ⚠ **And there IS a newer release now — `v0.39.2`** — published after that attempt. Reported, not taken: a minor bump changes what all 61 bundles run and would want a sweep behind it.

### The Updates card — ComfyUI and each node pack, from Manage ComfyUI

New [updater.js](server/maintenance/updater.js) + [InstallUpdates.jsx](client/src/components/admin/InstallUpdates.jsx), the **first card under Maintenance**. ★★ It first shipped beside the Node packs card under Manage ComfyUI, on the reasoning that *"can these bundles run?"* and *"is any of it out of date?"* are questions about the same install — and the owner could not find it: *"I don't see the feature to update node packs"*. Maintenance is where this file said updating belonged before that tab had anything in it, and it is where they looked. **Where a feature goes is settled by where someone reaches for it, not by what it is adjacent to.** The Node packs card now points at it, because its own "N can be updated" line promised updating and offered none. Routes: `GET /admin/updates` (no network — it is called on every page load and these rigs are routinely on a LAN that cannot reach github), then **adminGate**'d `POST /admin/updates/{check,comfyui,comfyui-deps,pack,stop,forget}`.

- ★ **ComfyUI is updated through the portable's OWN `update/update.py --stable`**, not a git sequence of ours — it stashes local changes, writes a `backup_branch_<date>`, and can replace itself, and reimplementing that would be inventing risk on a drive cloned to several rigs. The `update_new.py` self-update dance is mirrored too, since skipping it is how an updater quietly stops being able to update.
- ★★ **A pack is pulled `--ff-only`, and never while its tree is dirty.** Not a precaution in principle: `ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet` is a working copy the owner develops in place, so a pull that merged or discarded would eat their own commits. A dirty tree is **worded as a state, not a fault** — *"it has uncommitted changes (nodes.py) — commit or stash them first. This is a working copy, and a pull must not decide that for you."*
- ★ **The folder is matched against what is on disk, never path-joined from the request** — it would otherwise reach git as a directory argument straight from a browser. Five traversal shapes are pinned by tests.
- ★ **The incoming commits are listed before you pull**, because a node-pack update changes what a student's next job runs.
- ⚠ **A pack that could not be reached is reported as unknown, never as up to date.** One of them is, on this rig.
- ⚠ **`current_requirements.txt` has its own gate, naming the pin by name.** The portable's pinned list asks for bare `torch`, `torchvision`, `numpy` and `transformers`, and **this install is deliberately held at torch 2.8.0+cu128 for the Pixal3D and TRELLIS2 packs** — so that button refuses until the risk is acknowledged, and the acknowledgement is a separate deliberate tick, never derived from the presence of the danger (the shape that made `allowLow` and `acceptRisky` decorative twice).
- **Refuses in student mode**, because an update changes what the next job runs and is not live until ComfyUI restarts. **Nothing here restarts ComfyUI** — that stays the operator's call.
- **Checking is a background sweep the UI polls**, not an awaited request: one fetch per git pack is twenty round trips, four at a time, and a request held open for two minutes reads as a hung panel.

- ★★ **A card that renders as NOTHING while it loads is indistinguishable from a feature that is not there** — which is the other half of why it could not be found. `InstallUpdates` returned `null` until its fetch resolved, and the pack rows only appeared after someone pressed Check. Both are fixed: the card shows a shell while loading, and **every installed pack is listed on page load with its own (inert) Update button** — 20 clones here — so the capability is visible before the network is touched. Not being able to say whether a pack is BEHIND is no reason to hide the pack.
- ★ **And the first attempt at that listing made the request take 3.1 s**, during which the card still rendered as nothing. `packStates({fetch:false})` reads no network but runs ~6 git subprocesses per pack, which is 264 of them across 44 packs. The page-load route is now **filesystem only (~250 ms)**; the per-pack commit, dirty state and behind-count arrive with the check that was going to touch the network anyway. ★ *"No network" is not the same as "cheap".*

**What the first real check found on this rig** (44 packs): **`comfyui-manager` is 10 commits behind**; **`ComfyUI-HR-Endless-Sampler` has an uncommitted local edit to `gemma4.py`** that nothing had recorded — someone patched a pack in place and it would be lost by any Manager update; **`Pixal3D-ComfyUI`'s remote is unreachable**; 16 clones are level; and **24 of the 44 are not clones at all**, so they say why they cannot be updated instead of offering a button that could not work.

- **Tests:** new [updater.test.js](server/maintenance/updater.test.js), **77 checks** in `test:storyboard`, building **real git repositories** under the OS temp dir rather than stubbing git — including an up-to-date clone, a clone with local edits, a copy with no `.git`, and an upstream that moves between two reads. ★ **It caught a bug in its own subject within a minute**: tracked edits were parsed from `git status --porcelain`, whose first two columns are the status code and whose common case has a **leading space**, so the shared `_gitSync` helper's `.trim()` shifted the filename and `" M seed.txt"` came back as `"eed.txt"`. Now read from `git diff --name-only HEAD` + `git ls-files --others`, which emit bare paths. ★ The fixture also had to be corrected rather than the code: it asserted the dirty-tree refusal on a repo with no vendor updater beside it, so the refusal it got was the right one for the wrong reason.

### ★★ A field in a card, not saved by that card's Save button

Owner: *"I see that it is marked that no hf token is set in the maintenance tab, but I put one in manage comfyUI and saved the settings. I think a hook is missing."* Nothing was wired wrong — the route, the `hasHfToken` refresh and the downloader's read were all correct, and `PUT /admin/comfy` takes a fixed allow-list of keys so **Save settings could not have wiped a stored token either**. The HuggingFace token input simply sat *inside* the ComfyUI Settings card, among a dozen fields that card's single **Save settings** button persists, while being the one field it did not. The only thing saying so was a comment in the source.

- **Fixed by making behaviour match appearance:** `savePaths` now also persists a typed token, through its own adminGate'd route — the credential still never travels on the settings route, only the click is shared — and the field says that either button works. There was already a precedent in the same function: the scan folders live on another route and are saved alongside for exactly this reason.
- ★ **This is the same family as the interlocks satisfied by their own UI** (`allowLow`, `acceptRisky`): a control whose behaviour contradicts its appearance. The earlier two lied by being too permissive; this one lied by doing nothing. **A field that looks governed by a shared Save button must be governed by it, or must not sit inside it.**
- Verified by driving the exact failing sequence in headless Chrome — type a token, press *Save settings* — badge `not set` → `set`, toast *"ComfyUI paths, HuggingFace token saved"*, and with nothing typed the toast correctly stays *"ComfyUI paths saved"* rather than claiming a token was stored. The test token was cleared afterwards.

### A relighting LoRA, set up end to end (owner, 2026-10-08)

Owner: *"I want to test this relighting lora … can you setup everything with a comfyUI template usable in comfyUI and an experimental workflow for comfyQ, download the models etc."* New **[image_edit_qwen_2_1_relight_composite](workflows/image_edit_qwen_2_1_relight_composite/)** (experimental, **17 s warm / 47 s cold / 18.83 GB**): paste an object onto a background, and it re-lights the object to match the plate — key light, speculars, and the contact shadow that stops it looking stuck on.

- ⚠ **Inspected before anything was downloaded, and that was not ceremony:** the previous `RunningHubAI/rh-qwen-image-2.1-lora` repo looked equally generic and held two sexualised body-reshaping LoRAs (2026-10-06). This one is genuine — 光影溶图 ("light-and-shadow blending"), rank 32, BF16, no DoRA, 2000 steps on `Qwen/Qwen-Image-2.1` at ~1 MP, trigger word `pengyu`. Its **safetensors header was read over HTTP range requests**, so none of that cost a 160 MB download.
- ★★ **224 of 224 modules map, 0 unmapped** — and the reason to check is that it looks like it should not. The adapter addresses the **unfused** `img_mlp.gate_layer`/`proj` while this rig's UNET is **fused** as `img_mlp.gate_up`: the same shape that first read as "this LoRA does not fit" on the Viggle adapter. `comfy/lora.py:331` splits the fused weight for exactly this, and the halves line up (model `gate_up` [24576, 4096], half 12288; LoRA `gate_layer` B [12288, 32]). Its bare `transformer_blocks.` prefix is the *native* QwenImage LoRA form, mapped directly at :335.
- **Derived from `image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit`, not hand-written** — that sibling already runs this base with reference images, including the switch that lets "no picture at all" fall back to a plain latent. 16 reference slots down to 3, plus a `LoraLoaderModelOnly` between the UNET loader and the 2.1 cache node.
- ★ **The inherited node ids had to go.** `api-to-ui` refused the template with *20 round-trip differences* because the colon ids carried over from the sibling's flattened subgraph (`459:451`) do not survive ComfyUI's re-export — it renumbers to plain integers. In an **api-first** bundle those paths mean nothing, so they were renumbered (`459:451` → `451`, meta keys with them) and the template then generated **round-trip identical, 15 nodes**. The refusal was right: a template that cannot be compared is a template that can silently drift.
- ★★ **Verified by looking, on one seed.** Adapter 1.0 vs 0.0 differ by **14.58/255**, so it is loading and acting rather than silently inert — worth measuring, because the access-log recorder did NOT list the LoRA among the weights the run opened (`LoraLoaderModelOnly` resolves through `get_full_path_or_raise`, which the recorder's wrapper does not cover — a gap in the recorder, not evidence about the run). On a cartoon figure pasted onto a dark castle: **with** the adapter the figure is pulled down to the scene's gloomy key light and the white cut-out fringe at its feet disappears; **without** it the figure stays brightly lit and still reads as pasted on.
- ★ **A trap found by testing the obvious thing and having it fail.** Object in Image 1 + background in Image 2 with the default prompt returned **the object alone on white at the object's size** — because the result always follows Image 1, and a prompt that never says `<image1>`/`<image2>` gives the model nothing to connect. With the **background in Image 1** and those names in the prompt it composites correctly, at the plate's size, lit to match. Both halves matter, so both are in the description rather than left to be discovered. ★ The first failure was nearly written up as "the two-image form does not work"; retrying it fairly is what made it a documented rule instead of a wrong limitation.

### Conventions this added

- ★★ **The frame where an error is raised is not evidence of which component is wrong.** `aimdo memory compile error` came out of a vendor library, through ComfyUI's own model file, from a contract our own node pack had broken two files away. Before switching a vendor feature off, find the code that violated its invariant.
- ★★ **An operation that can legitimately change nothing must say so in those words.** "Done" on a no-op update cost a day. State before, state after, and name the no-op case.
- ★★ **Where a feature goes is settled by where someone reaches for it, not by what it is adjacent to** — and a card that renders as nothing while it loads reads as a feature that was never built.
- ★★ **A field that looks governed by a shared Save button must be governed by it, or must not sit inside that card.** A source comment is not an affordance.
- ★★ **When an adapter has a mandatory trigger token, put it in the GRAPH, not in a default prompt.** A default is a suggestion a student can edit away; a literal feeding a `StringConcatenate` is a guarantee. Forgetting `<mva>` costs no error and no clue — just a plausible picture the adapter never touched.
- ★ **A LoRA that does not match a model's own key branch may still map through the generic one.** `model_lora_keys_unet` registers every weight under its own `diffusion_model.…` name for ALL models before any model-specific branch runs, so "it does not fit the QwenImage branch" is not "it does not fit". Check shapes, not names.
- ★★★ **A model's TRAINING resolution is not the resolution to feed its reference.** The reference carries the subject; starve it and the model falls back on what it was trained on. ★ General tell: **when an output looks like the training distribution rather than the input, the input is too thin to compete** — before blaming the adapter, give it more to work from.
- ★★ **An author's "recommended checkpoint" is recommended against the base THEY trained on.** Check the precision/build: this fleet runs int8-convrot repacks, and an adapter trained on the matching build can beat the newer one trained on bf16.
- ★★★ **A feature that is built, routed and tested is not shipped until something in the UI calls it.** Three times in a week: the Updates card, its pack list, and the model downloader — whose only affordance was a browser link that did the exact thing it was written to prevent. Grep for a call to the route before calling it done.
- ★★★ **In a REMOVAL, an observed load proves nothing — the only thing that opened the weight may be the thing being removed.** The access log protects a *prune* (absence is not disuse) and inverts for a *removal*. Check which bundle the observation is attributed to before treating it as a reason to keep.
- ★★ **`observed during X` is a lead, not a verdict.** Weight attribution is by time window, so a bundle that runs for 18 minutes can be credited with a neighbour's weight it cannot load. Confirm against the graph before believing it in either direction.
- ★ **Never conclude "up to date" from a failed fetch.** Unknown and current are different answers, and these rigs are often on a LAN that cannot reach github.

## Latest progress (2026-10-07)

**A ten-lens adversarial review of the day before, then the one button that runs the whole library and writes a report you can paste back (owner, 2026-10-07)** — owner: *"do a full review of our latest work"*, then *"when we run the whole library it would be nice to have a text report that I could feed you back to fix everything"*, then *"I want a human to also be able to inspect the results, in the cards … the ingredients used (prompt, parameters, images etc.) and the result and their path to the disk"*.

### The review — 47 agents, 26 findings, 24 survived refutation

Ten independent lenses over 2026-10-06's eighteen commits (prune data-loss · confidence regressions · quarantine recoverability · the maintenance runner · the node-pack installer · the pack report · downloads · the audit scripts · UI trust · whether the claims written down are true), each finding handed to refuters told to kill it, plus the two completeness critics that died last time. **26 raised, 24 survived, 2 refuted, 6 coverage gaps.** Five were armed and were fixed the same day; the rest are a worklist.

- ★★★ **THE ONE THAT MATTERED: the prune tool was quoting the audit's OVERTURNED verdict.** The model audit records its first pass in `classify` and the verdict it reached **after challenging that pass** in `final` — `build_csv.py:255` reads `final or classify` for exactly that reason. `auditVerdicts` read only `classify`. **Measured: 62 rows disagree, and every one of the 17 that were unused had been overturned from DELETE toward KEEP or REVIEW — 93.10 GB, of which 12 sat at `medium`, the tier the prune route does NOT gate.** Wan21-WanMove (15.28), Wan2_1-T2V-14B (13.84), z_image_turbo (11.46), wan2.2_ti2v_5B (9.31), three SDXL checkpoints. The audit's own correction was being used as the argument for deleting the files it had decided to save. **Ungated surface after the fix: 24 files / 155.41 GB → 11 / 75.81 GB.**
- ★★ **The blind-scan brake could never fire for the case it was written for.** It counted `scanned.configured`, but the route appends ComfyUI's own user-workflows dir to that array before handing it over, so the count was never zero on any path that can move a file. It now counts what the **admin** configured. ⚠ **An empty list is the NORMAL state of a freshly cloned rig** (config.json is gitignored), and with it empty 5 more files / 69.32 GB read as unused carrying no `scan-incomplete` reason at all.
- ★★ **A safety interlock satisfied by its own UI, for the second time.** The server refuses a pack's risky `requirements.txt` "until you say so explicitly"; the client sent `acceptRisky: risky.length > 0` — true in precisely the case the guard exists for. Now a checkbox naming what gets replaced. ★ Same shape as the `allowLow` bug found on 2026-10-05: **an interlock whose condition is derived from the danger it guards is not an interlock.**
- ★★ **The downloads card re-fetched in an unbounded loop** — `rows` was a dependency of the effect that *sets* `rows`. **Measured at 5,624 requests in 10 idle seconds (~560/s) aimed at a rig serving a class**, for as long as a laptop had the tab open. Depends on a boolean now: 2 requests in the same 10 seconds, both the pre-existing lanes poll.
- **Also fixed:** the prune route drops its caches *after* the move as well as before, so the card no longer redraws byte-identical and invites a second click.
- ★★ **Two corrections to this file's own claims, which is where being wrong costs most.** (1) `familyKey` was **not** cross-checked against safetensors metadata — the audit's BUILD clusters come from `variants.core()`, which is filename tokens, so two name-based heuristics were compared with each other and agreement means consistency, not correctness. **An independent check (tensor shapes off the files) still has not been done.** (2) The provenance figure "268 of 274" was stale; the harvest reports **262 of 268**.
- **Refuted, and the refutations were right:** an empty `familyKey` predates the work and is gated anyway; `adminGate` failing open is a documented five-month-old property. ⚠ **But the refuters measured something worth keeping:** `curl http://<lan-ip>:3000/admin/config` answers 200 from the LAN with no credentials, and Vite proxies every `/admin` sub-path. **Set an admin password before a prune session** — today one student with the URL could empty a quarantine batch.
- ⚠ **Not fixed, the top of the worklist:** the prune modal still says "cannot be undone" and "frees X GB" — both false, it is a MOVE — and the quarantine it writes to has **no UI at all**, so the space is reclaimed by hand, which is the irreversible step performed outside the tool built to make it reversible. Then: the node-pack Install button offers the wrong repo for 162 of 187 mapped classes (including cloning ComfyUI itself into `custom_nodes`); a download is renamed and marked `done` in three states where the bytes are wrong; **both "modifies the install" buttons are inert** (`repair_all.py` / `portablize_paths.py` still resolve paths from the `_maintenance` folder that move deleted).

### Run the whole library, and audit the result

**New [librarySweep.js](server/benchmark/librarySweep.js) + the card in Maintenance.** Runs every workflow once with sample inputs — **about 2 h for 61 bundles on the 5090** — and answers the two questions nothing else can: *does each bundle still work*, and *which model files the library actually opens*.

- ★★ **The output is MEASURED, not trusted.** This rig has twice shipped a bundle that reported success and saved black images, and once passed a broken 4-step default on a sharpness statistic that cannot tell detail from structured noise. New **[tools/maintenance/output_stats.py](tools/maintenance/output_stats.py)** runs through ComfyUI's own python (PIL, numpy, cv2, av) and reports size, luminance, spread, frame count; anything **black / flat / tiny / empty / single-frame** is named for a human to open. **Verified the detector FIRES**: all-black → `[black,flat]`, one flat colour → `[flat]`, 16×16 → `[tiny]`, zero bytes → `[empty]`, real noise → `[]`.
- ★ **It runs through AdminCalibrator, one bundle at a time, because that is what masks each workflow's `disabledPerfFlags`** and restarts ComfyUI when they change. Sweeping the Qwen edit bundles with Sage on would produce exactly the black output the sweep exists to catch, and would blame the bundle. It therefore **refuses in student mode** with the reason, can be stopped between workflows, and restores a `source: "manual"` runtime (PiD).
