- ★★ **The recorder was installed from AdminCalibrator ALONE**, so a rig in student mode — how a rig spends its day — recorded nothing and the access log stayed empty. `ensureInstalled` now runs at the single spawn choke point every ComfyUI goes through, lanes included. **That fix is the only reason the first sweep's model observations exist at all.**

**The text report** (`GET /admin/library-sweep/report.txt`, Copy report / .txt on the card). Design rule: **the reader has no access to the machine, so everything needed to act on a line is on the line.** Measured at full scale — **61 bundles → 241 lines, 17.8 KB, ~4,500 tokens**, which pastes into a chat whole.
- Each problem carries its untruncated error, the measurements that condemned it, and ★ **the flags ComfyUI was ACTUALLY running with next to the flags that bundle disowns**, with `<-- NOT MASKED` and the likely cause in words. That one line is the report's reason to exist: a black render under sage from a bundle declaring `use_sage_attention` has a known cause and is not a broken graph.
- Ordered by what needs a decision, never alphabetically — failures, black/empty, produced-nothing, suspect, then one line each for the clean. **A report whose first screen is green is a report nobody reads to the end.**
- Two sections make it prune-grade: every weight the sweep was **seen** to open (naming the sole user where there is one), and a cross-reference that loudly flags any file the scan calls unused that a workflow actually opened. Both repeat that **never opened is NOT proof of unneeded**.

**Human inspection in the card** (owner's third ask). Each row opens Ingredients + Result.
- ★★ **Ingredients are the EFFECTIVE values, and that distinction is the feature.** Calibration sends only the media it stages and a fresh seed; everything else keeps the api.json literal — **so the prompt that actually ran is almost never in `paramValues`**, and a list built from those alone would show the most useful ingredient as blank. Each row says where its value came from, and a media input carries the absolute path of the real file plus the namespaced copy ComfyUI read.
- ★ **The result is SHOWN.** A luma figure is a proxy; the picture, clip or audio renders inline from ComfyQ's own `/images` route, with dimensions, luma, size, flags, **absolute disk path** and a download link. One row open at a time (sixty full-size previews is what made the results grid crawl once).

### Bugs the real data found, which is the argument for running it

- ★★ **`/images` was mounted only inside the student-mode block**, so it 404'd in admin mode — precisely the mode you are in while reading a sweep. The card could describe a result it could not show. Mounted for both modes now; it is read-only and scoped to the output/temp dirs by `_resolveSafe`.
- ★★ **Outputs were matched by `startsWith('bench_<id>_')`, and several bundle ids are PREFIXES of others.** Caught in the first sweep's own files: `image_edit_bernini_r_image_editing` was holding `…_with_reference`'s picture. Anchored on the timestamp that follows the id, in the sweep and the recovery tool.
- ★★★ **Weights were attributed by diffing the access log's NAME SET before and after each run — wrong on every sweep after the first.** The log is append-only and its index keeps each weight's *earliest* sighting, so a set difference reports only weights never loaded on this machine before. Caught **one bundle into the re-run**: a bundle the previous sweep credited with 5 models reported **0**. ⚠ **That is the dangerous direction** — the report cross-references "opened" against the prune list, so a weight wrongly absent reads as *"the sweep found no reason to keep this"* about a file loaded on every job. Now keyed on **time**, which the log already stamps: `namesOpenedBetween(root, from, to)`. **The sweep was stopped at bundle 1 of 61 and restarted rather than spend two hours on a report whose most valuable column was empty.**
- ★ **A text output is not a missing output.** Both Gemma captioners were flagged `NO OUTPUT` for behaving correctly — their result is a caption. **A detector that fires on correct behaviour is how people learn to ignore it.**
- ★ **`librarySweep.test.js` wrote its fixtures into `server/data`**, where a sweep keeps `library-sweep-latest.json` — the file the report route serves. So the route was serving a TEST as this machine's last real sweep, complete with a temp-directory ComfyUI path. `dataDir` is injectable now and the test writes only under its own temp dir.

### ★★ A LIVE REGRESSION the sweep found on its first real run: both Qwen 2.1 ControlNet bundles cannot run

⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-08 — both bundles run again, and the cause named below is wrong in its conclusion.** The wrapper was not what broke the malloc graph: our own `single_block` patch allocated inside the model's per-block scope on every SECOND block. `--disable-dynamic-vram` would have been a workaround with a real cost. Kept for the reasoning trail — see the 2026-10-08 entry at the top.

`image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny` and `image_qwen_image_2_1_control_depth` both die inside sampling with **`RuntimeError: aimdo memory compile error`**, raised at `comfy_aimdo/malloc_graph.py:16` in `_call`, reached via `malloc_graph_pop` → `iterate` → `comfy.sample.sample` → `common_ksampler`. Canny loads all four models first (controlnet-union, qwen3vl_8b, qwen_image_2.1, vae), so the checkpoint and the loaders are fine.

- ★ **Both siblings fail identically, which is the diagnosis.** The annotator is their only difference, so it is the shared custom pack: [ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet](https://github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet) registers a `WrappersMP.DIFFUSION_MODEL` wrapper **and** a `single_block` patch. comfy-aimdo compiles a malloc graph over the diffusion model's execution; a wrapper that shifts the allocation pattern mid-graph breaks that compilation.
- ★★ **It is a version regression, not a bundle bug.** The pack was written 2026-09-25 against **ComfyUI 0.37.0**. This rig now runs **0.39.1 with comfy-aimdo 0.5.5** and announces *"DynamicVRAM support detected and enabled"* at boot. Nobody knew the two bundles had stopped working — which is exactly what the sweep exists to find.
- **The switch is one flag:** `ComfyUI/comfy/cli_args.py:180` **`--disable-dynamic-vram`**, and `enables_dynamic_vram()` (:326) is true by default (it only goes false under `--highvram`, `--gpu-only`, `--novram`, `--cpu`). So confirming it is: relaunch with that flag, run only the canny bundle.
- ⚠ **If it is wired as a per-bundle opt-out, mind the POLARITY.** `requirements.disabledPerfFlags` currently describes opt-in speed-ups ComfyQ ADDS, so "disabled" means omitting an argument. Dynamic VRAM is on by default, so turning it off means ADDING one. `perfFlags.js` needs a table of *how to express OFF* per key, or `flagsFromArgv` will read it backwards — and `flagsFromArgv` has already caused one silent failure (below).
- ⚠ **And check the trade before shipping it:** dynamic VRAM is what lets this 32 GB card run the 38–39 GB Qwen Edit bundles by streaming weights. Turning it off for a bundle may swap one failure for an OOM, so measure those bundles' VRAM with it off first.

### The sweep's own three bugs, all found by running it rather than reading it

- ★★★ **Weights were attributed by diffing the access log's NAME SET before and after each run, which is wrong on every sweep after the first.** The log is append-only and its index keeps each weight's *earliest* sighting, so a set difference reports only weights never loaded on this machine before. Caught **one bundle into the second run**: a bundle the first sweep credited with 5 models reported **0**. ⚠ That is the dangerous direction — the report cross-references "opened" against the prune list, so a weight wrongly absent reads as *"the sweep found no reason to keep this"* about a file loaded on every job. Keyed on **time** now (`namesOpenedBetween`), which the log already stamps.
- ★★ **The perf diagnostic was broken for exactly the five bundles it exists for.** `flagsFromArgv` returns an **object**; calling `.includes(k)` on it raised a TypeError that abandoned the whole `rec.perf` record — and *only* when a bundle declares a flag, because `Array.prototype.filter` never invokes its callback on an empty array. 56 bundles recorded it fine, which is why it looked healthy. ★ **The lesson, twin of "a detector that fires on correct behaviour gets ignored": a detector whose only reachable path is the case it exists for, and which throws there, reports nothing and looks like a clean result.** Any conditional diagnostic needs a test that drives the condition, not the default.
- ★ **A failed bundle recorded no ingredients and no traceback** — backwards on both counts, since a failure is when you most need the prompt, the inputs and the frames. Ingredients are captured regardless of outcome (the service builds them before the submit); the worker now carries `traceback` / `exception_type` / `node_id` from the `execution_error` payload through to the row. Before that, the 120 lines that located the aimdo fault existed only in ComfyUI's stdout, which nothing stores durably.

### Human inspection, and the owner's follow-ups

- ★ **A caption IS the result.** A `description` workflow answers through a `PreviewAny` node as text in the prompt's outputs and touches the filesystem not at all, so the sweep recorded both Gemma captioners as having produced nothing and discarded the answer they exist to give. Read from ComfyUI's history now and appended as an output of `kind: 'text'`; the card shows it inline, and the report **prints it wrapped** rather than measuring it — nobody can judge "did this work" from a character count.
- ★ **Clickable paths go through the host.** A browser will not follow `file://` from an http page, so `POST /admin/reveal` runs `explorer /select` (`open -R` on mac, `xdg-open` on linux). ⚠ **It opens on THE RIG's desktop, not the viewer's** — the panel is routinely opened from a laptop — so the button says *"show on rig"* and a Copy sits beside it. The path is refused unless it is inside a folder ComfyQ already works with: 16 checks, every refusal landing before the spawn. ★ **That test earned itself immediately** — `allowedRoots` had included the install's whole PARENT, making the entire portable folder revealable; narrowed to `python_embeded`.
- **Clear the results** (`POST /admin/library-sweep/clear`): a partial or recovered report reads like a verdict on the library while covering half of it. It never removes the `bench_*` outputs — those are evidence.

### The review's worklist, continued

- ★★ **The prune card had stopped telling the truth in three places at once** — the modal said *Delete* and *"This frees X GB and cannot be undone"*, the toast said *"GB freed"*. A prune is a **MOVE**, so all three were false, and together they are dangerous: you prune to shrink the disk, see free space unchanged, and delete the quarantine by hand in Explorer — performing the one irreversible step outside the tool built to make it reversible. **And there was no quarantine screen at all**, so by hand was the only way. New [ModelQuarantine.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelQuarantine.jsx) drives the three routes that had existed since 2026-10-05 with nothing attached: batches newest first, per-file **Move back**, and an Empty step that names the irreversibility, the size, and the licence-gated refetch risk. It also warns that **a batch travels in a cloned drive image**.
- ★ **The CONFIDENT tier rendered with no explanation at all** — rows printed only `lowers` reasons, and a confident row has none, so the one tier with a bulk-select button showed two filenames and nothing else while its justification sat in JSON. Every reason renders now, doubts first.
- ★ **The Drive-letter swap did not stick.** It rewrites the workflow scan folders in the form (its own comment says why: a folder it misses makes that folder's models read as unused), but they persist through a different route, so *Save settings* wrote the four ComfyUI paths and then the config reload pulled the OLD scan dirs back over the swapped ones. On a cloned rig, that was the route to pruning against a folder that no longer resolves.

### Also

- **A HuggingFace token field exists at last** — `PUT /admin/hf-token` (its own adminGate'd route, write-only, empty clears) and a password input in Manage ComfyUI, where the downloader's own advice had been pointing at nothing. Round-tripped live: set, `hasHfToken` flips, lands in config.json, never in a response, cleared. ★ And the token was being attached on a **substring match of the whole URL**, so a mirror at `hf-cache.example.net/huggingface.co/…` would have been handed someone's credential — parsed now, six cases pinned.
- **No admin link in the student header** (owner): *"I do not want people to be able to jump to admin just like that (I know I should set a password, but still)"*. Verified 0 links and the word "admin" nowhere on the student page, while `/admin` typed directly still loads. **Not a boundary — the password is.**
- ★ **Rig lesson, paid for twice: do not edit `server/` while a long job runs.** `concurrently --kill-others-on-fail` tore down the whole dev script (Vite and ComfyUI with it) mid-session, and nodemon restarts killed the owner's first sweep at bundle 32 of 61 and cleared its in-memory status. **New [tools/maintenance/recover_sweep.cjs](tools/maintenance/recover_sweep.cjs)** rebuilds a lost report from the three traces a run leaves — the rewritten `runtime.json` files, the `bench_*` outputs, and the access log, attributing weights by time window. It recovered **32 bundles / 25m 17s / 57 models observed**, all clean. ⚠ **A bundle that FAILED wrote no runtime.json, so it cannot be recovered** — it reads as "did not run", and the report says so rather than implying the library is clean.
- **Verified for the record: the sweep harms nothing.** 0 model files touched, 0 staged calibration inputs left behind, the only `custom_nodes` change is our own version marker, and ~360 MB of `bench_*` files in the output folder.
- **Tests:** `librarySweep.test.js` **36 checks**, `sweepReport.test.js` **30**, `modelConfidence.test.js` 33 → **35**. All in `test:storyboard`.

### The clean sweep ran end to end — TWICE — 61 bundles, settling at **59 clean / 2 failed** (owner, 2026-10-07)

Owner: *"restart a sweep from scratch with all the features and data, not with some half baked recovered data it is not clear enough"*. The recovered 32-bundle report was cleared and a full run done with every feature in place. **The two failures are one real defect; most of what the runs turned up was a defect in the REPORT, which is the more useful half.**

★ **Two full sweeps ran, and the second is the one on disk.** Mine finished 20:39 — 2h 12m, 99 weights observed, **58 clean / 3 failed**, the third failure being LivePortrait's empty `warmupParams` rather than the bundle (fixing that and re-running it alone took the merged report to 59 / 2). The owner then started another at 21:31 which finished **00:02 — 2h 30m, 86 weights observed, 59 clean / 2 failed outright**, LivePortrait now passing on its own. Figures below name which run they come from where it matters. ⚠ **The second sweep is also what caught the regression in the next section, and the first could not have:** it was the first full run with the new guards live.

- ★★★ **[⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-10-08 — fixed in the pack, not by a flag; see the top entry] A LIVE REGRESSION nobody could have known about: both Qwen 2.1 ControlNet bundles cannot run.** `image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny` and `…_control_depth` die inside sampling with **`RuntimeError: aimdo memory compile error`**, raised at `comfy_aimdo/malloc_graph.py:16` via `malloc_graph_pop` ← `prefetch_queue_pop` ← `qwen_image21/model.py:387`, failing node `459:458 (KSampler)`. Canny loads all four weights first, so the checkpoint and the loaders are fine. **Both siblings fail identically, which IS the diagnosis** — the annotator is their only difference, so the cause is the shared pack [ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet](https://github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet), whose `WrappersMP.DIFFUSION_MODEL` wrapper and `single_block` patch shift the allocation pattern comfy-aimdo compiles a malloc graph over. ★★ **It is a version regression, not a bundle bug:** the pack was written 2026-09-25 against ComfyUI **0.37.0**; this rig now runs **0.39.1 with comfy-aimdo 0.5.5** and announces *"DynamicVRAM support detected and enabled"* at boot. **The switch is one flag** — `comfy/cli_args.py:180` `--disable-dynamic-vram`, with `enables_dynamic_vram()` true by default. ⚠ **Two things to mind before wiring it as a per-bundle opt-out:** the POLARITY is reversed from `disabledPerfFlags` (which disables by *omitting* an argument; this one disables by *adding* one), so `perfFlags.js` needs a table of how to express OFF per key or `flagsFromArgv` reads it backwards; and **measure the trade** — dynamic VRAM is what lets this 32 GB card run the 38–39 GB Qwen Edit bundles by streaming weights, so turning it off may swap one failure for an OOM.
- ★★ **`video_liveportrait_image2video` could never have been calibrated automatically, and the sweep is what asked.** Its `warmupParams` were empty, so the gauge staged the assets dir's median file — `sports_car.png` — into a graph whose first node is a face cropper: *"No face detected in FIRST source image"*. **Fixed by naming real inputs** (`asset:Woman.png` + `asset:d6.mp4`, the video picked by extracting frames and looking at them), verified by a re-run: **29 s, a 128-frame 512×512 clip at 16 fps, luma 129.79 / spread 49.42** — not black, not flat. ★ A June note had said "calibrate via a real booked run, not the auto-gauge" and left it there; `warmupParams` was the answer all along, and is the answer for **any bundle whose input must *contain* something** rather than merely be of the right type.
- ★★★ **ID-V2V's blocker merged upstream ten days ago and nothing noticed.** [docs/comfyui-idv2v.md](docs/comfyui-idv2v.md) said PR #15139 was *"open, not merged"*, that its fix was *"a local 4-file patch on the workshop rig"*, and therefore that *"a ComfyQ bundle assumes a stock ComfyUI"* — yet both ID-V2V bundles ran **clean** (81 frames at 24 fps, 960×640 in 88 s, 960×960 in 188 s). My first reading was that the patch had become unnecessary; checking properly gave the opposite mechanism and the right answer: **`git status --porcelain` on all four formerly-patched files is empty at tag `v0.39.1`**, and `git log -S"ref_pad_image"` names **`5c4d2568`, 2026-09-27, "Support ID-V2V (#15139)"**. The install is *stock* and carries the feature natively, so **any rig on ComfyUI ≥ 0.39.x can serve those two bundles with nothing applied to it.** The `requires-idv2v-patch` tag is gone from both metas and both docs are corrected. ★ **The lesson is about the doc, not the feature: it even carried the command to re-check (`gh api …/pulls/15139`) and nobody ran it.** A doc resting on an external blocker needs a date, a re-check, and something that actually performs it — here the sweep did, by running the bundles and making someone ask why they passed.

### ★★★ The second sweep caught a regression I had just introduced, and the guard I added to stop a false alarm is what hid it

**Both Gemma captioners produced nothing, and the report called them clean.** The 00:02 sweep recorded `outputs: []` for `describe_gemma4_image_description` and `…_video_description` — where the first sweep had their 1403- and 832-character answers. The captions are the entire point of those two bundles.

- **Cause: the ownership guard I added hours earlier.** To stop a caption being credited to the wrong bundle, `_collectTextOutputs` required ComfyUI's history entry to carry this run's `bench_<id>_<ms>` stamp. **That stamp rides on a save node's `filename_prefix` — and a `description` bundle has no save node**, so its prompt contains no stamp anywhere and the guard rejected its own entry. ★ **A guard that demands a token only produced by saving a file, applied to the one workflow class that saves no file: it rejected exactly the case the function exists for.** Fixed by inverting the test — reject an entry carrying **somebody else's** stamp, accept one carrying **none**.
- ★★ **And the reason nobody was told: the exemption that stopped the earlier false alarm.** The first sweep had flagged both captioners `NO OUTPUT` for behaving correctly, so I exempted the `description` category from that counter — in **both** `librarySweep._summary` and `sweepReport.triage`. When the captions then genuinely vanished, both detectors stayed silent, because they had been taught never to speak about that category. **An exemption that silences a false alarm silences the true one too.** The honest form is *"a caption COUNTS as output"* — which it now does, since text lands in `outputs` — so the category clause is gone from both and a captioner with no caption is reported like anything else. ★ This is the twin of the convention already in this file ("a detector that fires on correct behaviour gets ignored"): the fix for a false alarm must narrow what counts as a fault, never narrow who may be accused.
- ★★ **And the fix needed a second guard, because "carries no stamp" is not "is mine".** Accepting any stampless entry would take the **previous** bundle's — and the two captioners are the library's only stampless bundles **and run back to back**, so a second one failing before it queued would publish the first one's answer as its own. ComfyUI stamps every history entry with an `execution_start` timestamp (`status.messages`), so an entry older than this run cannot be ours. Same time-window reasoning that fixed weight attribution earlier the same day.
- ★★ **That one was found because a test reached the LIVE ComfyUI.** `global.fetch` was never stubbed at module level, so on a rig with ComfyUI running the sweep's `/history` call **succeeded**, a fixture bundle inherited the real instance's last caption, and an unrelated assertion failed with a result that had nothing to do with the code under test. ★ **A test whose outcome depends on whether a service happens to be running on the machine is not a test.** `fetch` is now blocked for the whole file and each block stubs what it needs.
- ★ **A third bug rode along, hidden by a test fixture.** The stamp regex was **lazy**, so `bench_video_ltx2_5_i2v_1791…` would be read as the stamp `bench_video_ltx2_5` and then fail to match its own bundle. It went unseen because the fixtures used `_1759` and `_17` — **a short number is not recognised as a stamp at all, so both the match and the no-match case passed for the wrong reason.** Now greedy with `\d{10,}` (what `Date.now()` actually produces) and the fixtures carry real 13-digit stamps.
- **Verified live, not just by test:** a 2-bundle subset re-run restored both captions through the real route — **1319 and 1091 characters, printed in full in the report, neither tagged stray** — and the merge put them back into the 61-row report without disturbing the other 59 (`mergedFrom: 2`, `noOutput: 0`).
- **Tests:** `librarySweep.test.js` 62 → **70**, `sweepReport.test.js` 38 → **39**. ★ One existing assertion had to be **inverted**: it read *"a description bundle with no file is NOT flagged"*, which is precisely the masking written down as a requirement.

### What the sweep's own report got wrong — the more useful half of the findings

- ★★★ **"Opened by only this bundle" was the most dangerous sentence in the report, and it was false.** The MODELS OPENED section printed `only <bundle>` beside each weight — but the recorder logs a **first sighting**, not exclusive use: ComfyUI keeps a model loaded between prompts, so the second bundle wanting the same weight resolves no path and leaves no trace. **20 of 61 rows reported zero models in the first sweep and 23 of 61 in the second, every one of them running straight after a sibling with the same weights** — and that the figure MOVES between two identical runs is itself the proof that it measures cache state rather than usage. `flux-2-klein-base-9b-fp8` read as *"only image_edit_flux2_klein_9b_image_edit"* while four other live bundles load it. ★ **Act on that line while removing a bundle and you delete a weight four others need — the exact failure this whole tool exists to prevent, written by the tool itself.** Now `first seen in …` / `seen in N runs`, with the caching reason spelled out where it would be misread, and `—` instead of `0 models` in the table.
- ★★ **Seven production bundles publish a meaningless text tile to students, and the sweep proved what a June note had only suspected.** `_collectText` in the **student** output path had no filter at all, so any `text` array became a result tile: the Bernini editors show **`"3"`**, **`"8"`**, **`"11"`** (node `76:57:1` taps a combo's line index through a `PreviewAny` — an **IN-PATH** node whose value the graph consumes, so it cannot simply be pruned), Stable Audio shows **`"150"`**, LivePortrait three tensor shapes (**`"1x512x512"`** plus two frame-batch shapes that follow the input, so they read `128x1024x1024` in one sweep and `128x512x512` / `128x480x480` in the next — the strings vary, the defect does not). ★★ **And I was one edit away from hiding it** — the queued fix was "filter incidental numbers out of the report". The rule now, in both the student collector and the sweep: **prose (it contains whitespace) is an answer; beside a real file a whitespace-free string is plumbing; and when text is ALL the run produced it is the answer however short** — so a captioner replying `"yes"` is never swallowed, and no knowledge of the workflow is needed. A stray is **tagged, not dropped**, and both the report and the card name it as the defect it is. New [outputCollector.test.js](server/executor/outputCollector.test.js), **13 checks**, every fixture a real payload shape from this sweep.
- ★★ **A caption was measured instead of printed.** A working captioner's **1403-character** answer appeared in the report as the single word `text` — the row a reader most wants to judge, reduced to a type name. Captions now print wrapped in the RAN CLEAN table, not only for problem rows.
- ★★ **Re-running one bundle erased the other sixty.** A subset run wrote a `…-latest.json` holding only its own rows and the report route served that as the machine's verdict on its library — so *checking a fix destroyed the two-hour record*, and the "Run the remaining N" button after a stop did the same. The published report is now the **merge** of this run over the last (the per-run `.jsonl` stays exactly one run), so the LivePortrait re-run corrected one line and left 61 standing: verified, `mergedFrom: 1`, summary recomputed to 59/2.
- ★ **The report said nothing about the machine that produced it.** `status()` omitted `machine`, and the route copied `machine: run.machine` from it — **a line that reads as if it handled the field while always copying undefined**. A complete 61-bundle report went out with no host, no GPU, no ComfyUI version and no commit, while both files on disk held all of it. ★ Worth remembering as a shape: explicit-looking code naming a field its source does not have.
- ★ **Three rows ran their columns together** (`…_with_reference32s`, `luma 171.534 models`): `padEnd` does nothing once a value exceeds its column, and the longest bundle id here is 48 characters against a 44-wide field. Widths now come from the real library and every column clips.
- ★ **A caption could be credited to the wrong bundle.** `_collectTextOutputs` took ComfyUI's newest history entry, which is only this run's if a run reached ComfyUI at all — a bundle failing before it queued left the *previous* bundle's entry newest. The prompt must now carry this run's `bench_<id>_<digit>` stamp, **anchored on the digit** because one bundle id is a prefix of another (the same collision that had already mis-credited a picture).
- **The numbers, for reference** (first sweep / second): **99 / 86 distinct weights observed** — the spread is cache state, see above; wall **2h 12m / 2h 30m**; slowest bundles the three Bernini video editors at 18–19 m each; **0 suspect outputs in both**; 70 of 70 prune-list files never opened (⚠ a reason to keep, never proof of unused — a setting nobody exercised leaves no trace either).
- **Tests:** `librarySweep.test.js` 36 → **62**, `sweepReport.test.js` 30 → **38**, new `outputCollector.test.js` **13**. All suites green over 61 bundles.

## Latest progress (2026-10-06)

**Managing the install, not just serving it: four admin tabs, model provenance, a prune tool with a confidence score, server-side downloads, node-pack installs, and `_maintenance` moved into the repo (owner request, 2026-10-06)** — owner: *"the `_maintenance` should move inside comfyQ … ComfyQ admin interface should have tabs … We should also have a feature that will let us see which models are installed and which models are missing"*. ⚠ **Nothing has been pruned yet — the owner is making a physical clone of the NVMe first, deliberately, so deletion is the session's last act.**

- **Admin panel is four tabs** instead of ten stacked cards ([AdminConfig.jsx](client/src/pages/AdminConfig.jsx)): **Workflows** (a new Running section + the library + add-workflow + the storyboard batch as its own card — owner: *"it is something else"*), **Manage server**, **Manage ComfyUI**, **Maintenance**.
- **Every workflow card says whether its models are here** ([ModelReadiness.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelReadiness.jsx)). ★ **Two sources on purpose:** PRESENT comes from the graph (`vramEstimate`), because what a bundle loads is the only honest answer to "can it run here?" — the hand-written `requirements.models` carries prose entries and repo ids no file check can satisfy. WHERE IT COMES FROM is the meta's job, filled by a **provenance harvest**: `tools/model-provenance` read the download links the original workflows' own **comment notes** already carried (the owner knew they were there) and reached **262 of the 268 model entries across 61 bundles, 86% of them from those notes** (the 6 that stay unsourced are named in bundleCompleteness.test.js; re-run the harvest for the current figure rather than trusting this one, since it moves whenever a bundle is added or removed).
- **`<nvme>\_maintenance` now lives at [tools/maintenance](tools/maintenance/)** and the original is **deleted**. 8 disk-layout assumptions removed: every script resolves the install through **`tools/maintenance/comfyq_paths.py`** (`COMFY_ROOT` env → `comfy_ui.root_path` from config.json → legacy sibling layout), so they work under any drive letter and from anywhere in the repo. Four of them are **buttons under Maintenance** now ([scriptRunner.js](server/maintenance/scriptRunner.js)): rebuild the audit, verify it, repair the install, portablise paths. ★ **The route takes a task KEY, never a script path** — a path from a browser would turn an admin button into an arbitrary-code endpoint running ComfyUI's own interpreter against the live install.
- ★★ **Rig lesson that cost a failed run: ComfyUI's embedded Python has a `python312._pth`, which PINS `sys.path` and makes it IGNORE `PYTHONPATH`.** So a script's own folder is never importable and `build_csv.py` died on `import variants` with the file sitting right beside it. Verified directly: `PYTHONPATH IGNORED`, and `sys.path[0]` is the ComfyUI dir, not the script's. The runner launches through a `runpy` bootstrap that puts the script's directory and `tools/maintenance` back on the path. **Any tool that shells out to `python_embeded` needs this.**
- ★ **The audit was calling three present models absent.** `rescan.py` counted a model as on disk only under `models/`, but node packs download their own detectors into their own folder — `yolox_l.torchscript.pt`, `dw-ll_ucoco_384_bs5.torchscript.pt` and `yolox_l.onnx` are in `comfyui_controlnet_aux/ckpts/…`. "9 workflow dependencies genuinely absent" was really **5**, and someone would have re-downloaded three files that have been here for weeks. Fixed; `vramEstimate.js` was widened for the same reason, so **both scanners now agree about what "present" means**. Rebuilt and verified through the real route: 260 models / 1342.7 GB, **ALL CHECKS PASSED**, and the hand-typed `ACTION` column survived untouched (only the tool's own `verdict` column moved, which is what it is for).

**Pruning the disk — the part that took the most care, because it lost a file (2026-10-06)** — owner: *"One use of this maintenance tab is to prune the disk from unused model"*, then *"I am a bit anxious about the data loss and model loss"*.

- ★★ **It deleted a model that was in use, and the gap is worth remembering: subgraphs.** `full_encoder_small_decoder.safetensors` (0.23 GB) is loaded by a `VAELoader` **inside a subgraph** of three Klein templates, and the reference walker did not descend into `definitions.subgraphs`. 12 of that day's 13 deletions were correct; the file was refetched from `black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-small-decoder`. **Every later guard exists because of this one.**
- **[modelUsage.js](server/workflows/modelUsage.js) is the engine** (shared with `tools/model-provenance/exclusive.cjs`, so the CLI and the panel can never disagree). A model is protected by any of: a bundle's api.json, a `_template.json`, a `lora` dropdown's `optionsFilter`, an external/candidate/demo/lane workflow folder, a **node pack's own source code**, a staged HF repo folder, or an **observed load**. `refsInGraph` reads **five** places — `inputs`, `widgets_values` (array *or* object), `widgets_values_named`, `properties.models[].name`, `extra.prompt` — plus nested nodes and subgraph definitions. Names are folded to lower case (Windows).
- ★ **Four false-confident classes found and closed, each worth GB:** files under `custom_nodes/` read as unreferenced (they belong to their pack); **staged HF repo folders** (16.65 GB scored high); **weights named only in a pack's Python** (7 LivePortrait files, one of them high); and the **Trellis2 repo-id dropdown** (7.54 GB — and its `variant-sibling` text actively argued *for* deleting).
- ★ **Pruning MOVES, it does not delete** ([modelQuarantine.js](server/models/modelQuarantine.js)): files go to `<drive>\_model_quarantine\<ISO batch>\models\…`, a rename on the same volume, so it is instant and reversible. Restore refuses to overwrite; emptying a batch is the only irreversible step and refuses reparse points.
- ★ **A confidence score per file** ([modelConfidence.js](server/workflows/modelConfidence.js)), with named reasons in plain language. Strong doubts force **low** (`variant-sibling`, `near-missing`, `generic-name`, `audit-keep`, `duplicate-basename`, `scan-incomplete`); softer ones cap at **medium** (`reference-template`, `recently-added`). Bulk-select only offers **high**; low needs `allowLow` per file. **The confident tier fell from 47 files / 32.1 GB to 2 files / 6.46 GB, and unused from 145 to 70 files** — the tool now offers 1.6% of the 413 GB a naive scan would.
- ★★ **And a guard can be decorative without anyone noticing: there were TWO generic-name lists.** `modelUsage` decided `genericName` and `modelConfidence` read a set of its own, so widening one changed nothing — `model.fp16.safetensors` was flagged generic by the scan and still scored **confident**. The set is now shape-based (a precision tag inside a generic stem is still a generic stem) and the scorer reads the row's flag. ★ Also measured `familyKey` against the **audit's own BUILD clustering**: 27 same-model pairs, agreement 24 → **27 of 27**. ⚠ **That is a weaker check than it first reads, and an earlier version of this entry overstated it.** The audit's BUILD clusters come from `variants.core(name)` = `''.join(tokens(name))` — **filename tokens, not the files' contents** (`build_csv.py` does read safetensors headers, but only to spot a *renamed* file, never for this clustering). So two name-based heuristics were compared with each other: agreement says they are consistent, not that either is right. **An independent check would have to read the tensor shapes/dtypes off the files themselves, and has not been done.** A version digit glued to a word (`Wan2.1_VAE` vs `wan_2.1_vae`) is now stripped — **but only after the variant filter**, because doing it first turns `bf16` into `bf` and drops agreement to 7.
- ★ **What ComfyUI actually opens is now recorded** ([modelAccessLog.js](server/workflows/modelAccessLog.js)): ComfyQ's own custom node wraps `folder_paths.get_full_path` and `comfy.utils.load_torch_file` and appends to `comfy_root/comfyq_model_access.jsonl`. Every other signal is inference; this is an observation. ⚠ **It can only ever PROTECT** — absence of a record is not evidence of disuse, since the log starts when the recorder is installed and a bundle nobody ran leaves no trace.
- **Scan dirs are configurable and recursive** (`maintenance.workflowScanDirs`, relative to the ComfyUI path, plus `ComfyQ\workflows` and ComfyUI's own user workflows); a **blind scan cannot reach medium**, and a folder it was told about but could not read is reported rather than silently scoring its models unused.

**Downloads and node packs — fetched onto the rig that needs them (2026-10-06)** — owner: *"can we trigger the installation from our backend rather than from comfyUI's?"* and, on downloads, *"Can't we just tell a browser to download in a specific folder"*.

- ★ **Downloads run on the server, not in the browser** ([modelDownloader.js](server/models/modelDownloader.js)), and the reason settles the owner's question: the admin panel is routinely open **on another computer** (there is a Copy-admin-link button for exactly that), so a browser download lands on whoever's laptop is open rather than on the rig that needs the weights — and `showDirectoryPicker`, the only web API that could choose a folder, needs a secure context, which plain HTTP on the LAN is not. The destination folder is read **off the HF `/resolve/<rev>/<path>` URL** first and the meta's `type` only as a fallback; anything else is refused rather than guessed. Range resume, a `.part` renamed only when the byte count matches (a half file would read as INSTALLED to the prune tool, which would then call it unused), a free-space check with 2 GB headroom, and a plain-language message for a gated repo. `comfy_ui.hf_token` is stripped from `GET /admin/config`.
- **Node packs** ([nodePacks.js](server/workflows/nodePacks.js), [nodePackInstaller.js](server/models/nodePackInstaller.js)). ★ **Measured first, and it shaped the feature: every class every one of the 61 bundles needs is installed here — 0 missing.** So the case this serves is a fresh machine or a rig imaged before a pack was added, which means it **could not be proven by what is here**: it is tested against fixtures that build a ComfyUI deliberately short of a pack (33 checks), and the missing-pack UI was verified by handing the page the answer a fresh machine would give. ⚠ **With no class list at all the answer is UNKNOWN, never "nothing is missing"** — calling a fresh rig ready because ComfyUI happened to be down is the one wrong answer here. Who provides a class comes from **ComfyUI-Manager's `extension-node-map.json` read off disk** (5590 repos, 40160 classes) rather than from Manager's API, so it also answers with no internet.
- ★ **Installing is `git clone --depth 1` into `custom_nodes`** — the pack authors' own published instruction and the supported extension point, so ComfyUI's code is never touched and a real remote is left behind. The URL is matched against a strict shape on three known hosts before it becomes an argument to git.
- ⚠ **pip is a SEPARATE, explicit step, and this is the reason:** one `python_embeded` is shared by every lane and every rig imaged from this drive, and **18 of the 44 installed packs have requirements that replace something shared** — `comfyui-easy-use` pulls `onnxruntime`, the exact package whose CPU wheel once overwrote the GPU one and sent DWPose to the CPU at 64.5 s instead of 5.1 s with no error anywhere. Requirements are read and shown, risky lines named, and pip refuses until that is acknowledged.
- **Worth knowing: 24 of the 44 installed packs are not git clones at all** (ComfyUI-Manager unpacks a copy), so an Update button would be a lie for most of them. The card says which can be updated instead of offering it to all.

**Also (2026-10-06)**

- **[Start-ComfyQ.bat](Start-ComfyQ.bat)** — ComfyQ by double-click (owner request). Takes its folder from `%~dp0` and names no drive, installs dependencies on a first run, opens the page once the interface answers, and treats a double-click while ComfyQ is already up as "show me the page" rather than failing on a port it cannot have.
- **Removed at the owner's request, with their weights:** the **Marigold V2** depth/normals pair and the **Viggle Turbo** Qwen bundle (4.92 GB), the **two Flux.2 Dev** bundles (85.76 GB — *"they take a lot of room and are not that usefull"*), and the **two custom-node Pixal3D/Trellis2** bundles the owner built before ComfyUI supported those models natively.
- ⚠ **`RunningHubAI/rh-qwen-image-2.1-lora` was inspected read-only and nothing was installed.** Of its three files, `qwen-image-2.1-fix-1.0-comfy.safetensors` is **byte-identical in size to the copy already on this rig** (111,612,000 bytes) and already driven by [image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i_fix](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i_fix/); the other two are sexualised body-reshaping LoRAs (the README's trigger phrase is explicit), and the README body is largely referral marketing. On a student-facing rig that is the owner's call, and the only generally useful file is one we already have.

## Latest progress (2026-10-05, evening)

**Pruning now MOVES a model instead of deleting it — plus a second confirmed data-loss path closed (owner request, 2026-10-06)** — owner: *"I am a bit anxious about the data loss and model loss. Spawn a critic agent and review all this pruning mechanism."* A six-lens critic with three refuters per finding raised **15 candidates**. ⚠ **It hit the session limit with 20 of 52 agents done**, so only one candidate was fully verified by the workflow and the completeness pass never ran — the rest were triaged by hand here.

- ★★★ **THE CHANGE THAT MATTERS: a prune is now a MOVE, not an unlink.** Every other safeguard — the scan, the score, the gate — lowers the *chance* of a wrong verdict; none made a wrong verdict *recoverable*. A rename on the same volume is instant and costs no space, so certainty was being paid for with irreversibility for no reason. New [modelQuarantine.js](server/models/modelQuarantine.js): files go to `<drive>\_model_quarantine\<ISO batch>\models\…`, which is **where `_maintenance/prune-models.ps1` already puts its own**, so the two agree. ★ Per-batch stamping means the same filename pruned twice keeps both copies; a restore **refuses to overwrite** rather than silently replacing whichever copy was wanted; emptying a batch is the only irreversible step and it refuses to follow a reparse point out of the folder. **Verified live end to end**: a 3 MB probe moved to `J:\_model_quarantine\2026-10-06T06-38-03-109Z\…`, listed, restored, bytes intact, batch emptied.
- ★★★ **CONFIRMED (2/2 refuters): ComfyUI-Trellis2's fp8 set — 9 files, 7.54 GB — was offered for deletion, and the reason shown argued FOR deleting it.** Both pack-code matchers missed it: the pack offers its model sets as a **dropdown of HuggingFace repo ids** (`["microsoft/TRELLIS.2-4B", "visualbruno/TRELLIS.2-4B-FP8", "TencentARC/Pixal3D-T"]`) and then does `os.path.join(folder_paths.models_dir, modelname)` with the chosen value **in a variable**, so the literal-folder matcher saw nothing; and the fp8 weight names are not literals either — they live extensionless and slash-qualified inside the staged repo's own `pipeline_fp8.json`. Meanwhile `microsoft` *was* claimed, because one other line spells it out. **Same pack, two builds, one protected and one offered, purely on regex shape.** ★ And the only thing holding those 9 rows at `low` was `variant-sibling`, whose text read *"check you are not deleting the one a workflow actually wants"* — an admin who checks concludes *keep the bf16, delete the fp8 lookalike*. **The brake was the argument for pulling the trigger.** Fixed both halves: an `owner/name` literal now claims that folder, but **only when `models/<owner>/<name>` really exists** (disk-verified, not a guess about every slash-bearing string); and the reason now states the fact and points at no conclusion — *"a different build of the same model is in use (…). A node pack or a workflow setting can switch between builds, so this one may be needed as well — find what loads it before deleting either."* 79 → **70 unused**, and pack code now protects **95 files / 126.83 GB**.
- ★★ **The route's "re-derives usage before deleting" promise was only half true.** `packCodeClaims` and `referenceNames` cache for ten minutes and the file index for thirty seconds, so **a node pack installed minutes ago was invisible to the very check meant to catch exactly that** — and 126 GB here is protected by pack code alone. The prune route now drops all three caches first. ★ Seen incidentally during the live probe: the file was too new to appear in `/usage`, yet the prune's fresh re-derive found it.
- ★★ **`medium` was ungated AND bulk-selectable, and that is where deliberate keep-decisions live.** *"Select the N shown"* rendered on every tier, the *Everything* view opens on the **25 largest** files, and the route gated only `low`. Bulk-select is now offered **only on the confident tier** (2 files / 6.46 GB here); every other tier says *"Tick rows individually here"* and carries a warning explaining what that group is. Verified in headless Chrome across all four tiers.
- ★★ **A blind scan reached `medium`, which was ungated.** `scan-incomplete` is now a **strong** signal (forces `low`), and the route **refuses outright** with 409 when a configured folder could not be read. The comment always claimed it capped the whole report; now it does.
- ★★ **The prune log was written once, after the whole loop, as a single rewritten JSON array** — so an interruption lost the record of everything already moved, and a truncated file read as *"no log yet"*, which the next prune would overwrite. Now **append-only JSON Lines, one record per file, written before the next file is touched**; a damaged tail costs one line. ★ And it now records **how to get the file back**: the `url`/`source` harvested into the metas *and* the audit's hand-written `redownload` note — which `auditVerdicts` was already loading for its verdict and then throwing away.
- **New `maintenance.quarantineDir`** (empty = beside the install). Routes: `GET /admin/models/quarantine`, `POST …/restore`, `POST …/empty` (both **adminGate**); a restore invalidates the caches, because the file is back under `models/`.
- **Triaged by hand, not fixed, with reasons:** `familyKey` keeps a digit glued to a word, so `Wan2_2-Animate…` and `wan_animate_2…` get different keys and **no `variant-sibling` warning fires on ~40 GB of medium rows** — real, and the next thing to fix. `recently-added` expires on the clock, so a row held at medium only by recency becomes `high` after 30 days. ⚠ **Checked and NOT live here:** an extensionless model reference would be invisible to every matcher, but no bundle on this rig has one — every candidate turned out to be a sampler name, an `upscale_method` or a `filename_prefix`.
- **Tests:** new [modelQuarantine.test.js](server/models/modelQuarantine.test.js) **23 checks** (move, two prunes of one filename keeping both, restore, refusing to overwrite, escaping `models/`, escaping the quarantine, the only-irreversible-step guard); `modelUsage.test.js` 50 → **53** pinning the repo-id dropdown case. Nine suites green.
- ⚠ **Coverage is incomplete and should not be read as a clean bill.** 32 of the critic's 52 agents died on the session limit — most of the route/recovery and UI-trust candidates went unverified, and the completeness critic, whose whole job was to say what nobody looked at, never ran. Worth re-running.

**Model downloads, onto the rig that needs them (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"Begin downloads and node packs install"*. The **model half is in**; node-pack installs are not started.

- ★★ **Server-side, and that is not a preference.** The admin panel is routinely open on *another* computer — there is a Copy-admin-link button for exactly that — so a browser download lands on whoever's laptop is open rather than on the rig. And `showDirectoryPicker()`, the only web API that could choose a folder, needs a secure context, which plain HTTP on the LAN is not (HTTPS was tried and reverted in May). New [modelDownloader.js](server/models/modelDownloader.js), available in **both** modes, reading config fresh on every use.
- ★★★ **Placement is the part that must not guess, and the URL usually knows.** A file in the wrong folder is invisible to ComfyUI **and** reads as unused on the next prune. The provenance harvested from the workflows' own notes is mostly HF `/resolve/<rev>/<path>` links, and that path *is* the layout the repacker intended — so [modelDestination.js](server/models/modelDestination.js) reads the folder off the link first and only falls back to the declared `type`. ★ **Validated against the real disk: of 235 entries with a direct URL, 204 are placed by the link and 30 by the type, and where the file is already installed the resolver agrees with its actual folder 206 times out of 206 — zero disagreements.** The 1 remaining undecidable case **asks** rather than guesses (HTTP 409 + `needsDir`), and the UI offers the install's real model folders.
- ★ **`unet` in a link is redirected to `diffusion_models`**, because ComfyUI reads both but every UNET on this rig lives in the latter, and a model in the other one is invisible to the dropdown an admin would check with.
- **What makes it worth having over a link:** HTTP **Range resume** (these are 1–40 GB files on a workshop LAN); a **free-space check** before the first byte, keeping 2 GB spare, because filling this volume takes out the sqlite queue and the serving ComfyUI with it; and ★★ **a `.part` file renamed only once the byte count matches** — a half file would read as *installed* to everything else, including the prune tool, which would then call it unused and offer to delete it.
- **Refusals, each with a reason an admin can act on:** a **gated** repo says *"accept its licence on HuggingFace and set an access token under Manage ComfyUI"* (8 of this library's links answer 401 — FLUX.2, LTX-2.5); an **already-installed** model is never overwritten, because a download is a repair, not a replacement; **plain http** is rejected; a filename from a URL is reduced to a basename and the path pinned inside `models/`, so `../../../../evil.safetensors` lands in `loras/evil.safetensors` rather than anywhere else.
- **New `comfy_ui.hf_token`**, treated as a secret exactly like the password hashes: `GET /admin/config` strips it and reports only `hasHfToken`. Routes: `POST /admin/models/download` · `/cancel` · `/forget` (all **adminGate**) and `GET /admin/models/downloads` · `/models/folders`.
- **Verified end to end through the live route**, with nothing written to disk: a gated repo queued and failed with the advice above; an undecidable destination returned 409 asking for a folder; an installed model was refused by name and folder; plain http refused; a failed row forgotten. **UI**: [ModelDownloads.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelDownloads.jsx) in Maintenance, polling only while something is in flight, warning when no token is set.
- **Tests: 29 checks** in [modelDownloader.test.js](server/models/modelDownloader.test.js), against a **real local HTTP server** rather than a stubbed fetch — because what goes wrong here is protocol behaviour and a stub only proves the stub agrees with itself. Pinned: resume continues rather than restarts *and the bytes match*; ★ **a server that IGNORES `Range` restarts cleanly instead of appending** (which would silently corrupt the file); a complete `.part` is just renamed (416); ★ **a truncated body fails and does NOT create the model file**, keeping the `.part` to resume; gated/404 messages; queueing one at a time.
- ★ **Two slips of mine worth recording.** (1) I smoke-tested a server module with `node -e "require('./server/index.js')"` — which **booted a second ComfyQ**. No harm (nodemon restarted, ComfyUI stayed up, student mode intact) but `node --check` is the right tool; requiring an entrypoint runs it. (2) The `allowLow` self-clearing `useEffect` was placed **above** the `const pickedLow` it reads — a temporal-dead-zone error the bundler compiled happily and the page threw on every render. ★ **The build cannot catch that; the headless-Chrome check did**, which is why it is worth running on every UI change.

**An adversarial review of the prune tool found a confirmed data-loss path, and the confident tier fell from 32.1 GB to 6.46 GB (2026-10-05)** — a four-lens workflow (reference shapes · identity · scoring · route+UI) raised 8 candidate weaknesses, each then handed to a verifier told to REFUTE it. **2 survived, both real.** Run before any pruning, which is the only reason they are findings rather than incidents.

- ★★★ **CONFIRMED DATA LOSS: a node pack can open a weight by a path it BUILDS IN CODE, and nothing saw it.** `comfyui-liveportraitkj/nodes.py` joins `folder_paths.models_dir` with `"liveportrait"` and then with each filename, and its `DownloadAndLoadLivePortraitModels` node — whose *only* input is `precision` — loads all five unconditionally. So **`stitching_retargeting_module.safetensors` scored HIGH CONFIDENCE DELETABLE** while the rig-verified [video_liveportrait_image2video](workflows/video_liveportrait_image2video/) bundle loads it on every job. Six of its siblings were saved **only** by a hand-written `audit-keep` entry in the model audit's `decisions.json` — which had a hole exactly at that one file.
  - ⚠ **And it was a two-step deletion, not a one-off.** The basename index keeps the first copy it meets, and `animal/` sorts before the human set, so the report described `liveportrait/animal/stitching_retargeting_module.safetensors` while `liveportrait/stitching_retargeting_module.safetensors` — the exact path `nodes.py` builds — was **absent from the report entirely**. Delete the animal copy (harmless, which is the trap: it builds trust), and within 30 s the same high-confidence row points at the copy in use. Unrecoverable too: the pack's refetch guard is `if not os.path.exists(model_path)` on the **directory**, which still exists.
  - ⚠ **Worse, 32 rows / 27.98 GB were `low` solely because of `audit-keep`** — and `auditVerdicts` swallows a read failure, while `tools/maintenance` was only *copied* into the repo with the original slated for deletion. Move or rename that one file and the confident tier jumped **47 rows / 32.1 GB → 79 / 60.08 GB**, pulling in all six remaining liveportrait weights.
  - **Fix: scan the packs' own Python.** New `packCodeClaims()` greps `custom_nodes/**/*.py` for weight filenames and for `models_dir, "<folder>"` claims — **2110 files, 21 MB, ~112 ms**, cached ten minutes. ★ This is the model audit's own method (`rescan.py`: *"scanning custom-node source"*). ★★ **The folder half needs a guard:** treating ComfyUI's SHARED folders (`diffusion_models`, `loras`, `vae`…) as pack claims protected **285 GB** and left the tool with nothing to offer, so only a pack's *own* folder counts. Now **79 files / 119.25 GB are protected by pack code**, all seven liveportrait weights among them, and the `decisions.json` single point of failure is down to **11 rows / 5.26 GB**.
- ★★ **CONFIRMED: the low-confidence gate could be disarmed invisibly.** `allowLow` was component state reset in exactly **one** place — the success path of `prune()`. Not on a group change, not on *Clear selection*, not on *Rescan* — and its checkbox only renders while a suspicious row is selected, so the rest of the time it was **invisible and still true**. Tick it, think better of it, clear the selection, switch to *Everything*, *Select the N shown* — and the gate was already open with no warning on screen. **Fixed by deriving rather than storing it:** the flag sent is the tick AND a low row still being selected, and the tick is dropped the moment none is. *A safety interlock that outlives what it was granted for is not one.*
- ★ **Two more holes closed while verifying.** (1) **Duplicate basenames** — 14 filenames exist in more than one place on this disk (the liveportrait `animal/` vs human sets, three Pixal3D `ckpts` trees, four `model.safetensors`). The index now records the copies it skipped and any row with a sibling copy is forced **low**, naming the others: usage is matched by basename, so a reference could have meant either, and the report only ever describes one. (2) **Files inside `custom_nodes/`** — kjnodes' `intrinsic_loras`, WanVideoWrapper's `text_embed_cache` — belong to their pack by definition, and the prune route pins deletions to `models/` and would have refused them anyway. Offering them was a promise the tool could not keep.
- ★ **My own new test caught a bug my live spot-check had missed.** Both pack-scan regexes had been mangled writing them through a heredoc into a template literal — `\s*` became `s*`, so **folder claims never matched at all**. The live check looked fine because every liveportrait weight happened to be caught by the *filename* half. The failing assertion is what exposed it.
- **The honest result: the confident tier is 2 files / 6.46 GB**, down from 47 / 32.1 GB — `v2-1_768-ema-pruned` (SD 2.1, 2022) and `iclight_sd15_fc`. 79 unused / 420.63 GB in all, 25 medium / 167.78 GB, 52 low / 246.39 GB. And **nothing is now offered that the route would refuse**.
- **Tests:** `modelUsage.test.js` 45 → **50**, `modelConfidence.test.js` 26 → **28**. The pack-code checks pin the filename half, the own-folder half, that a SHARED folder is *not* claimed, and that a file named inside a shared folder still is.
- ⚠ **Correction to the 3D-removal entry above:** it reported `models/microsoft` + `models/Pixal3D` + `models/facebook` (~42 GB) as freed by deleting those two bundles. **Premature** — the `Pixal3D-ComfyUI` and `ComfyUI-Trellis2` packs are still installed and their code claims those folders, so the weights stay protected until the packs go too. The tool now says so itself.

**The two custom-node 3D bundles removed — and removing them exposed a false-confident class worth 16.65 GB (owner, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"remove the workflows for pixal3D and treillis2 that are not from the comfy core nodes. The custom ones I built before the comfyUI official support of those models."* 63 → **61 bundles**.

- **The split was clean and verified by reverse lookup, not by guesswork.** `Pixal3DImageTo3D` / `Pixal3DModelLoader` / `Pixal3DExportGLB` come from the **`Pixal3D-ComfyUI`** pack and `Trellis2LoadModel` / `Trellis2ShapeGenerator` / `Trellis2ExportMesh` from **`ComfyUI-Trellis2`**, while `Pixal3DConditioning`, `Trellis2ShapeStage`, `EmptyTrellis2LatentStructure` and `VaeDecodeShapeTrellis` all live in **comfy-core `comfy_extras/nodes_trellis2.py`**. So: removed **[3d_pixal3d_image_to_mesh]** and **[3d_trellis2_image_to_textured_mesh]**; kept [3d_pixal3d_multi_views](workflows/3d_pixal3d_multi_views/) and [3d_pixal3d_trellis2_image_to_model](workflows/3d_pixal3d_trellis2_image_to_model/), which run the core nodes on Comfy-Org's repacked `*_int8_convrot` files.
- ★★★ **Found while checking what the removal would free: 12 files / 16.65 GB were scored HIGH confidence while the bundles that need them were still installed.** A bundle can declare a HuggingFace **repo** it stages through rather than a filename (`microsoft/TRELLIS.2-4B`, `Pixal3D/briaai_RMBG-2.0`), and such a repo lands on disk as a folder of weights that **no graph names file by file** — so every file inside read as unreferenced, and because nothing resembled them either, as *safe to delete*. ★ `buildModelUsage` now **claims the folder**: a declared repo-shaped model protects every on-disk file under a matching path segment, attributed to the bundle that stages it. 12 of 13 immediately became protected. Pinned by a test.
- ★★ **The generic-name hazard runs BOTH ways, and the protecting direction was invisible.** `models/facebook/dinov3-vitl16-pretrain-lvd1689m/model.safetensors` (1.13 GB) read as *used by three LTX bundles* — but all those templates really name is `gemma-3-12b-it-qat-q4_0-unquantized_readout_proj/model/model.safetensors`, whose basename is also `model.safetensors`. Usage is keyed by basename, so the collision **falsely protected** it. Over-protection costs disk rather than data, so it is not urgent, but it was unseeable: the confidence score only runs on *unused* rows. Every model row now carries `genericName`, so a dubious protection is at least inspectable. ⚠ **Two files on this disk have such a name**, and that dinov3 one is almost certainly orphaned now that the TRELLIS2 bundle has gone.
- **What the removal actually frees, reported and NOT deleted** (the owner is cloning the NVMe before any pruning): the staged repo folders — **`models/microsoft` 16.64 GB, `models/Pixal3D` 24.35 GB, `models/facebook` 1.13 GB ≈ 42.1 GB** — of which 16.65 GB is indexed weights now reading unused and the rest is config/tokeniser files the index does not track. Only **`moge_2_vitl_normal_fp16.safetensors` (0.62 GB) is shared** and stays: `3d_pixal3d_trellis2_image_to_model` and `video_edit_ltx2_3_3dreal_vid2vid` still load it.
- ⚠ **The custom packs themselves are still installed** — `custom_nodes/ComfyUI-Trellis2` (0.36 GB) and `Pixal3D-ComfyUI` — and nothing in ComfyQ uses them now. ★ **Worth knowing before touching them: those two packs are the reason this install runs torch 2.8.0+cu128** (a deliberate downgrade, recorded in the project memory). Removing them might lift that constraint, but changing the environment is exactly the risky move on a drive cloned to several rigs — owner's call, not a tidy-up.
- [docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md) "the five 3D bundles" is now three, with the reason recorded.

**The deleted VAE refetched, and a confidence score on every deletable model (owner, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"we need something more full proof when we do that. You should refetch what you deleted. We should include a confidence score on each model marked as deletable."*

- **Refetched.** `full_encoder_small_decoder.safetensors` is back — 249,519,092 bytes in `models/vae/`, from `black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-small-decoder`. ★ **It was never gated**; the 401s in the earlier verify run were the *Klein* repos, and I had carried that assumption across to this one. A plain `curl -L` sufficed. Confirmed back in place, classified as used by the three Klein templates, and gone from `missing[]`.
- ★★ **The headline number changes honestly: the confident reclaim is 32.1 GB, not 479.** Of 145 unused files — **high 47 / 32.1 GB · medium 30 / 184.13 GB · low 68 / 263.01 GB.** "Nothing references it" was always a weaker claim than it sounded, and now the card says so instead of offering 479 GB behind one badge.
- ★★ **The score is never a bare number: every verdict carries its named reasons, and the UI prints them.** A score you cannot interrogate converts doubt into a comforting badge, which is worse than no badge. New [modelConfidence.js](server/workflows/modelConfidence.js).
- ★★★ **`variant-sibling` is the signal that earns the feature.** A "family key" strips build/precision/size tokens (`fp8`, `bf16`, `int8`, `4step`, `768p`, `convrot`, `v1.0`, …), so two builds of one model collapse together — and a file whose family matches something **in use** drops to low with the sibling named. It fires on **exactly the pair that nearly cost 1.82 GB** (`…turbo_8step_v1.0_comfyui_bf16` against the in-use `…turbo_4step_v1.0_768p_comfyui_bf16`), and on this rig it catches 40 GB of LTX-2 19B, both 19 GB Qwen originals, and `qwen_2.5_vl_7b` against the in-use `qwen_2.5_vl_7b_fp8_scaled`.
- **The other signals**, each with a real case here: `near-missing` (a loader wants a name whose family matches this file — the 24K/24k shape); `generic-name` (`model.safetensors` and friends, where matching by basename means nothing); `audit-keep` / `audit-delete` — ★ **the model audit's own written verdicts now flow in, a direct dividend of moving `_maintenance` into the repo**; `recently-added` (mtime under 30 days — someone may be mid-task); and `scan-incomplete`, which ★ **caps every row in the report**, because a folder the scan could not read makes it wrong about all of them, not just that folder's models.
- ★★ **`reference-template`: 60 of the 145 unused files (407.99 GB, 85%) are named by material that SHIPS with ComfyUI** — its bundled templates (580 JSON), each node pack's example workflows (371) and `ComfyUI/blueprints` (116). Scanned in 822 ms, cached ten minutes. **They do not make a model used** — a bundled template names a model whether or not you have it, and counting them would move 408 GB off the list and gut the tool — but they are the *explanation* for why the file is on disk, so they demote to **medium** rather than hiding the row. Deleting one is fine; it just means that built-in template needs its model again.
- ★ **Doubt about WHICH file this is cannot be argued away.** `variant-sibling`, `near-missing`, `generic-name` and `audit-keep` force **low** outright; `reference-template`, `recently-added` and `scan-incomplete` cap at **medium**; an audit DELETE raises but **cannot rescue** a row that resembles a used file (pinned by a test).
- **The route enforces it.** `POST /admin/models/prune` refuses a **low** row unless `allowLow: true`, quoting the reason back: *"low confidence — looks like another build of ltx-2.3-22b-dev.safetensors, … which is in use"*. Verified live, 0 bytes deleted. **Medium is allowed** — it means "we know why this is here", not "we are unsure what it is".
- **The card opens on the confident set only** (47 files / 32.1 GB), with chips for the wider groups, each row showing its reasons inline, and a separate acknowledgement box that is the only way past the server's refusal. Verified in headless Chrome, no console errors.
- **Tests:** new [modelConfidence.test.js](server/workflows/modelConfidence.test.js), **26 checks** in `test:storyboard` — the family key on the real minimax pair, each signal in isolation, that a used model is scored `null`, that `scan-incomplete` caps everything, that a confident row still carries a reason, and that an audit DELETE does not overrule a lookalike. `mtimeMs` now rides on the model index (free with the stat already done), so the clock is injected and the test does not depend on it.

**`_maintenance` moved into the repo, the prune scan made safe, and a deletion of mine caught by the tool built after it (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"this new tool should go into our maintenance tab… one use of this maintenance tab is to prune the disk from unused model"*, plus *"[the scan dirs] should be relative to the path specified in the Manage ComfyUI tab… we should be able to add custom paths. By the way is it recursive?"* **Yes, recursive** — `walkJson` descends every subdirectory and takes every `*.json`, with no depth limit.

- ★★★ **I deleted a model that was in use, and this entry leads with it.** `full_encoder_small_decoder.safetensors` (0.23 GB) went with the Flux.2 Dev weights as "exclusive". It is not: it is loaded by a **`VAELoader` inside a SUBGRAPH** of three live Klein bundle *templates* (`image_flux2_klein_9b_t2i`, `image_edit_flux2_klein_9b_image_edit`, `…_ref`). The scanner in use at the time walked `nodes` but **not `definitions.subgraphs`**. **Impact is bounded:** all three bundles still run — their api.json loads `flux2-vae.safetensors`, which is present — so only "Open in ComfyUI" hands over a graph with a gap. ⚠ **And nothing reported it**, because the readiness chip walks the api.json only. Restore from `https://huggingface.co/black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-small-decoder/resolve/main/full_encoder_small_decoder.safetensors` (gated: needs a token). **All 13 of today's deletions were re-audited against the fixed scanner: 12 were correct, this was the one.**
- ★★★ **A worse one was caught BEFORE any pruning: `minimax_h3_fl2v_turbo_8step_v1.0_comfyui_bf16.safetensors` (1.82 GB) sat on the delete list while two bundle templates load it.** A UI graph hides the answer in **four** places, and that node is the proof — its `inputs` is a socket **array** (so `Object.values` finds no filename at all), its positional `widgets_values` names the **4-step** file, and its `widgets_values_named.lora_name` names the **8-step** one. **They disagree, and the named object is what a current ComfyUI frontend honours.** Measured over the library: **42 of 194 scanned files carry `widgets_values_named`, 85 carry `properties.models`.** `refsInGraph` now reads `inputs`, `widgets_values` (array *or* object), `widgets_values_named`, `properties.models[].name` and `extra.prompt` (a whole API graph embedded in a UI template), and takes the union — for a delete tool, over-protecting is the only safe direction when two fields of one node disagree. 147 → **145 unused**, two files rescued.
- ★★ **Case folding, another silent deletion path on Windows.** Names are matched lower-cased, with the on-disk spelling kept for display. Keyed case-sensitively, a template naming `ltx-av-…-24K.safetensors` against a file called `…-24k.safetensors` read as **both** "a loader wants a missing model" **and** "this file is unused" — i.e. it offered a model in use for deletion.
- ★★ **New `missing[]`: a loader asks for it and it is not on disk.** The other half of the question, and the half nothing answered — which is how the deletion above went unnoticed. Live: **6**, including mine with the three bundles named, and `ltx-av-step-1751000_vocoder_24K` wanted by three LTX templates (pre-existing, not caused by today).
- ★ **Three more holes closed.** (1) **Lane user dirs** — each parallel lane runs its own ComfyUI with its own `--user-directory`, so "Open in ComfyUI" on a lane saves graphs in `.comfyq-lanes/<id>/user/<profile>/workflows`; one such file already exists here. ⚠ Scanned per *profile*, never the lane root: the lane's `user/__manager/cache/*model-list.json` names **527** weight filenames, and walking it would mark nearly the whole disk as used and turn the prune tool into a silent no-op — hence a `SKIP_JSON_DIRS` guard (`__manager`, `.git`, `node_modules`, …) that `walkJson` had never had. (2) **The bundle half now LISTS each bundle folder** instead of opening two hardcoded filenames: the registry accepts any `*template.json`, so a non-canonically-named template was invisible and a weight only it loads would have been offered for deletion. (3) A note that merely *mentions* a filename is documentation, not a load.
- **`maintenance.workflowScanDirs` now resolves relative entries against the ComfyUI root** (the Manage ComfyUI path), matching what `comfy_ui.python_executable` and `output_dir` already do. ★★ **The resolution lives inside `buildModelUsage`, not `configManager.resolvePaths()` — because neither caller passes through it.** The admin route reads the raw config and `exclusive.cjs` parses `config.json` by hand, so resolving there would have reached neither and the panel and the CLI would compute different folders. Absolute entries still work and are now covered by the **Drive-letter swap** (whose field list is hardcoded and would otherwise skip them, the way it already covers `assets.dir`). The report returns each entry *with its resolved absolute path*, because the old `fs.existsSync(rawEntry)` check resolved a relative entry against the server's cwd — scanning nothing while reporting itself present.
- **UI:** the prune card gained a **Folders scanned** disclosure listing the always-scanned ones as fixed context (so nobody adds them as a row) and saying plainly that scanning is recursive, editable rows for extra folders with add/remove/Save each showing where a relative entry resolved to, and the missing-models section. New **`PUT /admin/maintenance/scan-dirs`**, ★ **adminGate'd unlike the sibling config PUTs** — emptying this list is exactly how a used model becomes "unused", and the destructive prune route re-derives from the same config.
- **[tools/maintenance](tools/maintenance/COMFYQ-NOTES.md)** — 26 files, 857 KB. ★ **The scripts made EIGHT assumptions about the disk's layout** (two levels up to ComfyUI, the drive letter off their own path, `ComfyUI_windows_portable_nvidia` by name) and the move broke all of them. They now ask ComfyQ: [comfyq_paths.py](tools/maintenance/comfyq_paths.py) resolves `COMFY_ROOT` → `comfy_ui.root_path` from config.json → the historical layout; `prune-models.ps1` reads the same config in PowerShell and `AUDIT.bat` gets its interpreter from [python-path.js](tools/maintenance/python-path.js) because a `.bat` cannot read JSON. **Source and curated snapshots versioned; the scans gitignored** — `object_info.json` (4.4 MB), `inventory.json`, `links.json`, `nodes.json` describe one disk at one moment, so committing them would commit a lie about every other rig. Verified: all 12 scripts compile under `python_embeded`, and `rescan.py` ran end to end from its new home (321 workflows parsed, wrote inventory.json). ⚠ **The original folder was COPIED, not moved** — the gitignored scans only exist there.
- **Tests:** [modelUsage.test.js](server/workflows/modelUsage.test.js) 20 → **43 checks**, pinning every reference form (subgraph, `widgets_values_named`, `properties.models`, `extra.prompt`, case folding), the lane dir *and* that its `__manager` cache is not read, relative-vs-absolute resolution, and that a scan without an outside folder calls its models prunable.
- ⚠ **Not acted on, needs the owner's eye:** of the 145 unused files, **61 (409.81 GB, 85%) are named by ComfyUI's own bundled templates, node-pack example workflows, or `ComfyUI/blueprints`** — none of which is scanned. Folding those into "used" would leave only ~72 GB prunable and gut the tool, but they are the *explanation* for why those files are on disk; a separate "explained" class, not a scan dir. Also unscanned and offered as scan-dir candidates: `D:\comfyq_pending_workflows` and `D:\Code` (0 GB at risk today). ★ And `properties.models[].url` in UI templates is an unharvested provenance source.

## Latest progress (2026-10-05, later still)

**Prune unused models, from the Maintenance tab — 482 GB reclaimable on this rig (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"this new tool should go into our maintenance tab… One use of this maintenance tab is to prune the disk from unused model."* The Maintenance tab now has a real feature, built on a shared engine so the panel and the command line can never disagree about what is unused.

- **New [server/workflows/modelUsage.js](server/workflows/modelUsage.js)** classifies every weight file on the disk, and **[tools/model-provenance/exclusive.cjs](tools/model-provenance/README.md) was rewritten to use it** rather than carrying its own copy. ★ That sharing is the point: if the admin panel and the CLI disagreed about "unused", one of them would be inviting someone to delete a model that is needed. **Measured on this rig: 291 weight files, 1309.76 GB, of which 147 files / 482.19 GB are referenced by nothing at all.**
- ★★ **Four protection classes, each of which has already caught a real near-miss:** (1) a bundle's **api.json**; (2) a bundle's **`_template.json`** — "Open in ComfyUI" hands the *template* to an admin, so a weight only it loads is still in use, and that alone protects a **42.98 GB** checkpoint here; (3) a **`lora` dropdown's `optionsFilter`** — the dropdown offers *every* file matching the prefix, so the **14 `krea2_*` LoRAs appear in no graph at all** and a grep over the workflows finds nothing; (4) a **candidate / demo / ComfyUI-user workflow** — not something ComfyQ serves, but somebody's work (23 files, several hundred GB on this machine).
- ★★ **A filename-shaped string is not a load.** The LTX 2.5 templates mention `ltx-2.3-22b-dev.safetensors` **eighteen times** — every one of them a cloud-API model name typed into a `GemmaAPITextEncode` widget, not a file any loader opens. The model audit reached the same verdict independently. Such a file is **kept** (the safe direction for a delete tool) but reported apart as *worth a look*, which is where the big reclaimable files hide.
- ★★ **That classifier uses a NAMED LIST, not a heuristic, and the first attempt proves why.** The obvious guess — "a node that loads a model has Loader in its name" — flagged `DWPreprocessor`, `RIFE VFI` and `ASASRApplyConditioning`, all of which genuinely take a model through a dropdown: five files offered for deletion that are in use. On a prune tool, being wrong in that direction is the whole risk. The list currently holds one class; **`/object_info` is the general answer** (it declares whether an input is a combo of filenames or a plain STRING) but needs ComfyUI running. A subgraph instance's type is a UUID, so it abstains rather than votes.
- **Routes:** `GET /admin/models/usage` and `POST /admin/models/prune` (**adminGate**). ★ **The delete re-derives usage server-side and refuses anything it does not independently judge unused** — a page left open while a workflow was added cannot talk it into removing something that is now needed. Verified by POSTing six deliberate targets and watching every one bounce, with **0 bytes deleted**: a model a bundle runs, a LoRA only a dropdown offers, the 43 GB template-only checkpoint, a `../` traversal, an absolute path, and a file that is not there. Paths are also pinned inside `models/`.
- **New config `maintenance.workflowScanDirs`** — folders of workflow JSON outside the bundles. ⚠ **Scanning less makes models look deletable**: this rig's `J:/_demo_workflows` protects 23 files, so the card **names every folder it checked** and warns loudly about any configured one it could not read. Set to `["J:/_demo_workflows"]` here (config.json is gitignored, so each machine needs its own).
- **What is removed is logged** to `server/data/pruned-models.json` with name, size, kind and date — these are multi-GB downloads and eight of them are licence-gated, so a record is the difference between "deleted" and "lost".
- **UI** ([ModelPrune.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelPrune.jsx)): biggest-first list with checkboxes, 25 shown until you ask for all 147, a running selected total, and a confirm modal that lists every file and says plainly that some are gated downloads. Verified in headless Chrome against the dev server: *"482.19 GB reclaimable"*, the basis line, 25 rows, and the review bucket as a separate collapsed section — no console errors.
- **Tests:** new [modelUsage.test.js](server/workflows/modelUsage.test.js), **20 checks**, in `test:storyboard`. It pins each protection class, the review flag, that a real loader is never flagged, the exclusivity question (dropping a bundle frees what only it used, including its dropdown's LoRAs), and ★ that a scan **without** the outside folder calls its model prunable — which is exactly why the UI names what it scanned.
