**The two Flux.2 Dev bundles removed — 85.76 GB, the most expensive pair in the library (owner, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"lets remove the two flux2 dev workflows and models aswell, they take a lot of room and are not that usefull"*. 65 → **63 bundles**: `image_flux2_dev_t2i` and `image_edit_flux2_dev_image_edit`. Neither was active nor held a lane.

- ★★ **It was the only pair that could never share a card, and the numbers say why it went:** a **33.02 GB UNET plus a 33.14 GB bf16 text encoder**, so on the 32 GB 5090 ComfyUI streamed the weights from RAM — a **≈203 s cold run with ≈158 s of it model load**, for a picture its 17.5 GB Klein sibling makes while leaving room for a second workflow beside it. ★ The two Mistral text encoders alone (bf16 33.14 + fp8 16.80 = **49.94 GB**) outweighed the whole rest of the FLUX.2 family on disk.
- ★ **Nothing was shared — 0 GB overlap**, verified rather than assumed. Dev and Klein have no file in common (Klein runs `flux-2-klein-9b-fp8` + `qwen_3_8b_fp8mixed` + `flux2-vae`), so the four Klein bundles could not be touched. All five Klein weights were checked present afterwards. **Exclusive and deleted: 85.76 GB** — `flux2_dev_fp8mixed` 33.02, `mistral_3_small_flux2_bf16` 33.14, `mistral_3_small_flux2_fp8` 16.80, `Flux_2-Turbo-LoRA_comfyui` 2.57, `full_encoder_small_decoder` 0.23.
- **New reusable tool: [tools/model-provenance/exclusive.cjs](tools/model-provenance/README.md)** — *"which model files are exclusive to these bundles, and which are shared with ones that stay"*. Written because this is the second removal in a day and the Marigold one nearly cost a 19 GB production model. It builds the keep-set from every other bundle's **api.json AND template**, the candidates, the demo and ComfyUI user workflow dirs, every other meta's `requirements.models`, and ★ the `lora` dropdowns' `optionsFilter` prefixes (a dropdown exposes *every* matching file, so those are reachable without appearing in any graph). It only ever reports; deletion stays a separate, hard-coded step.
- ★ **The delete script's allow-pattern now also has a FORBID pattern.** A glob over "flux2" would match the **Klein** models that four live bundles need, so the script refuses any target matching `/klein/i` on top of requiring an explicit allow match. Hard-code the targets; the pattern is the seatbelt, not the plan.
- ⚠ **What is lost:** Dev is the full-size model, so it had the edge on prompt adherence and fine detail over the distilled 9B Klein, and nothing else in the library reads a prompt quite that way. It is a 66 GB download to get back, and it still would not fit beside anything on a 32 GB card. Recorded in [docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md) §6d, and the "six t2i generators" list is now five.
- ★ **Side finding worth a look, not actioned: `--orphans` reports 262 GB of weights that no workflow anywhere references** — including `flux-2-klein-4b` (7.22), `qwen_image_edit_fp8_e4m3fn` (19.03), `qwen_image_fp8_e4m3fn` (19.03), five Wan 2.1/2.2 checkpoints (13–17 each), `z_image_turbo_bf16` (11.46), `ltx-2.3-22b-distilled-1.1_transformer_only_fp8_scaled` (23.49) and `pid_1.5_flux2_1024_to_4096_4step_bf16` (2.61, the FLUX.2 PiD lane that lost the A/B to the Qwen one). That is the model audit's job and needs the owner's eye on each — but it is where the remaining space is.
- ★ **Harvest reporting bug fixed in passing: "found nothing" is not "has nothing".** The audit CSV is an optional input, so a run without `MODEL_AUDIT_CSV` reported 11 entries as unsourced that a previous run had already written a source into — sending you hunting for a link sitting in the file. Entries that already carry one are now counted separately, and both runs agree. ⚠ The absolute figure quoted here has since moved with the library — as of the 2026-10-06 removals it is **262 of 268**.

**Every model now says where it comes from — and the links were already in the repo (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner's lead: *"most of the original comfyUI workflow should have them in comment notes inside the workflow"*. Right, and it carried further than expected. **276 of 282 model entries across 65 bundles now have a source**, harvested by the new [tools/model-provenance](tools/model-provenance/README.md).

- ★★ **Pool the index across bundles, keyed by filename, or leave a sixth of the library unsourced for nothing.** ComfyUI's own templates document their models in `Note` / `MarkdownNote` nodes — 53 of 68 templates carried HuggingFace URLs, 282 of them, many `/resolve/main/<subfolder>/<file>` i.e. a direct download **with its destination folder**. Per bundle that covers 199/294 (68%); **pooled across every bundle, candidate and api.json it is 243/282 (86%)**, because a shared weight like `flux-2-klein-9b-fp8` only needs *one* bundle to document it. Order of preference: note → the audit CSV's `redownload` column → `known-sources.json` → an explicit `override`.
- **Two fields, because the difference decides whether a Download button can work:** `url` is a direct fetchable file, `source` is a page for a human when only the repo is known, `auto` marks what a node pack fetches itself (never reported missing, never offered for download), `note` says why. Added to the zod schema — ★ **without that, zod would have silently stripped all four**, the same way `whenEmpty` was dropped by the editor round-trip on 2026-09-27.
- ★★ **Never invent a `url`, and verify the ones you harvest.** [verify.cjs](tools/model-provenance/README.md) HEAD-checks all 140 distinct links: **131 ok, 8 gated, 1 missing**. The one 404 was mine — a direct link I constructed by analogy with a sibling's layout (`Comfy-Org/flux2-klein-9B/split_files/loras/…`). It is now `source`-only with a note saying the constructed path 404s. **A wrong direct link downloads the wrong weights silently**, which is worse than a gap.
- ★★ **A 401 does NOT confirm the path.** HuggingFace answers 401 for *any* path inside a gated repo, so a wrong filename in a gated repo is indistinguishable from a right one. That is not academic: **the LTX deblur template's note points its pixel-spatial-upscaler adapter at `Lightricks/LTX-2.3-22b-IC-LoRA-Deblur`, which does not hold it** (the 2026-09-14 entry records this), and because that repo is gated the bad link reads as healthy. Hence an `override` flag in `known-sources.json` that beats the note — ★ **and it has to run LAST**: placed before the fallbacks, the audit's own copy of the same wrong link refilled it on the very next line.
- ★ **The two tools would otherwise fight.** `verify --fix` drops a dead link; the next `harvest` puts it straight back from the note that was wrong. Dead links are now recorded in `dead-links.json`, which the harvest refuses to re-add. The harvest is **idempotent** — a second `--write` changes nothing.
- ★ **`Object.assign` cannot remove a key**, so an override that clears a `url` did nothing until the merge explicitly deleted the four fields the tool owns before assigning. The symptom was an override that "worked" in the computation and never reached the file.
- **8 links are gated** (black-forest-labs FLUX.2 ×2, Lightricks LTX-2.5 ×6) → **the download feature will need a HuggingFace token and an accepted licence**, not just a fetch. Worth knowing before building it.
- **6 entries (5 files) are deliberately left unsourced** — `3DREAL-strong`, `DynamicCharacterSheet_krea2_v1`, `QuadView_krea2_v1`, `qwen-image-edit-2511-multiple-angles-lora`, `wan_2.1_idv2v_int8_convrot`. Community files whose origin is recorded nowhere; a guess would be worse than the gap. They are **named** in [bundleCompleteness.test.js](server/workflows/bundleCompleteness.test.js), whose new 6th check fails any bundle declaring a model with no `url`/`source`/`auto` — so the rule is self-enforcing and the list can only shrink deliberately.
- **Surfaced on the card**: the registry summary now carries `models[]`, and [ModelReadiness.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelReadiness.jsx) joins it to the graph-derived disk check by filename — each row gets a **download** link (direct) or **source** link (repo page), and an auto-fetched model is listed as such rather than as a file to find. ★ **Two different sources on purpose:** whether a model is PRESENT comes from the graph, where it COMES FROM comes from the meta. Verified in headless Chrome: paths, sizes, kinds and a working direct link per model, no console errors.
- ⚠ **Data quality found on the way:** 5 entries declare a HuggingFace repo id rather than a file (`microsoft/TRELLIS.2-4B`) and 2 are prose (`"InsightFace + MediaPipe face croppers (auto-downloaded)"`). All 7 are now `auto: true` with a note, so they are declared and excused instead of unfixably "missing". ★ The lookup has to try the **raw declared value before the basename**, or a repo id is reduced to its last path segment and never matches.

**The admin panel is four tabs instead of ten stacked cards, with a real "Running" section (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner's layout, adopted nearly as given: **Workflows** (Running · Workflow library · Storyboard batch · Add workflow) · **Manage server** (Student access · Admin password · Network presence · Cleanup · Clear job history) · **Manage ComfyUI** (Settings · backend) · **Maintenance**. A mechanical pass over [AdminConfig.jsx](client/src/pages/AdminConfig.jsx) with no behaviour changes riding along, committed on its own.

- **Why it was worth doing:** ten cards in one scroll put the workflow library *below* the ComfyUI paths and the federation toggle, so the thing an admin opens the panel for was the thing they had to scroll past server administration to reach.
- **New [RunningWorkflows.jsx](client/src/components/admin/RunningWorkflows.jsx)** — one row per lane with its name, id, port, busy state and resident VRAM, and **empty when nothing is served**, which is the honest reading of an admin-mode machine (it answers HTTP and schedules nothing). ★ **The two stops are different actions and are labelled differently:** closing an **extra** lane returns its VRAM and the machine keeps serving the others (`DELETE /admin/lanes/:id`), while stopping the **primary** lane is *stop serving* — back to admin mode, students can no longer book (`resetToAdmin`). `laneManager.close()` will close the primary quite happily and leave the machine in student mode with nothing to serve, so the UI must not offer that as "close lane". Mirrors the per-card controls in [WorkflowSelector.jsx](client/src/components/WorkflowSelector.jsx) so the two cannot drift.
- **The library card's own lanes summary was deleted** rather than left beside the new card — two places reporting what is being served is how they come to disagree.
- **Maintenance is an honest placeholder.** It names what belongs there (update ComfyUI, audit the disk, repair) and says plainly that nothing runs yet, rather than shipping buttons that do nothing. It fills up when `_maintenance` moves into the repo.
- ★ **Network presence was not in the owner's proposed grouping** (it was in the "what we have" list and not the "what we should have" one). Placed under **Manage server** — it is this server's own presence on the LAN, not a ComfyUI concern. Say so if it belongs elsewhere.
- **The open tab is remembered in `localStorage`** (`comfyq.adminTab`), because activating a workflow restarts the server and an admin thrown back to the first tab every time would curse us.
- **Verified in headless Chrome against the dev server, not just by building:** a bundler cannot catch a restructured-JSX runtime error. All four tabs click through showing exactly the right cards, the Running card shows the live primary lane (`utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale`, :8188, 28.55 of 32 GB, 1.45 GB free) with **Stop serving** rather than Close lane, the tab survives a reload, and **zero console errors**. ⚠ The stop buttons were deliberately *not* clicked — the rig is serving.
- The restructure also left `Radio` imported and unused in AdminConfig; dropped.

**Marigold V2 and the Viggle Turbo bundle removed, with 4.92 GB of weights (owner, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"we can remove the workflows and models for marigold, aswell as for the viggle turbo version of qwen"*. 68 → **65 bundles**: `utility_marigold_v2_depth`, `utility_marigold_v2_normals`, `image_qwen_image_2_1_viggle_turbo`. None of the three was active or held a lane.

- ★★ **Read the bundle's model list literally and you delete a 19 GB production model.** Marigold declares `qwen_image_edit_2509_fp8_e4m3fn.safetensors` because it is *built on* Qwen-Image-Edit — that file serves [image_edit_qwen_multiple_scene_angles](workflows/image_edit_qwen_multiple_scene_angles/). Viggle likewise declares the Qwen 2.1 base trio shared with [image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i/). **35.13 GB of the 11 files these three bundles named had to stay.** A removal must cross-reference, never trust the meta's list as "its own" models.
- **The cross-reference that decided it** — keep-set built from 198 workflow files: every other bundle's **api.json AND its _template.json** (a human opens the template in ComfyUI, so a weight it loads is still in use), every candidate in `_candidate_workflows/`, `J:/_demo_workflows`, ComfyUI's own `user/default/workflows`, every other bundle's `requirements.models`, and ★ **the `lora` dropdowns' `optionsFilter` prefixes** — a dropdown exposes *every* file matching its prefix, so those are reachable without being named in any graph. Exclusive after all that: **8 files, 4.92 GB** (the 1.27 GB Viggle adapter, 6 marigold_v2 files, and a `marigold_v2_albedo_conditioning` that no workflow ever wired). The three empty `marigold_v2` folders went too.
- ★ **A deletion script must hard-code its targets, not glob them.** The list came from the analysis and was re-checked against a `(marigold_v2|Qwen-Image-2.1-viggle-turbo)` allow-pattern before each `rm`; a glob over "marigold" would have been fine here and is exactly the habit that takes a shared file one day.
- ★ **The backslash trap bit the analysis itself.** Marigold's graph names its files the way ComfyUI reports them on Windows — `marigold_v2\marigold_v2_depth_log_stage2.safetensors` — and the first version of the script lost its `[\\/]` basename split to heredoc escaping, so all six marigold files read "not on disk" and would have been silently skipped. The estimator itself splits correctly; the throwaway script did not. **When a file that is plainly there reports missing, suspect the script.**
- `MarigoldV2PostProcess` is **comfy-core**, not a node pack, so there was nothing to uninstall. 65 bundles, 0 flagged for a missing model, `test:storyboard` green, and the four shared Qwen weights verified present afterwards.
- [docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md) §6b rewritten from "merge them behind a Fast toggle" to the outcome: **a fast mode that ghosts on any multi-subject prompt is worse to hand a student than no fast mode**, because the failure reads as bad luck rather than a setting.

**Model readiness on the workflow card — and the flag was lying before it was ever shown (owner request, 2026-10-05)** — owner asked to move `_maintenance` into ComfyQ, reorganise the admin panel into tabs (Workflows / Manage server / Manage ComfyUI / Maintenance), and show per card which models are installed, which are missing, and where to get them. **This is step 1 of that: make the check correct, then show it.**

- ★★ **The check already existed, was already on the wire, and already reported a false alarm.** `estimateWorkflowVram().unresolved` — model names in the graph not found on disk — has been attached to every summary by [routes/workflows.js](server/routes/workflows.js) since 2026-09-21, surfaced only as tooltip text. But it scanned `<root>/models/**` alone, and a node pack that fetches its own weights keeps them inside itself: `comfyui_controlnet_aux` puts the DWPose pair in `custom_nodes/comfyui_controlnet_aux/ckpts/`. So **[video_edit_ltx2_5_iclora_pose_control](workflows/video_edit_ltx2_5_iclora_pose_control/) reported two missing models on a rig where they are installed and the bundle runs fine.** ★ **A false "missing" is worse than no flag at all** — it teaches an admin to ignore the one signal that says a workflow cannot run here. The index now walks `custom_nodes/` as well, pruning `.git`/`node_modules`/`__pycache__`/`.cache`: **304 weight files indexed in 39 ms, 24 of them inside node packs, and all 68 bundles now read clean.** That bundle's estimate rose 37.59 → **37.92 GB**, which is the honest figure.
- **`models/` is walked first and first match wins**, mirroring ComfyUI's own `folder_paths` lookup order, so a filename present in both places resolves to the copy under `models/`.
- ★ **New `unresolvedInactive`** — missing, but only reachable through a switch branch the defaults do not take. Such a bundle runs today and fails the moment a student flips Fast mode, so it is reported apart from `unresolved` and shown amber rather than red. All three states verified against the real install.
- **Components now carry `rel`**, the path relative to the install root (`models/unet/x.safetensors`, `custom_nodes/…/ckpts/yolox_l.torchscript.pt`) — the "link to the models on disk" the card wanted. Nothing consumed `rel` before, so re-basing it from the models dir to the install root cost nothing.
- **New [ModelReadiness.jsx](client/src/components/admin/ModelReadiness.jsx)** on every library card: a chip (grey *N models on disk* / amber / red *N models missing*) that expands to the file list with sizes, kinds and on-disk paths. ★ **It reads the GRAPH, not `requirements.models`.** The hand-written meta list cannot serve as a readiness check: of its 294 entries some are prose (`"InsightFace + MediaPipe face croppers (auto-downloaded)"`), some are HuggingFace repo ids (`microsoft/TRELLIS.2-4B`), and six carry a Windows backslash subpath — all of which would read "missing" for ever on every rig. They get cleaned up when provenance is harvested.
- **Provenance groundwork, measured — the download URLs are mostly already in the repo** (owner's lead: "most of the original ComfyUI workflows should have them in comment notes"). **53 of 68 templates carry URLs in their note nodes (282 HuggingFace links)**, many of them `/resolve/main/<subfolder>/<file>`, i.e. destination folder included. Per bundle that covers **199/294 declared models (68%)**; ★ **pooled into one global filename → URL index across all bundles it reaches 248/294 (84%)**, because a shared weight like `flux-2-klein-9b-fp8` only needs *one* bundle to document it. The `_maintenance` audit CSV's `redownload` column adds 13 more (**89%**), leaving ~12 files for hand work — and CLAUDE.md already names most of those sources. **Harvest globally by filename, not per bundle.**
- ⚠ **The model index is cached for 30 s**, so a freshly downloaded model will not clear a red flag at once. Invalidate it explicitly when the download feature lands.
- **Tests:** [vramEstimate.test.js](server/workflows/vramEstimate.test.js) 14 → **22 checks** (a node-pack weight counts as installed, a git object is never indexed, `models/` wins a name clash, every component carries a forward-slash install-relative path). ★ Writing the new fixtures beside their assertions failed first time: the 30 s index cache makes a file created after the first estimate invisible, so fixtures belong at the top with the others.
- **Sequenced next:** admin tabs as their own commit (it is a mechanical pass over a 1392-line file, so it must not be entangled with behaviour) · harvest provenance into the metas · the `_maintenance` move, splitting versioned scripts from the per-machine generated snapshots (`object_info.json` 4.4 MB, `links.json`, `inventory.json` — committing those would commit a lie about every other rig) · then server-side downloads and node-pack installs. ★ **Downloads must be server-side, not browser-side:** the admin panel is routinely opened from another machine (there is a *Copy admin link* button for exactly that), so a browser download lands on the wrong computer — and `showDirectoryPicker()`, the only API that could choose a folder, needs a secure context, which plain-HTTP-on-the-LAN is not.

## Latest progress (2026-10-05, later)

**The SD3.5 bundle removed, and the Qwen unblur adapter finally SEEN working — it beats its own author's warning (owner, 2026-10-05)** — owner: *"the creative re-render is no use, we can remove them / seedvr and qwen unblur are good fit"*.

- **Removed [utility_sd35_creative_upscale]** — 69 → 68 bundles. Nothing referenced it (`activeWorkflowId` and `extraLaneWorkflowIds` both clear of it). ⚠ **The 13.91 GB `sd3.5_large_fp8_scaled.safetensors` and the 8.06 GB `sd3.5_large_controlnet_blur.safetensors` are still on disk** — deliberately left, since SD3.5 renders cleanly at 1 MP and is a perfectly good plain t2i model if one is ever wanted. Delete them if the space is needed.
- ★★ **The perf-flag masking worked end to end, on the owner's own action.** They hit *Activate & serve* on the unblur bundle; ComfyQ relaunched ComfyUI **without `--use-sage-attention`** while correctly leaving `--fast fp16_accumulation` on (the bundle disables only sage). Verified in `/system_stats` argv. **So no restart was ever needed from me** — `requirements.disabledPerfFlags` is the whole mechanism, and the operator's normal action triggers it.
- ★★ **[utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale](workflows/utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale/) is verified and calibrated: 4 s warm / 13 s cold / 28.55 GB.** No black images (mean luma 134, i.e. the sage failure mode is genuinely gone). ★ **And it contradicts its own model card:** the author warns *"the base model produces better overall quality, but it does not preserve those patterns"* — measured here on a 391×521 blurred face at adapter strength 1.0 vs 0.0, **the adapter is clearly better**, resolving individual hair strands where the base model alone leaves them smeared. The A/B is one field (adapter strength 0), which is what made the comparison cheap. **4 s is Fast mode** (4-step Lightning, the default); the meta estimate was aligned to the measured value.
- **The library's upscalers now read clearly:** **SeedVR2** for faithful restoration at any size or aspect (and its size control works since the 2026-10-01 fix), **Qwen Edit unblur** for blurred faces specifically at about 1 MP, **PiD** for an exact 4× enlargement that adds little. That is three tools with three jobs, not three near-duplicates.

## Latest progress (2026-10-05)

**SD3.5 Large + Stability's blur ControlNet, built and measured — and it LOSES to SeedVR2, which is the useful result (owner request, 2026-10-05)** ⚠ **The bundle was REMOVED the same day on the owner's call (*"the creative re-render is no use"*); the findings below are kept because they are the reason.** — owner: *"Yes lets pull sd3.5 and build the bundle"*. Built **[utility_sd35_creative_upscale](workflows/utility_sd35_creative_upscale/)** (12 nodes, 8 params, experimental, **25 s warm / 31 s cold / 20.46 GB**). ⚠ **It is shipped clearly labelled as the weaker option; SeedVR2 remains the upscaler to reach for.**

- **One download covered everything.** `Comfy-Org/stable-diffusion-3.5-fp8` → `sd3.5_large_fp8_scaled.safetensors` is a **complete checkpoint** — diffusion model + VAE + **all three** text encoders including the `clip_g` this rig was missing (verified by parsing its header over an HTTP range request before downloading: 3041 tensors, `model.diffusion_model` + `first_stage_model` + `text_encoders.{clip_l,clip_g,t5xxl}`). So `CheckpointLoaderSimple` alone, no `TripleCLIPLoader`, and the separate 1.29 GB clip_g download was unnecessary. 13.91 GB total. **ComfyUI saw it with no restart.**
- ★★ **Measured verdict, by opening 1:1 crops rather than trusting dimensions: SeedVR2 wins on both a claymation still and a photographic portrait.** SeedVR2 resolves individual hair strands cleanly and rebuilds convincing clay fibre; SD3.5 gives coarse spiky clumps and a persistent **canvas-weave texture** across flat areas like skin, at every size tried (1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 3.1 MP). The weave is the tell.
- ★★ **SD3.5 Large cannot be pushed past about 1 MP, which disqualifies it as an upscaler backbone.** Isolation test — ControlNet strength **0**, denoise 1.0, 1248×1664 (2.08 MP) — returns **blocky garbage at the DiT patch grid**, while the identical setup at **1024×1024 renders a clean photograph**. So the model, the fp8 quantisation, the encoders, the VAE and the rig's perf flags are all fine; the failure is purely resolution. The blur ControlNet masks the collapse (it holds structure) but cannot restore the quality. ★ **That isolation run is what turned a vague "this looks worse" into a diagnosis — run the generator alone before blaming the ControlNet.**
- ★ **The first build was wired wrong and the right fix was counter-intuitive: denoise must be 1.0.** Starting from the VAE-encoded *blurry* picture at denoise 0.55 keeps the blur — the thing the bundle exists to remove — because the blur ControlNet also pulls back toward that same blurry reference. Full denoise, with structure coming only from the ControlNet, is Stability's intended use and is visibly better. Below ControlNet strength ~0.7 the model starts weaving canvas texture into flat areas, so 0.9 is the usable setting.
- ★ **Node trap worth remembering: `ImageResize+` does NOT snap its OUTPUT to `multiple_of`** — it snaps the requested *box* before the aspect maths (`comfyui_essentials/image.py`), so 1234×567 asking for width 2048 yields height **941**, which breaks SD3's /16 patch grid. **`ImageScaleToTotalPixels` with `resolution_steps: 16` is the right node** — core, and it rounds *both* output dimensions onto the grid while preserving aspect. Verified: 1234×567 → 2624×1200 and 433×577 → 1248×1664, both /16, aspect held.
- **Where it still earns its place:** it re-imagines rather than restores, it takes a prompt, and it can invent detail on material too far gone for a restorer — but only up to about 1–1.5 MP. **The real path to a Magnific equivalent is tiling**: run the blur ControlNet at ~1 MP per tile, which is exactly how Magnific works and exactly what sidesteps SD3.5's ceiling. That needs a tiled-diffusion / Ultimate-SD-Upscale pack, which is **not installed** (the usable primitives today are `ImageTile+`/`ImageUntile+`, `easy kSamplerTiled` and PiD's own tiled decoder).

## Latest progress (2026-10-01, later)

**"An upscaler like Magnific that works at odd resolutions" — the rig already had one, and the bundle's own label was hiding it (owner question, 2026-10-01)** — owner: *"Do you know of any other upscale, unblur workflow the kind magnific AI did … We need a comfyUI workflow for comfyQ that can work at odd resolutions"*. No new bundle was needed; **[utility_seedvr2_4k_upscale](workflows/utility_seedvr2_4k_upscale/)** was mislabelled and half-disabled.

- ★★ **Two bugs in one card, and together they look exactly like "it cannot do odd resolutions".** (1) The size param was labelled **"Target resolution (px, longest edge)"** but `SeedVR2VideoUpscaler.resolution` is *"Target resolution for the **shortest** edge"* — on a square test picture the two coincide, so it reads correctly and only lies on non-square input. (2) The graph hardcodes **`max_resolution: 4096`**, a cap on the longest edge that **wins over** the resolution setting. **Measured on a deliberately awkward 1234×567 (neither side divisible by 8, aspect 2.1764): asking for 2048 and asking for 4096 return the byte-identical picture, 4096×1882** — the cap decides and the student's slider does nothing. Lift the cap to 8192 and the same request returns **4456×2048**, i.e. exactly the short edge asked for with the aspect held to one pixel of rounding. **Fix is meta-only**: the label now says SHORTEST edge, `max_resolution` is exposed as an honest cap (default still 4096, so existing classes see no change), and the description works the example through. api.json and template untouched → no template regeneration, and the calibration graph is unchanged so **8.63 GB stands**.
- ★ **SeedVR2 is the Magnific-like tool here, and it is not close.** Same 1234×567 blurred input, 1:1 crops compared by eye: **PiD 4×** returns a clean but still soft upscale that invents almost no texture, while **SeedVR2** reconstructs real clay grain, fine creases and pores. ★ **PiD also crops to a multiple of 8** — 1234×567 came back 4928×2240, i.e. 4× of 1232×560, silently losing 2×7 px and shifting the aspect to 2.2000. SeedVR2 preserved 2.1764 exactly. For odd sizes, prefer SeedVR2.
- ★ **SeedVR2's `input_noise_scale` / `latent_noise_scale` are NOT a Magnific "creativity" slider — do not ship them as one.** `latent_noise_scale: 0.35` does not add invented detail, it **destroys the picture** into blur (mean difference 5.9/255, and the crop is mush). They were tested precisely because they looked like the obvious creativity control. Left at 0.
- **What a real Magnific equivalent would need, and what is already on disk** (checked, nothing installed by this work): `SUPIRApply` **exists in comfy-core** (`model/patch/supir`) but there are **no SUPIR weights** and it needs an SDXL base. **`sd3.5_large_controlnet_blur.safetensors` is on the rig (8.06 GB)** — Stability's own upscale/deblur ControlNet, the nearest thing to Magnific's method — but **the SD3.5 Large base and `clip_g` are missing**, so it is ~16 GB of downloads from usable. No Ultimate SD Upscale / tiled-diffusion / SUPIR pack is installed; the usable tiling primitives are `ImageTile+`/`ImageUntile+` (essentials), `easy kSamplerTiled` (needs an EasyUse PIPE_LINE) and PiD's own tiled decoder. One ESRGAN model, `4x_foolhardy_Remacri.pth`.
- **Rig tidy-up noted, not actioned:** three abandoned Chrome part-files sit in `models/controlnet/` — `124177.crdownload` (0.29 GB), `261122.crdownload` (0.25 GB), `777406.crdownload` (0.59 GB). Dead weight, and a hint that something the owner wanted never finished downloading.

## Latest progress (2026-10-01)

**Qwen-Image-Edit 2511 "Unblur / Upscale" adapter as an experimental utility — built but NOT yet rendered, because testing it needs a restart the owner is holding (owner request, 2026-10-01)** — owner: *"I would like to test this model, can you spin up an experimental workflow … restart on my demand"*. New **[utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale](workflows/utility_qwen_2511_unblur_upscale/)** (25 nodes, 6 params, experimental, category **`i2i`** so it sits with the other upscalers).

- ⚠ **NOT verified by rendering, and that is deliberate.** Qwen-Image-Edit + `--use-sage-attention` **saves all-black images** with no error (the 2026-09-14 finding), and this rig runs Sage globally — the serving ComfyUI's argv confirms `--use-sage-attention --fast fp16_accumulation`. So any test render right now would be black for a reason that has nothing to do with the adapter. The bundle declares `requirements.disabledPerfFlags: ["use_sage_attention"]`, which means **ComfyQ masks the flag by itself the moment the bundle is activated or calibrated** — the owner's own "Activate & serve" performs the restart, so no restart was needed from me. Until then it has **no `runtime.json`**; `estimatedDurationSec: 50` is borrowed from the sibling bundle on the same 38 GB base, not measured.
- ★★ **The author's own warning is the headline and is in the bundle description:** *"This adapter was experimental and trained to recover facial patterns from partially blurred images. **The base model produces better overall quality, but it does not preserve those patterns.**"* So it is a **trade, not an improvement** — it holds a face's identity at the cost of overall cleanliness. "Adapter strength 0" gives the base model on the same picture, which is the comparison that matters here.
- **Key mapping: 720/720 modules, 0 unmapped — onto BOTH bases.** Rank 16, **no DoRA**, touching **all 60 blocks** on `attn.{to_q,to_k,to_v,to_out.0,add_q_proj,add_k_proj,add_v_proj,to_add_out}` + `img_mlp.net.2`, `txt_mlp.net.2`, `img_mod.1`, `txt_mod.1`. ★ Two naming traps, both of which first read as "this LoRA does not fit": (1) the keys are **PEFT form `…lora_A.default.weight`** — ComfyUI handles it explicitly and the variable in `comfy/weight_adapter/lora.py:166` is even called **`qwen_default_lora`**, so it is a supported Qwen case, not a problem; (2) **`qwen_image_edit_2509_fp8` stores its keys with a `model.diffusion_model.` prefix while `2511_bf16` does not** — a storage convention ComfyUI strips, so a naive comparison reports 0/720 against 2509 and 720/720 against 2511. Normalised, both map fully, which matches the card's "Compatible with versions 2509 and 2511". **Built on 2511** anyway: it is the adapter's native base, so the test measures the adapter rather than cross-base transfer.
- **Four checkpoints installed** (`_5/_10/_15/_20`, 225 MB each, same rank) and exposed through the existing **`lora` dropdown type** with `optionsFilter: 'Qwen-Image-Edit-Unblur-Upscale'` — so comparing the training stages is a dropdown, not four bundles. Author recommends 20 or 15. **No restart was needed for ComfyUI to see the new files** — it picked all four up in `/object_info` immediately.
- **Trigger phrase is literally `unblur and upscale`** and it is the prompt default. Licence is **Apache 2.0** for the adapter, unusually permissive for this family (the Qwen Edit base it runs on is still Qwen Research).
- **It re-renders at roughly 1 MP, it is not an enlarger** — `FluxKontextImageScale` normalises whatever is uploaded, so it genuinely enlarges a small or soft picture but will not make a poster. The description says so and points at the PiD (exactly 4×) and SeedVR2 bundles for pure enlargement, and warns that a re-render can quietly change a face or turn small print to nonsense.
- ★ **Inherited schema drift caught by the template round-trip:** the sibling's api.json never set `ModelSamplingAuraFlow.sampling`, which is an **optional** input defaulting to `"flow"` — harmless at runtime, but the generated template gains it and the round-trip diff refused to write. Stating it explicitly in the api.json fixed it. **The same omission is still in [image_edit_qwen_multiple_character_angles](workflows/image_edit_qwen_multiple_character_angles/)** — behaviourally irrelevant, worth tidying if that bundle is ever touched.

## Latest progress (2026-09-30)

**The community "Qwen-Image-2.1-Fix" adapter, as a comparison bundle — and most of the gain turns out to be the SETTINGS, not the LoRA (owner request, 2026-09-30)** — owner: *"I would like to test this: e-n-v-y/Qwen-Image-2.1-Fix. Can you do a workflow based on the classic qwen2.1 t2i we already have so I can compare results"*. New **[image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i_fix](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i_fix/)** (experimental, **46 s warm / 51 s cold / 15.08 GB**).

- ★★ **The LoRA is NOT the change — the author's settings are half of it, and the README does not say so.** The README is two sentences (*"A lora that, when used with the proper settings, fixes most of the image generation issues with Qwen Image 2.1. Download the workflow zip for the correct settings."*) and the settings are only inside `Qwen Edit 2.1 Optimal Settings.json.zip`. Unpacked, they are: **`UNET → LoraLoaderModelOnly(1.0) → APG → FreSca → KSampler`, steps 20, cfg 3, sampler `seeds_2`, scheduler `sgm_uniform`** — where the stock bundle is 25 steps, **cfg 1**, euler/simple. `APG (eta 1, norm_threshold 10, momentum 0.3)` and `FreSca (scale_low 1, scale_high 2, freq_cutoff 8)` are both **comfy-core**, and `seeds_2`/`sgm_uniform` are both in this ComfyUI's enums, so nothing new was installed but the adapter.
- ★★ **Measured on the rig, and the isolation test is the interesting one.** Two prompts, one seed, three configurations: **A** plain bundle at its defaults · **B** this bundle at its defaults · **C** this bundle with **Fix strength 0** (the author's settings, adapter off). Looking at the pictures: A renders a watch dial with garbled numerals and imperfect hands; **B** gives the glossiest photograph (contrast, shallow depth of field, micro-detail) but **changes composition a lot** — it turned a close-up into a full figure, ignored "seen from above", and on the violinist produced an anatomically impossible bowing arm; **C kept A's composition and prompt adherence while gaining most of B's detail, and had the cleanest hands of the three.** So a good part of what looks like "the fix" is cfg 3 + `seeds_2` + FreSca, not the DoRA. Two prompts at one seed is not a study — which is why the bundle exposes **Fix strength (0 disables the adapter, everything else identical)** so the comparison is one field, not a second bundle.
- ★ **It is 3.3× slower and that is arithmetic, not a fault:** 46 s vs the plain bundle's 14 s. cfg 3 makes the model run twice per step (the stock cfg of 1 has no negative pass at all) and `seeds_2` is a second-order solver that runs it twice again → **20×2×2 = 80 model evaluations against 25×1×1 = 25, a predicted 3.20× against a measured 3.29×.** VRAM is unchanged (15.08 vs 14.44 GB) because a DoRA merges into the computation rather than adding resident weight.
- **It is a DoRA, rank 32, and it maps cleanly** — 106 MB, keys already in ComfyUI `diffusion_model.transformer_blocks.N.…` naming (hence the file's `-comfy` suffix), touching **blocks 0–21 of 32** on `attn.to_q/k/v/out` + `img_mlp.gate_up/out`. Checked against this rig's fused int8-convrot UNET header the way the Viggle LoRA was: **132/132 modules mapped, 0 unmapped**. ★ Note it ships `dora_scale` tensors — a key-mapping script that strips only `lora_A/B/alpha` will report half the modules unmapped, which is a false alarm, not a broken adapter. The author's own workflow loads `qwen_image_2.1_int8_convrot.safetensors`, i.e. the exact build this rig runs.
- **cfg 3 is what gives this bundle a negative prompt at all.** The plain bundle deliberately has none (at cfg 1 the negative branch is inert). The default is the author's own list from the zip (*"artifacts, gpt-image, washed-out colors, low quality, … badly drawn hands, wrong number of fingers"*).
- ★ **Pre-existing bug fixed in passing: `image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i`'s api literal had drifted from its meta default** (literal = a fashion-editorial portrait, default = the Parisian café sign) — the same trap the ControlNet bundles hit on 2026-09-25, which fires whenever the parameter is not sent. Literal realigned. ★ **Its template was patched in place, NOT regenerated**: regenerating flattened a subgraph template with 2 MarkdownNotes into 10 plain nodes, so it was restored from git and only the prompt widget was rewritten. **Reach for `api-to-ui` when a bundle has no template or its graph changed shape — not for a widget value.**
- The zip is titled "Qwen **Edit** 2.1 Optimal Settings" and its notes/SaveImage prefix still say FLUX.2-Klein: it is an adapted template, and its `LoadImage` is bypassed. It is a **t2i** recipe despite the name.

## Latest progress (2026-09-27, later still)

**The ControlNet node pack lives in `custom_nodes` as a plain git clone — the junction is gone (owner question, 2026-09-27)** — owner asked why `J:\ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet` existed at all, said it belongs in the portable install's `custom_nodes`, and asked for the procedure to be disambiguated. It was right to ask.

- ★★ **The junction was an outright bug on this fleet, not just untidy.** A Windows directory junction stores an **absolute** target — it read `J:\ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet`. The NVMe mounts under a **different drive letter on different rigs**, so on any machine where it is not `J:` the junction dangles and the pack silently disappears. ★ And the failure would not look like a missing pack: ComfyUI's built-in `load_controlnet_qwen_fun` **accepts** this checkpoint and loads 5 of its 16 blocks at the wrong layers (see the 2026-09-25 entry), so the rig would render **quietly wrong pictures** instead of erroring. I had chosen the junction to honour "do not touch the portable install" — but that rule is about not modifying **ComfyUI's own code**; `custom_nodes/` is the supported extension point where the other 47 packs already live, so a clone there was always the correct reading.
- **Now:** `<ComfyUI>/custom_nodes/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet` is a real directory and a real git clone with `origin` set. **The clone IS the working copy** — edit in place, commit, push; `git pull` + restart ComfyUI to update a rig; `git clone` into `custom_nodes` to install a new one. No second checkout, nothing to keep in sync, no absolute path anywhere. The out-of-tree copy was deleted after verifying it was clean, fully pushed, and byte-identical to the clone apart from git's CRLF conversion.
- ★ **Removing a junction: use `cmd /c rmdir <path>`, never `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force`** — the latter can follow the reparse point and delete the **target's** contents, which here was the only local copy of the pack.
- **Verified without disturbing the serving rig:** ComfyQ refuses a ComfyUI restart in student mode (by design), and the running process holds the old modules in memory, so it proves nothing about the new path. Instead a throwaway **`--cpu` ComfyUI on port 8199** with its own `--user-directory`/`--temp-directory` was booted: its log shows the pack loading from `...\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet`, no import failures, and `/object_info` lists **both** node classes with the checkpoint visible to the loader. The serving instance then rendered a canny job correctly. ⚠ **The serving ComfyUI still has its modules loaded from the path the junction used to occupy**; it picks up the clone at its next restart (any "Activate & serve", or an admin restart).
- **The pack's README already said "clone into `custom_nodes`"** — the junction was a local deviation from the project's own published instructions, which is exactly how this kind of drift hides. README now also documents updating and that the clone is the working copy ([735e4b8](https://github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet)).

## Latest progress (2026-09-27, later)

**Qwen 2.1 image edit — every reference picture optional, all 16 the model supports, and no pictures means plain text-to-image (owner request, 2026-09-27)** — owner: *"I would like to be able to edit images with optional reference: no images means just by text and we should be able to add as many images as the model supports."* [image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit/) went from 10 slots with image 1 **required** to **16 slots, every one optional**.

- **16 is the model's own maximum, read off the live node, not guessed.** `TextEncodeQwenImage21`'s autogrow declares `names=[image_1 … image_16], min=0` (`comfy_extras/nodes_qwen.py`) — so sixteen slots and **zero** images are both explicitly legal. Verified by POSTing a graph with every `images.*` key deleted: accepted, rendered.
- ★★ **The trap: with no pictures the encoder's LATENT output is a fixed 1024×1024 SQUARE.** `latent_w = latent_h = resolution or 1024`, and this bundle sets `resolution: 0` → 1024. So simply unlinking every slot gives a graph that runs, succeeds, and **silently ignores the student's aspect ratio**. Measured on the rig: the same prompt asking for 16:9 came back **1024×1024**. The graph already had the `ComfySwitchNode` that picks between the encoder latent and an `EmptyLatentImage` on the `ResolutionSelector` — it just had to be flipped by something.
- ★ **New generic param field `whenEmptySet`** ([optionalMedia.js](server/workers/optionalMedia.js) `applyWhenEmptySet`, applied in `worker.submit` between `unlink` and materialization): `[{ nodeId, field, value }]` set when the param is left **empty**, so the graph takes its other branch by itself. It is the companion to `whenEmpty: "unlink"` — removing an unused loader is only half the job when the rest of the graph still expects it — and the two judge emptiness identically **on purpose**, because a slot unlinked without its switch flipped is exactly the broken graph this prevents. It also replaces the "toggle above the image field" pattern (LTX 2.5 deblur, union-control): a toggle that disagrees with what was actually uploaded is a silently wrong result, and nothing in the form shows it. 8 new tests in [optionalMedia.test.js](server/workers/optionalMedia.test.js).
- ★ **Pre-existing bug found and fixed: `whenEmpty` was in NEITHER editor round-trip list**, so an admin opening any bundle in the metadata editor and saving **silently dropped `unlink`** — turning unused reference slots back into black placeholder images the model actually looks at. Affected the Qwen edit, the Krea 2 style reference and the FastVideo i2v. Both `whenEmpty` and `whenEmptySet` are now preserved in the `edit-data` merge ([admin.js](server/routes/admin.js)) and the editor's save payload ([WorkflowMetaEditor.jsx](client/src/components/admin/WorkflowMetaEditor.jsx)), the same way `disabledWhen`/`linkedValues` are.
- **Verified by rendering, not by graph shape.** Five input combinations produce a valid graph with no dangling links (0/1/3/16 pictures, and a reference with no image 1). Three were rendered through ComfyQ's **own** submit transforms and looked at: **no pictures → 1376×768** (16:9 honoured, a clean lighthouse); **image 1 only (1536×1024)** → **1536×1024**, the claymation portrait kept exactly — same face, pose, framing, clay texture — with the season changed to winter, and the 16:9 request correctly **ignored**; **image 1 + image 2** → the man from `<image2>` composited into `<image1>`'s scene at image 1's size.
- **Output size rule, now stated in the bundle description:** with Image 1 the result follows it (the encoder's tooltip is explicit that any other size *shifts the edit*); with nothing uploaded the aspect-ratio and megapixel fields decide. They cannot be greyed out when a picture is present — `disabledWhen` only tests equality against a known value, and an upload's value is an unpredictable filename — so the labels say so instead.
- **No recalibration needed, and that was checked rather than assumed:** calibration's `warmupParams` leave all fifteen extra slots `null`, so the measured graph is still 12 nodes with one image — identical in shape to the 2026-09-22 run. **15 s warm / 15.97 GB stand.**
- **The template was regenerated and it mattered: the old one was already STALE** — 2 `LoadImage` nodes against the api.json's 10, unnoticed since 2026-09-21, because the bundle's extra loaders were only ever added to the api.json. ★ **Note that the new `bundleCompleteness` test does NOT catch this** — it checks class composition, and `LoadImage` was present. Staleness in general is not decidable from the two files (a subgraph template legitimately differs from its flattened api.json); the round-trip through [tools/api-to-ui](tools/api-to-ui/README.md) is what actually proves a match. Regenerating flattened the subgraph, so the model-download note was carried across by hand and a rewritten usage note added; re-exported through `ui-to-api` and diffed: **0 differences**, and `MarkdownNote` confirmed absent from the prompt.
- **[docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md) §6a** — this bundle is now a strict superset of `image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i`. **Recommendation is still to keep both**: the t2i card shows 3 fields, this one shows 21, sixteen of them uploads a generation-only class will never touch.

## Latest progress (2026-09-27)

**Every bundle now ships an editable ComfyUI graph too — and a converter that makes one from an api.json (owner request, 2026-09-27)** — owner: *"You only built api versions of the workflow so I cannot test them directly in comfyUI, I want ... a human readable version and an api version for each workflow"*. The two ControlNet bundles built on 2026-09-25 had an `.api.json` only. **All 66 bundles now carry both files.**

- ★★ **A missing template is INVISIBLE, which is why this went unnoticed.** The registry treats it as a degraded feature, not an error: `hasTemplate` goes false and "Open in ComfyUI" silently falls back to download-and-drag. The bundle still loads, calibrates, serves and passes every test — it is only discovered by an admin trying to open the graph. So the rule is now a **test**, not a note: [bundleCompleteness.test.js](server/workflows/bundleCompleteness.test.js) (5 checks, in `test:storyboard`) fails when a bundle has no template, when the filename is not canonical, when the "template" is really a second copy of the api.json (which would satisfy a naive existence check and load as an **empty graph**), or when it shares no node class with the graph that runs. Its failure message names the exact command to fix it.
- **New [tools/api-to-ui/convert.mjs](tools/api-to-ui/README.md)** — the mirror of [ui-to-api](tools/ui-to-api/convert.mjs), same trick: drive ComfyUI's **own frontend** in headless Chrome. `app.loadApiJson()` builds real nodes from the live node defs, wires the links and calls `graph.arrange()` twice; `graph.serialize()` writes the UI format. Widget order, input slots and node sizes come from ComfyUI, not from our guesses.
- ★ **It refuses two things that both fail silently by hand.** (1) **An unknown `class_type` does not throw** — `loadApiJson` swaps in a placeholder node carrying `has_errors`, so a missing node pack yields a template that opens looking almost right and cannot run; any unresolved node aborts that bundle and names it. (2) Every generated template is loaded straight back and exported through **`graphToPrompt()`** (the Export-(API) path) and **diffed against the api.json it came from, input by input** — a mismatch is printed and nothing is written. Both guards were verified by deliberately breaking an api.json: an unregistered class and an input field the node def does not declare were each caught, with no file written, exit code 1.
- **Generated: [image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny/) (14 nodes) and [image_qwen_image_2_1_control_depth](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_control_depth/) (15), both round-trip identical.** The custom `QwenImage21FunControlNetLoader`/`Apply` resolved to real node types (they would have become placeholders had the pack not been installed). Staged through the real `POST /admin/workflows/:id/open-in-comfyui` route and confirmed on disk in ComfyUI's workflows dir.
- **What a generated template is NOT:** ComfyUI's `arrange()` gives a tidy left-to-right layout but **no groups, notes or subgraphs** — those exist only in a hand-authored or editor-exported template. A bundle converted *from* a UI graph keeps its original; this is for bundles built api-first. Each generated one gets a single `Note` naming the bundle and saying ComfyQ runs the api.json, so an admin who edits the graph knows to re-export through `ui-to-api` to change what executes (`Note` is frontend-only and never reaches the prompt — the round-trip check proves it rather than assuming it).

## Latest progress (2026-09-25)

**ControlNet for Qwen-Image 2.1 — a published node pack, and two experimental bundles (owner request, 2026-09-25)** — owner asked whether `alibaba-pai/Qwen-Image-2.1-Fun-Controlnet-Union` could be used in ComfyQ, then asked for a community node pack and two bundles to test.

- ★★ **ComfyUI cannot load this checkpoint, and fails SILENTLY.** `comfy/controlnet.py: load_controlnet_qwen_fun` is hardwired for the *original* Qwen-Image — `num_control_blocks=5`, `main_model_double=60`, `injection_layers=(0,12,24,36,48)`, `latent_format=Wan21()`. The 2.1 file is **16** control blocks over a **32**-block model injecting every second layer, 64-channel latents, **129** control features (not 132). The detection branch keys only on `control_blocks.0.after_proj.weight` + `control_img_in.weight`, which the 2.1 file also has, so it **is** routed there — and `controlnet_load_state_dict` uses `load_state_dict(strict=False)`, warning on missing keys and logging unexpected ones at **debug** level. You would get a controlnet holding 5 of 16 blocks, injecting at layers 36 and 48 of a 32-block model, with nothing in the console. Upstream `master` is identical and 0.37.0 is the current release, so updating does not help.
- **Published: [github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet](https://github.com/b2renger/ComfyUI-QwenImage21-FunControlNet)** (MIT code; weights are Qwen Research and are neither included nor downloaded). ⚠ **This originally said the pack was a separate checkout linked into `custom_nodes` as a directory junction — that was wrong and was undone on 2026-09-27 (see below).** It is an ordinary `git clone` inside `custom_nodes`, like every other pack.
- ★ **Nothing in the ComfyUI install is patched.** Two hooks carry it: a `WrappersMP.DIFFUSION_MODEL` wrapper runs the control chain (it needs the same `mod`/`attn_fn` the base blocks get, and `post_input` fires *before* those exist), and a `single_block` patch does the injection. Registering a `single_block` patch also sets `hooked` in the 2.1 forward, which **disables the prefix cache** — load-bearing, since a cached step runs target rows only and the hints would stop lining up.
- **The algorithm** (from Alibaba's `videox_fun/models/qwenimage21_transformer2d_control.py`): the chain runs **once per step, up front** — not interleaved. Block 0 seeds it with `before_proj(control) + joint_stream`, each block emits `after_proj(c)`, and base block *k* adds `hints[k] * control_context_scale`.
- ★★ **The control latent MUST go through `latent_format.process_in()`.** `VAE.encode()` returns the raw latent while the sampler's `x` is already normalised, and `QwenImage21.process_in` is `(latent - mean) / std` per channel with std ≈ 3.5–3.9. Without it the picture **still follows the control structure** but comes back smeared, washed out and vertically streaked — which reads as "the controlnet is too strong" and would cost a day of tuning strength. Found only by opening the image.
- ★ **The control block must be built `fused_mlp=False`** — the checkpoint stores the MLP unfused (`gate_layer`/`proj`) while the 2.1 UNET on disk is fused (`gate_up`); building it fused leaves those weights unmatched and **silently random**. The pack's `detect_control_config()` reads block count and widths off the file, and the loader **refuses** a mismatched layout instead of loading part of it.
- **New bundles (experimental):** [image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_control_canny/) (**16 s warm, 22.56 GB**) and [image_qwen_image_2_1_control_depth](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_control_depth/) (**19 s, 24.07 GB**). Both wrap the base Qwen 2.1 t2i graph, scale the uploaded picture to the chosen output size with `ImageScale` (so the control latent aligns cell for cell rather than being resampled), and expose control strength. Category **`t2i`, not image-edit** — structure guidance is generation by the same rule the owner applied to the Krea 2 style reference: nothing of the supplied picture survives as content. **Verified by eye:** canny traces outlines exactly, depth keeps volume and massing while inventing the detail, and strength 0 on the same seed produces no structure at all.
- **`requirements.models[].type` gained `'controlnet'`** ([schemas.js](server/config/schemas.js)) — the enum had no value for it, so both metas failed zod and the registry silently **skipped the folders entirely** (they were absent from `/workflows`, not flagged unavailable). Adding an enum value is backwards compatible.
- ★ **Rig lesson:** an api.json literal that is inherited from the bundle it was derived from will run whenever that parameter is not sent. Both bundles first rendered the *base* bundle's fashion-editorial prompt, which looked like the ControlNet had failed on the depth one. **Keep the api literal in step with the meta default.**
- **Known gaps:** neither bundle ships a `_template.json`, so "Open in ComfyUI" falls back to download+drag. Inpainting is not wired (the branch supports it — `control 64 | mask 1 | masked 64` — the pack zeroes the last two). Only canny and depth of the eight conditions are exercised.

## Latest progress (2026-09-23)

**Viggle Turbo — Qwen Image 2.1 in 4 steps instead of 25; and the "uncensored GGUF" is a dead end twice over (owner request, 2026-09-23)** ⚠ **The bundle was REMOVED on 2026-10-05 at the owner's request, with its 1.27 GB adapter; the findings below are kept because they are the reason it went.** — owner asked to try `KasugaiSakura/Qwen-Image-2.1-Uncensored-GGUF` and `Viggle/Qwen-Image-2.1-viggle-turbo`, spin both up as experimental bundles, and write a pruning worksheet.

- ★ **[image_qwen_image_2_1_viggle_turbo](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_viggle_turbo/) — 14 s → 7 s at a usable quality (2×), same 14.45 GB.** (An earlier version of this entry claimed 2 s at 4 steps; that setting ghosts — see the correction below.) Viggle's v0.2 LoRA (rank 256, alpha 256, 1.27 GiB, DMD2/SenseFlow distillation of Qwen-Image-2.1) applied with `LoraLoaderModelOnly` @ 1.0 on the existing Qwen 2.1 graph; **4 steps**, cfg 1, no negative prompt. VRAM is unchanged because the adapter merges into compute rather than adding resident weight — so it pairs in a lane exactly like the base bundle.
- ★ **The LoRA does patch correctly** (the real risk was the LoRA's **unfused** `img_mlp.gate_layer`/`proj` keys in diffusers `transformer.` format against this rig's **fused** `img_mlp.gate_up` int8-convrot build). `comfy/lora.py:331-333` splits a `.img_mlp.gate_up` weight into `gate_layer`/`proj` halves, line 338 registers the `transformer.` prefix, and `QwenImage21(QwenImage)` (`model_base.py:2660`) makes the guard at line 326 fire. Confirmed by comparing the two safetensors headers directly: **all 227 LoRA modules map onto model targets, 0 unmapped**. (`_swiglu_eager` in `ops.py:949` chunks `gate, up`, so the first half really is the gate — the split is not reversed.)
- ★★ **CORRECTION — the 4-step default this bundle originally shipped was BROKEN, and the check that passed it was invalid** (owner: *"the qwen image are all broken, it's fast but images lack structure"*). The original verification compared **Laplacian variance** ("sharpness") and mean brightness, and **that statistic cannot tell a detailed picture from structured noise — ghosting scores just as high as detail**. No image was ever looked at. Looking at them: at 4 steps the model draws **several overlapping transparent copies of each object**. ★ **The lesson is the one already written into the promotion recipe and ignored here: "then check the output CONTENT — a black image still reports success." An image metric is not a substitute for opening the picture.**
- **What it was NOT:** not the VAE (the owner's first suspicion) — the base `image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i` renders flawlessly with that exact `qwen_image_2.1_vae_bf16` file, and our graph matches **ComfyUI's own `image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i.json` template node for node**, including `EmptyLatentImage → KSampler.latent_image` (the encoder's third, LATENT output is only for the edit variant). Not the key mapping (227/227). Not the int8-convrot basis — **the same LoRA at 25 steps is perfect**, which is what rules that out.
- **It is the distillation's own limit at very low step counts, and it scales with how busy the picture is.** Measured, one prompt and seed: **4 steps** clean on a tight portrait, a faint halo on a single object, **badly duplicated on "an apple AND a glass"**; **8 steps** still duplicated; **12 steps clean**; 25 always clean. Viggle's README documents exactly this — v0.2 has "less ghosting of duplicated subjects" than v0.1 and still "falls short on multi-reference composition". **Fixed: default 12 steps (7 s warm vs the base's 14 s at 25 — still 2×), range 4–25**, and the description says plainly when to raise it.
- **Schedule theories that were tested and did NOT fix it**, so nobody repeats them: ComfyUI's `simple@4` already ≈ Viggle's 4-step training schedule (`time_snr_shift(0.69, linspace(1,0.25,4))`, `model_sampling.py:289`); Viggle's 5-step split fed as raw sigmas via `ManualSigmas`; the same sigmas shifted by both ComfyUI's multiplicative form **and** diffusers' exponential `mu` form; and `ModelSamplingAuraFlow` at shift 2.0 and 3.0 — the last being the pattern ComfyUI's own few-step Qwen template (`image_qwen_image_2512_with_2steps_lora.json`) uses. All still ghosted. Step count, not schedule, is the lever here.
- **Deliberately NOT implemented: the 5-step schedule.** Viggle's README recommends 5 steps with `sigmas=[1.0, 0.875, 0.75, 0.5, 0.25]`, but says those are *raw* sigmas to which **the diffusers pipeline applies its own resolution-dependent time shift**. Feeding them raw through KJNodes' `CustomSigmas` into `SamplerCustom` would skip that shift, i.e. sample a different schedule — so the bundle ships the README's other blessed path, **4 steps on the normal scheduler** ("the training schedule and also works, with slightly less detail and diversity"). Revisit only with a measured A/B.
- ⚠ **Licence:** both repos are **Qwen Research License — research/evaluation only, no commercial use** (Viggle also ships a NOTICE requiring "Built with Qwen" attribution). The base Qwen Image 2.1 already on the rig carries the same licence, so nothing changed by installing this — but it is in the bundle description because a commission would be a problem.
- ★★ **The "uncensored" GGUF fails twice, and neither failure is fixable by downloading a different quant.** (1) **It is not uncensored in any weight sense.** Its own README: *"GGUF quantizations of Qwen/Qwen-Image-2.1 … using the **original upstream base weights**"* — "uncensored" refers to the absence of a pipeline-level safety checker, which ComfyUI never runs anyway. Identical weights to the base model already installed, so identical output. (2) **It cannot load on this rig.** Verified by parsing the GGUF header myself off a 64 KB HTTP range request: `magic=GGUF version=3 tensor_count=297` **`kv_count=0`** — no metadata at all, so `general.architecture` is absent, so city96/ComfyUI-GGUF v1.1.10 falls to `tools/convert.py detect_arch`, whose `arch_list` has **no Qwen entry** → `ValueError: Unknown model architecture!`. `IMG_ARCH_LIST` (which does contain `qwen_image`) is unreachable on that path. ★ And it is **the same repo that already failed**: every download link in its README points at `abenzerps/Qwen-Image-2.1-Uncensored-GGUF`. Loading any Qwen 2.1 GGUF here needs either a correctly-tagged file from another uploader or replacing the GGUF pack with leejet's fork — which registers the same node class names, so it would change GGUF behaviour rig-wide including Ideogram 4's text encoder. **Not done; owner decision.**
- ★ **Rig gotcha — launching ComfyUI with redirected stdout crashes on Windows.** `python.exe main.py … > log.txt` dies with `UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec can't encode character '✓'` — ComfyUI prints a ✓ and the console falls back to cp1252 when stdout is a pipe rather than a terminal. Set `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` when capturing its output. (ComfyQ's own spawn is unaffected; it doesn't redirect through a cp1252 pipe.)
- **[docs/workflow-redundancy.md](docs/workflow-redundancy.md)** — a pruning worksheet over all 63 bundles (now 64): which pairs do the same job for a student, with pros and cons per group. Headlines: the **four SeedVR2 bundles do two jobs** (fp16 vs int8, where int8 is 2.5×/1.9× faster *and* has more controls), and **four variant pairs exist only because an input could not be optional** before `whenEmpty: unlink`. Also records what is **not** redundant, so the six t2i generators and five 3D bundles don't get cut by accident.

## Latest progress (2026-09-22)

**The six uncalibrated bundles measured — and two calibration biases found that had been quietly wrong for months (owner request, 2026-09-22)** — owner: *"I want you to calibrate the uncalibrated workflows"*. Six bundles had no `runtime.json` at all: the five promoted the day before plus `video_minimax_h3_t2v_turbo` (which had only a hand-written `estimatedDurationSec: 35` in its meta). All six now carry a measured duration **and** a measured VRAM peak. Every other bundle has a duration but **no VRAM** — that only became possible on 2026-09-21, so the library is still mostly un-sized by measurement.

| Bundle | warm | cold | **measured VRAM** | static estimate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i` | 14 s | 19 s | **14.44** | 16.1 |
| `image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit` | 15 s | 21 s | **15.97** | 16.1 |
| `image_krea2_turbo_style_reference` | 28 s | 55 s | **22.05** | 18.11 ← estimate was **optimistic** |
| `video_fastvideo_fasth3_t2v` | 46 s | 57 s | **27.02** | 40.63 |
| `video_fastvideo_fasth3_i2v` | 51 s | 78 s | **29.41** | 40.63 |
| `video_minimax_h3_t2v_turbo` | 27 s | 59 s | **29.01** | 41.38 |

★ **Measurement moved the answer in BOTH directions, which is the argument for doing it at all.** The three H3 video bundles' static sums (40–41 GB) exceed this 32 GB card, so the estimator labelled them "streams from RAM" and the lane check would have refused them outright — measured, they fit (just) at 27–29 GB and can be served, though they own the card alone. Krea 2 style-reference went the other way: **22.05 measured against 18.11 estimated**, so the estimate would have admitted a second lane that does not fit. Never pack a card from the static number when a measured one can be had.

- ★★ **The VRAM baseline was polluted by the previous run — a 20 GB error** ([benchmarkService.js](server/benchmark/benchmarkService.js) `_watchVram`). Calibration `/free`s before the timed run, but **ComfyUI answers /free with a 200 while the weights are still resident** (verified: the card sat at 22 GB right after a successful free and only fell to 1.7 GB a minute later). `baseCard` was the **first** sample, so the FastVideo i2v — calibrated straight after its t2v sibling — reported **8.79 GB against a 31.14 GB card peak**; re-run clean it is **29.41 GB**. Two fixes: the floor is now the **lowest** reading seen rather than the first (reading low overstates growth, and overstating is the safe direction — understating is what admits a lane that does not fit), and the new **`_waitForVramSettle()`** polls until the card stops giving memory back before the run starts (bounded to 25 s, because a second lane holding a model resident never drops and waiting for it would hang every calibration). The watcher also reads **immediately** instead of at the first 2 s tick, by which time a fast loader has already put GB on the card. Tests: [vramWatch.test.js](server/benchmark/vramWatch.test.js) (6 checks, in `test:storyboard`). **Any VRAM figure measured before this date, on a card that was not idle, is suspect.**
- ★ **Calibration stages an asset into EVERY media param, `required: false` included** — there was no way to say "leave this one empty", which matters now that `whenEmpty: "unlink"` exists: the Qwen edit would have been measured with **ten** reference pictures instead of the one a student sends. **The mechanism already existed with no code change: a `null` value in `warmupParams`.** It survives the zod `z.record(z.any())`, passes through `_buildCalibrationParams` untouched (it is neither `undefined` nor `''`, so the asset resolver skips it), and `unlinkEmptyMedia` then prunes the loader — i.e. calibration measures exactly the default form. Applied to all 12 optional slots across the Qwen edit, Krea 2 style reference and the FastVideo i2v.
- ★ **The calibration asset is a 512×512 file, so every bundle whose output size follows its input has been timed at a quarter of a real job.** `_resolveAsset` picks the **median by byte size** of the assets dir — on this rig that is `sports_car.png`, **0.26 MP**. The Qwen edit's latent comes from the encoder's reference output (`KSampler.latent_image` ← a switch that is `false`), so its output follows image 1: it measured **5 s**, and **15 s** once `warmupParams` named a real 1 MP picture (`asset:Woman.png`), which matches its t2i sibling's 14 s. Fixed for that bundle; **the same bias applies to every other image-input bundle whose graph has no `ImageScaleToTotalPixels` in front** (the FastVideo i2v does normalise, to 0.4 MP, so its frame size is asset-independent). When an estimate looks implausibly fast, check what it was measured on before believing it.
- **Calibrate at the DEFAULT the student submits.** The FastVideo warmups had shortened the clip to 1 s; the rest of the library measures its default (the MiniMax turbo warmup uses its own 124-frame default). Removed, so both now measure the graph's literal 5 s. Worth knowing for H3: **duration barely scales with clip length** — 1 s cost 38 s and 5 s cost 46 s, because the 32B text encoder, the VAE and the audio pass dominate the 8 sampling steps.
- **`vramResidentGb` reads 0.02 GB for all of these and should not be trusted** — it is `torch_vram`, and int8/convrot weights allocate outside PyTorch's allocator (the same reason `peakGb` takes the larger of torch-held and card-growth). Lane admission uses `vramPeakGb`, which is the honest figure; the plan's idea of packing on "resident" needs a different source before it can work.
**Backfill: 14 more bundles measured — and the static estimator turns out to under-report on half the library (same session)** — owner asked for the likely lane-pairing candidates to be measured rather than the whole library. With 22 bundles now carrying a measured peak, the comparison against the static estimate is the headline: ★★ **11 of 22 were under-estimated by more than 1 GB — the direction that admits a lane which does not fit.** The static sum is model **files on the active path**; what it cannot see is runtime working memory, the decode peak of a video pipeline, and any model a node resolves internally. Worst cases: `utility_seedvr2_3b_int8_video_upscale` **3.69 → 12.64** (3.4×), `utility_pid_image_upscale` 7.72 → **12.32**, `utility_video_frame_interpolation` 0.06 → **2.41** (40×, its `film_net` is named nowhere in the graph), `utility_seedvr2_hd_upscale` 6.78 → **10.09**, the Gemma captioners 8.44 → **10.55**. It over-reports too, harmlessly: `utility_seedvr2_4k_upscale` 15.81 → **8.63**, and the H3 video bundles 40+ → 27–29.
- ★★ **This repo's own headline pairing was wrong.** The 2026-09-21 entry below says *"Flux2 Klein + SeedVR2-3B is 21 GB and fits even here"* — that was 17.29 + 3.69 **estimated**. Measured it is **17.53 + 12.64 = 30.17 GB against 30 GB usable (32 minus the 2 GB card headroom), so it does NOT fit on the 5090.** `image_krea2_turbo_t2i_lora` + SeedVR2-3B (16.92 + 12.64 = 29.56) does, barely, as does Klein + the PiD upscaler (29.85). **Do not quote a pairing from static numbers** — on a 32 GB card the slack is a couple of GB and the estimate is routinely wrong by more than that.
- New measurements: frame interpolation **2.41** · SAM3 **2.78** · TripoSplat **3.25** · SeedVR2-4K **8.63** · Pixal3D multi-view **8.76** · SeedVR2-HD **10.09** · Gemma video/image captioners **10.54/10.56** · PiD upscale **12.32** · SeedVR2-3B **12.64** · Krea 2 t2i+LoRA **16.92** · Flux2 Klein t2i **17.53** · Klein edit-ref **18.65** · Klein edit **19.0**.
- **PiD keeps its hand-measured 18 s.** Re-calibrating it reported 8 s warm exactly as the 2026-09-14 note predicted (its progress bar counts **tiles**, so the first tile is billed as model load); the runtime.json now carries the manual timings **plus** the automatic run's `vram*` fields, which are valid — tile-counting breaks the load/generate split, not the memory watch.
- ★ **Student-mode calibration ignores `disabledPerfFlags`.** `AdminCalibrator._ensureWorker({workflowId})` masks them and restarts ComfyUI so the gauge "neither saves black images nor times a different configuration" — but in **student mode** [workflows.js](server/routes/workflows.js) calls `BenchmarkService.calibrate()` directly, which only swaps to the lane's worker. So calibrating **`utility_seedvr2_7b_int8_image_upscale`** (declares `fp16_accumulation` off) on a serving machine would time a run that **saves black images**, and `image_ideogram4_t2i` / both `utility_marigold_v2_*` (sage off) likewise — silently, which is the same shape as the original Sage bug. Those four were **deliberately left out of this batch**; the route now refuses instead of measuring a broken run.
- **A failed calibration used to leak its VRAM poller.** `_watchVram()`'s `stop()` was only reached on success, so every failed run left a `setInterval` hitting `/system_stats` every 2 s for the life of the server. Now stopped in a `finally` — which is also what made the new tests terminate instead of hanging.
- **Repeated runs of the same bundle differ by ~1.5 GB** on the small ones (frame interpolation measured 2.41 then 0.88 GB on the same asset — frame buffers, not weights). Where two figures exist the bundle keeps the **high-water mark**; admission must not be handed the optimistic one.
**FastVideo i2v → first and/or last frame, both optional (owner request, 2026-09-22)** — owner: *"I want to test the fastvideo with first and last frame (both optional)"*. ★ **The first frame was never required by the model — only by the template's sizing trick.** `MiniMaxH3ImageToVideo` declares **both** keyframes `optional=True` and resizes whatever it is handed (`comfy_extras/nodes_minimax_h3.py:131-153`), so the official template's `LoadImage → ImageScaleToTotalPixels → GetImageSize → width/height` chain was not feeding the node a correctly-sized picture — it only **chose** the output size from the first frame, and that alone is what made the upload mandatory (the 2026-09-21 entry below says the frame is required; **that is now superseded**). Replaced that chain with the **same `ResolutionSelector` the t2v sibling uses** (aspect + megapixels exposed, default `16:9 (Widescreen)` @ 0.4 MP ≈ 848×480), deleted `141`/`142`, and both frames are now `whenEmpty: unlink`. All four combinations prune to a valid, fully-reachable graph (20/21/21/22 nodes, no dangling links).
- ★ **The two keyframes are NOT treated alike** — worth knowing before blaming the bundle for a squashed picture: the first frame is a *geometry anchor* resized with crop `"disabled"` (**a plain stretch to the canvas**), the last frame is a *follower* resized with `"center"` (**aspect-preserving cover-crop**). So a portrait photo as the FIRST frame in a widescreen video comes out squashed, while the same photo as the LAST frame is merely cropped. Said in the description and in the aspect field's own label.
- **Verified on the rig, all four cases** (1:1 with two square pictures so the stretch could not confound it), by comparing the video's end frames against the pictures fed in — mean absolute difference out of 255:

| case | frame 0 vs picture A | last frame vs picture B |
|---|---|---|
| neither (control) | 82.5 | 68.6 |
| first only | **4.7** | 81.8 |
| last only | 81.5 | **11.6** |
| both | **4.4** | **11.6** |

  Each produced 640×640 · 124 frames · 24 fps **with audio**. The first frame comes back almost exactly (~4.5); the last is looser (11.6) — consistent with it being cover-cropped and a destination the model has to *arrive* at rather than start from. Recalibrated: **54 s warm / 66 s cold / 29.75 GB**.
- **The `_template.json` was patched to match and round-trip-verified**, not left stale: it had still carried the old sizing chain and no last-frame loader (the subgraph exposes `last_frame`, the official template simply never wired it). Re-converted through [convert.mjs](tools/ui-to-api/convert.mjs) → **22 nodes, class-type composition identical to the api.json, same width/height/first/last wiring**. ★ Worth the effort because "Open in ComfyUI" hands the *template* to an admin — a stale one silently offers a graph without the feature.
- **Note:** with no frames at all this bundle now does exactly what `video_fastvideo_fasth3_t2v` does. The t2v sibling is kept because it shows a student no upload fields at all, which is one less thing to explain in a class — but they are now redundant if that stops mattering.
- **Still uncalibrated:** `3d_trellis2_image_to_textured_mesh` has timings from an earlier good run but **no VRAM** — its auto-calibration returns in ~1 s with 0 steps, so a measurement taken now would record a cached no-op rather than the pipeline. It needs a real booked job. The five bundles promoted on 2026-09-21 remain **experimental**: they are now calibrated, but none has been eyeballed for output quality, so the Validate button should stay untouched until someone looks at a picture.

## Latest progress (2026-09-21, later)

**Four candidate workflows promoted (experimental), an "unlink" mode for unused reference images, and VRAM measured during calibration (owner request, 2026-09-21)** — owner added four candidates and asked for everything the cards need (VRAM, description, prompt guides) in experimental mode. All four converted with [convert.mjs](tools/ui-to-api/convert.mjs) through ComfyUI's own `graphToPrompt` (28/14/9/20 nodes, no dropped-link warnings — they are all subgraph exports, so hand-flattening was never an option).
- ★ **New generic param field `whenEmpty: "unlink"`** ([optionalMedia.js](server/workers/optionalMedia.js) `unlinkEmptyMedia`, applied in `worker.submit`, 6 tests). The existing optional-image mechanism fills a neutral **placeholder** and relies on the graph hiding it behind a switch. That is wrong for a *reference* slot: Qwen Image 2.1's `images.image_N` is a ComfyUI **AUTOGROW** input with no switch, so a black placeholder would be **used as a reference picture** and quietly change the result. `unlink` instead deletes the loader and every link to it, which is exactly what an unused autogrow slot expects. Verified per bundle that the pruned graph has no dangling links.
- **[image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_t2i/)** — Qwen Image 2.1 text-to-image (6.8 + 8.8 + 0.6 GB = **16.2 GB**). Strong text rendering; native 2K at 4 MP; the description carries Qwen's documented RGBA wording for transparent PNGs, and explains why there is no negative prompt (cfg is fixed at 1). Steps 25 default, 40–50 is Qwen's own setting.
- **[image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit](workflows/image_qwen_image_2_1_image_edit/)** — the same model editing a picture with **up to ten reference images**, addressed in the prompt as `<image1>`, `<image2>`… Image 1 is required; slots 2–10 are optional and `unlink`ed when empty. The converter's `ImageCompare` node was pruned (an OUTPUT node that would drop a before/after tile into the gallery).
- **[image_krea2_turbo_style_reference](workflows/image_krea2_turbo_style_reference/)** — ★ category **`t2i`, not `image-edit`** (owner): the supplied pictures carry the STYLE, nothing of theirs is being edited, and per the taxonomy rule that makes it generation. Krea 2 Turbo + `krea2_style_reference` LoRA: generate from a prompt in the STYLE of one to three supplied pictures (`TextEncodeQwenImageEditPlus.image1/2/3`, whose 2nd and 3rd are optional node inputs, so `unlink` is safe). Exposes style strength. The in-path `PreviewAny` (30:20) was rewired to its source and dropped — it fed the encoder's prompt, so left alone it would have published the expanded prompt as a stray text result.
- ★ **The two official FastVideo templates CONTRADICT each other, and the t2v one is the stale one.** `video_fastvideo_fasth3_t2v`'s note says *"this distilled checkpoint supports t2va only. FL2VA (first/last-frame) and Ref2VA were not distilled"*; `video_fastvideo_fasth3_i2v`'s note says *"supports t2va and fl2va only.
